# Changelog

All notable changes to hypergumbo are documented in this file. The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/).

- Released **tool** is at: v8.0.0
- Released **schema** is at: v0.20.1

This changelog tracks the **tool version** (package releases). The **schema version** is tracked separately in `schema.py` as `SCHEMA_VERSION`. The schema version changes when `docs/schema.json` has significant updates: breaking changes to the behavior map output format (minor bump) or additions like new type definitions for YAML validation (patch bump).

> **Looking for the reader-friendly summary?** See **[docs/RELEASE-NOTES-8.X.md](docs/RELEASE-NOTES-8.X.md)** for the audience-organized release notes of the current line — each opens with a TL;DR naming the breaking changes. Earlier lines: [7.x](docs/RELEASE-NOTES-7.X.md), [6.x](docs/RELEASE-NOTES-6.X.md), [5.x](docs/RELEASE-NOTES-5.X.md). This file is the **implementer log**: structured, mechanism-level entries per release.

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- **Two community I/O overlays now ship in the wheel and load by default, and every run that uses them says so out loud (ADR-0047 rulings 1/5/6, WI-riros).** They previously sat under `docs/`, which `pyproject.toml` does not package — so an installed hypergumbo could not read them and they shipped to nobody. `python-http-clients.yaml` and `go-web-frameworks.yaml` move into `hypergumbo_core/io_primitives_overlays/`, declare `provenance: community` and a dated `retrieved:`, and are selected **per language** (INV-lufib: a catalogue applied to every language once hard-failed any repo containing a second one).
- **The permission is conditional and the condition is the stderr line, not the JSON field.** A run that loads community rows prints, per queried language, the file, its retrieval date, where to override it, and how to switch it off — shaped after the `in_progress` warning and wired at the same three consumers (`io-boundaries`, `verify-claims`, `slice --io-boundary`) so it cannot fire in one and be silent in the other. `catalog_provenance` gains a `shipped_default` key alongside — a **third state**, because "hypergumbo shipped it and does not vouch for it" is a value `user_supplied` cannot express, and collapsing it into either neighbour re-opens the INV-zosun gap.
- **An unvouched row adds findings and never licenses the all-clear, which is the property the whole feature rests on.** INV-buzab makes "the catalogue classified this call" mean "this call was examined", and an examined call stops blocking a clean verdict — so shipping these rows unmarked would have silently turned "never writes to the host filesystem" from `inconclusive` into **`confirmed`** for every repository that uses `requests`, on the strength of rows describing only its *network* surface. That is INV-zubuh's "presence of SOME rows must not vouch for the rest" arriving through a new door. Rows from a shipped default are stamped `unvouched` at merge (not read from the file, so a file cannot claim otherwise) and a call they classify still counts as unexamined. The detection direction is untouched: the egress is visible, and adding a sink can only ever add a finding.
- **A shipped default may not declare `module_completeness`, refused at load rather than reviewed at merge.** That entry is the single grant of confirmability — a closed-world claim that an unmatched call into a module is an examined negative. Granting one by default would hand every user a claim about their own dependencies that they never made. `hypergumbo-self.yaml` therefore stays under `docs/`: it is hypergumbo's own overlay for hypergumbo's own dependency set and carries exactly those grants.
- **A default overlay does not restate what the catalogue claims about the stdlib.** `merge` is self-over-argument with the overlay as receiver, so without care Python went `provenance_declared` → `in_progress` the moment defaults loaded, and every Python run would have emitted "io-boundary results may be incomplete" beside the true disclosure. Community rows are outside the stdlib enumeration `provenance_declared` asserts, so a default now preserves the base status and provenance; a user overlay's effect on status is deliberately left exactly as it was.
- **`--no-default-overlays` on `io-boundaries` and `verify-claims`** omits them, and the registry knows about the new directory: `io_primitives_overlays` is registered with **no user channel of its own**, because a user's own rows for this content belong in the `io_primitives` channel — a second home for one kind of edit would be the drift the registry exists to prevent.

- **The catalogue registry now answers extensibility, and a new family cannot land without answering it (ADR-0047 ruling 7, WI-tupid).** `CatalogSpec` gains `user_channel` (the per-family overlay directory under `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/hypergumbo/`) and `no_channel_reason`, both **required with no default** — that absence is the mechanism, not an oversight: a defaulted field would let the next catalogue family silently inherit "no channel, no reason" and the ruling would stop binding. A test pins the required-ness itself, so a later convenience default fails rather than quietly disarming the gate.
- **`scripts/yaml-catalog-index --check` — already a CI gate — now refuses an incoherent answer**, which is what makes the fields mean something rather than merely exist. Five shapes are refused: declaring both a channel and a reason for having none; declaring neither; a channel name that is not `<directory>.d` (derived, so the two names cannot drift); and a `channel_scope` or `channel_gated` set on a family with no channel to apply them to. The registry itself passes the gate it just gained.
- **All eight families carry their ruling-10 verdict, pinned as a table rather than a count** so a future edit that flips one has to change the test and say so. Channel: `io_primitives`, `taint_sources`, `taint_sanitizers` (existing), `frameworks` (new). No channel: `cfg_nodes` and `url_folding`, each with a written reason — grammar node types a user cannot know better than, and rows naming Python engine functions inside the shipped package, which would make a user file inert without also accepting user code.
- **Two families needed a qualifier rather than a yes/no, and the fields carry it.** `dataflow_patterns` is **mixed**: its grammar rows are internal by the `cfg_nodes` reasoning, while its `library_patterns` rows are regexes over call syntax that a user with an in-house collection type has a legitimate row to add — so `channel_scope="library_patterns"` grants the section, not the file. `function_summaries` gets `channel_gated="CAVEAT_USER_SUPPLIED_SANITIZER"`, because a terminating user summary **is** a sanitizer declaration and changes what the tool will *claim*, not only what it can see.
- **The operator view and the architecture doc both surface the answer**, so it is read where the question gets asked. The CLI reports it **per family, not per file** — deliberately: `dataflow_patterns`' channel is section-scoped and only 9 of its 20 files carry that section, so a file-level "149 of 156 extensible" would overstate what a user may actually contribute.

- **A clock read is `host_info_read` in every catalogued language (WI-tubij).** The WI-pavob ruling made a clock read an I/O boundary; that is a statement about the *vocabulary*, not about Rust, so it binds all fifteen catalogues. Before this, `host_info_read` meant "reads the host, and also the clock" in erlang and elixir and "reads the host, but never the clock" in the other thirteen — exactly the per-language drift the axis discipline exists to prevent. A monotonic clock counts: on Linux `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` runs from boot, so an early read leaks approximate uptime, which distinguishes machines.
- **Enumerated per language against named evidence, not from memory** — the item's own first instruction. Local toolchains where they exist (rustc 1.94.0 `library/std/src/time.rs`, Python 3.12.3 introspection, node v20.19.6, glibc headers, GNU bash 5.2.21) and allowlisted upstream source otherwise (`golang/go`, `openjdk/jdk`, `erlang/otp`, `elixir-lang/elixir`, `haskell/time`, `swift-corelibs-foundation`). One shipped gap surfaced from the enumeration rather than from the existing rows: erlang catalogued `os:system_time` and `os:perf_counter` but **not `os:timestamp/0`**.
- **The refusals are recorded with reasons, not just the additions.** Arithmetic on an already-captured value is not a read (`Instant::duration_since`; source-confirmed that `Instant::elapsed` *is* `Instant::now() - *self` and therefore *is* one). Sleeping is not a read. `clock_settime` is a host *write* and a different boundary question. `time.localtime(t)` is refused because the argument *replaces* the read and the catalogue matches on name, not arity — while `datetime.now(tz)` is kept because there the argument only shapes a read that always happens.
- **Catalogue inheritance is respected rather than duplicated.** `_CATALOG_PARENTS` gives `cpp→c`, `kotlin→java`, `scala→java`, `elixir→erlang`, so kotlin and scala gain the JVM clock surface with no rows of their own, cpp inherits the libc surface and adds only `std::chrono`, and elixir inherits the `:os`/`:erlang` rows and adds only its native `System`/`DateTime` ones.
- **Keying is a measured safety constraint, not a style choice.** Rows go under `functions:` by default; `methods:` only where genuinely a method *and* the language already declares method-keyed modules. bash, c, cpp, elixir, erlang and haskell declare **zero** today, and a method row there would hand the language its first method-keyed module, admit it to `method_starved_modules`, and — since erlang stamps `remote`/`local` and haskell `application`, none of which is a catalogue kind — manufacture the starvation report the rows exist to help retire (INV-pimir). In all six the clock API is genuinely a function or a shell variable, so truth and safety coincide. A test pins it.

### Added

- **ADR-0046: taint precision is now two numbers, correctness and usefulness (owner ruling, WI-gohok).** Measurement 0005 surfaced a limit that is not a bug in any producer: the taint model, and every precision rubric to date, cannot separate a value that *leaks* from one a config-driven program was *told to act on*. Caddy reads its config from stdin; that config names a log file; the bytes reach `os.Chmod` through a citable chain. Under the 0001 rubric that is a true positive — an independent adjudication commissioned for the question agreed, and accepted the consequence: **every** filesystem, network and subprocess operation whose argument comes from a configured value is a true positive, for any config-driven server. True and uninformative at once, over a class that is every config field of every module in a plugin-registry architecture.
- **The ruling keeps the finding true and adds a second figure.** Correctness precision is unchanged (`TP / adjudicated`), so 0001's "exploitability is not the question" is not relitigated and comparability with 0001–0006 is preserved by construction. **Useful precision** — `(TP − VACUOUS) / adjudicated`, same denominator, numerator-only deduction — is published alongside. This is the quantity the standing decision band was already written against (*"≤25% useful ⇒ recall stays stopped"*) and which nothing could compute: 0006 itself calls 33.9% "an upper bound on useful precision, not a measure of it."
- **The exclusion is structural, not a severity judgement**, which is the amendment that keeps adjudication objective — the property that produced 94.6% independent-panel agreement on 0006. A finding is CONFIGURED-ACTION only when three things are each *citable*: a declared-configuration source, a deserialization into a declared configuration schema, and a sink argument read from a field of that schema which the sink operation exists to consume. Any clause that cannot be cited means the finding counts as useful — the test defaults to counting findings useful, so it can only understate the damage.
- **The two vacuity classes are reported separately because they have opposite lifetimes.** CONFIGURED-ACTION is a permanent limit of the model; KIND-MISDECLARED (a Haskell `IORef` filed under `untrusted-input-no-database`) is a **defect** — INV-nular — that gets fixed, not excluded. Conflating them would launder a bug through a rubric change.

### Fixed

- **A Rust receiver type the source spelled out in full now reaches the edge (INV-linub L3).** The first half of this fix recovered an external receiver's module path through `use_aliases`, which works only when the type was imported *by name*. When the source writes the path instead — `fn dump(f: &mut std::fs::File)`, or `let start = std::time::Instant::now()` with no `use` — `_normalize_rust_type_to_bare_name` reduces it to `File` / `Instant` ("strip module paths", per its own docstring, which is correct for the first-party symbol lookups it feeds), and the bare name is then absent from `use_aliases`. The path was present in the text and dropped anyway. A second file-scoped map now carries the written path alongside the bare one, consulted only on the unresolved-external branch where a bare name is useless.
- **It cannot invent a path, which is what keeps the L4 gate honest.** A value is returned only when the written text already contains `::` *and* the path's terminal segment equals the bare name the normalizer produced. That equality binds the two readings of one type: `std::io::Result<std::fs::File>` normalizes to `File` while the text starts with `Result`, so it yields nothing rather than stamping `std::io::Result` on a `File` receiver and matching the wrong rows. A module slot holding a *wrong* path is worse than an empty one — it converts a correct refusal into a confident mismatch. The pre-existing negative control (a prelude `Vec` with no `use` and no written path) still degrades to `external`.
- **Measured A/B over 11 Rust repositories, per-arm cache, arms strictly sequential, the arm proven live before each run.** Catalogued trait-method call sites matching a method-kind row: **41 of 1202 → 98 of 1206 (3.4% → 8.1%)**. Rust call sites receiving an I/O boundary classification: **303 → 360 (+18.8%)**, entirely in `host_info_read` (68→116) and `fs_read` (50→59). **Six of the eight boundaries are unchanged as controls** — `env_read` 29, `env_write` 19, `fs_write` 63, `ipc_recv` 7, `logging` 15, `subprocess` 52.
- **It moves no taint flow, and the reason is structural rather than incidental: 2 → 2 across the same 11 repositories.** Of the 57 newly matched sites, 48 are `Instant::elapsed` — a `host_info_read`, which *is* an auto-derived taint source, but a clock read reaching a write-side sink is rare and none does here. The other 9 are `fs_read` rows (`exists`, `canonicalize`, `is_dir`, `metadata`), which are **by design** neither source nor sink: `AUTO_SOURCE_LABEL_MAP` excludes `fs_read` deliberately, because reading a file does not by itself make its contents sensitive. So this is an io-boundaries improvement, not a taint improvement, and it is reported as one.
- **What still blocks the sink side is named, not left implicit.** `write_all` did not move (5 matched, 125 still missing) and `lock` is the largest single miss at 440. Both are dominated by two receiver shapes this fix does not reach, because they lose the type further upstream — in the receiver walk rather than in normalization: a struct-field receiver (`h.f.write_all(...)`) and a chained receiver (`File::create(p)?.write_all(...)`). Those are filed separately rather than folded in, since each needs its own measurement.

- **One I/O expression is one taint flow, not one per catalogue row that describes it (INV-sukoh).** `python.yaml` deliberately carries a parent *attribute* row beside a child *slot-family* row, and the child's own note says why: *"a method call on the mapping carries `os.environ` as its module slot, where the parent's attribute row cannot reach."* They were meant to be complementary — parent for a bare `os.environ[...]` read, child for `os.environ.get(...)`. On the `.get` form **both** reach, because the analyzer emits **two edges for the one expression**: a `module_attribute_reference` to `os.environ` and an `ast_call_direct` to `os.environ.get`. One read became two flows.
- **The relation is computed from the catalogue, not listed.** An entry is a slot-family *parent* when its qualified name is the **module slot** of another entry matched at the **same caller** — the catalogue's own structure, so a user-supplied catalogue with the shape is covered without a table anyone has to remember to extend. Four shipped rows have it today, all Python: `os.environ` (env_read), `sys.stdin` (ipc_recv), `sys.stdout` and `sys.stderr` (logging).
- **Two things the item did not have, both found by checking for analogues.** It is **symmetric** — two of those four parents are *sinks*, so `sys.stdout.write(x)` doubles a sink exactly as `os.environ.get()` doubles a source, and the item measured only the source side. And it is **not row-unit-only**: `collapse_unadjudicated_flows` runs on `{structural, ddg_mixed}`, so a confirmed `ddg` walk — which is a pair claim — passes through untouched and the two rows never merge, making the doubling cost a whole **situation**. 0005's population was structural and ddg_mixed, which is why it read as row-only there.
- **Measured A/B on the two repositories 0005 named**, per-arm cache, arms strictly sequential, each arm proven before it ran. mitmproxy: summed `collapsed_flow_count` **29 → 20 (−31.0%)** over 6 changed rows of 12; poetry: **5 → 3 (−40.0%)** over 1 changed row of 2. Every changed row shows the filed shape exactly — `['os.environ', 'os.environ.get'] → ['os.environ.get']`. **Control: 8 of 8 claim verdicts unchanged** (`violated` on both repositories, so the run is not vacuously inert), with evidence counts, flow origins, excluded-flow and sanitized-flow counts all unchanged. The situation count did not move on either repository.
- **Subsumption is conditional on the child, which is what makes it safe.** A bare `os.environ["X"]` emits only the attribute edge, so nothing subsumes it and the read is still reported; dropping the parent unconditionally would delete the read outright. A `callee`-seeded source neither subsumes nor is subsumed — it seeds its BFS at the callee, so parent and child search different subgraphs. **What is deducted is a name, not a flow:** where both forms genuinely occur in one symbol the two edges are indistinguishable from the single-form case, because edges are deduplicated on `(src, dst, edge_type)` (INV-vukiv) and the multiplicity is gone before this sees it. The coarser name is dropped, the finding is still reported, and no verdict moves.

- **Two of that re-derivation's three sensitivities are withdrawn: the vocabulary ruling they awaited already existed (WI-gibom follow-up).** `argv` and application config under `env_read` were published as open questions on INV-nular's word that they *"need a vocabulary ruling, not a row edit"*. The spec already contains it, in the sentence INV-tutar's resolution added: *"`env_read` is an ambient CONFIGURATION read (environment variables, system properties, **argv** — values that **may carry a credential**)"*. The membership rule is capability to carry a credential, not being one — which is precisely why `runtime.GOOS` was split out and argv was not. **The useful figure does not move: 24.1% either way**, because neither class was ever deducted; what changes is that the record no longer publishes a doubt the project had already resolved. One sensitivity stands — `io_lib:format` declared `logging` against its own note *"Returns iolist, not direct I/O"* — taking it to 21.4% if it resolves against the row.
- **The evidence agrees with the definition rather than merely deferring to it.** argv is world-readable in `ps`, which is why a `--password` flag is a known hazard; and one of the five application-config situations is plausible reading `Application.get_env(:plausible, :paddle)`, a payments provider's configuration. INV-nular's own text had already reached the answer without noticing — it calls `Application.get_env` *"an ambient configuration read"*, the spec's exact phrase, and then files the question as open. **What was genuinely unresolved is not a vocabulary question**: the boundary means *may carry a credential* while the label it derives says `host_secret`, and that gap is the one ADR-0046 exists to *measure* rather than close. Deducting the rows would do by catalogue membership what the ADR decided to do by measurement, and would push in the false-all-clear direction.
- **0006's useful precision is now derived instead of asserted: 24.1%, not "≤25.0%" (WI-gibom).** The measurements index published `≤25.0%` for the one quantity a program-level decision band is written against (*"≤25% useful ⇒ recall stays stopped"*), and that figure was **not derivable from the record**: it was added a day after 0006, in the commit that published 0008, and 0006's body names exactly five vacuous TPs, which supports 29.5%. Re-derived under ADR-0046's two named classes over 0006's own ledger: **27 useful of 112 adjudicated = 24.1%**, with **11 KIND-MISDECLARED** and **0 CONFIGURED-ACTION**. The bound survives; the derivation did not. Correctness precision is untouched at 33.9%.
- **CONFIGURED-ACTION contributes zero, which refutes the expectation ADR-0046 itself recorded.** The ADR predicted the number would fall *because* its new class had never been applied to 0006. It does not apply there at all, and the reason is structural rather than lucky: 0006's claim set admits only `env_read` and `host_info_read` sources, so clause 2 — deserialization into a type whose fields are *declared* as a configuration schema — has no candidate to match. The ADR's motivating shape (caddy's JSON into a tag-declared struct) belongs to 0005's cohort. The number did fall, 33.9% → 24.1%; every point of it is KIND-MISDECLARED. Nearest miss disclosed: cilium reads `os.Getenv(SockPathEnv)` and dials the socket it names — clauses 1 and 3 cite, clause 2 does not, so it counts useful.
- **Six of the eleven deductions are a bash defect section A named only in part**, and each is cited to a line: `>` redirects whose every target is `/dev/null` (cert-manager ×4, spacedrive, guacamole-client), fixed the next day by PR #541. The other five are shellcheck's Haskell `IORef`/`STRef` under `db_read`, which `haskell.yaml`'s own `boundary_ruling: unruled` note already calls "in-process mutable references, not a database".
- **One reversal, recorded because only the second check caught it.** gocryptfs was first labelled discard-only on four `/dev/null` sites; a raw re-read of the file found a fifth, `exec 200> "$LOCKFILE"` — a numbered-fd redirect to a real path whose value is env-derived (`TMPDIR` → `TESTDIR` → `LOCKFILE`). A genuine environment-to-filesystem write. The first cut would have published 23.2%.
- **Two of section A's own rows are struck rather than carried, and three unruled questions are declared rather than deducted.** INV-nular's reproduction refuted `>&2 counts` (fd duplication emits no edge at all — a recall gap, the opposite of the over-report filed) and `>> is reported as >` (distinct `dst` ids and `io_mode`, pinned since INV-vavup); a refuter's report is a lead, not a finding. Conversely, `argv` and application-config under `env_read` are documented deliberate rows awaiting a vocabulary ruling, so deducting them would be the record inventing the ruling: they are published as sensitivities with arithmetic (**15.2% / 19.6% / 21.4%**, and **10.7%** if all resolve against). Two new sink-side defects found in passing — `io_lib:format` declared `logging` while its own note concedes it "returns iolist, not direct I/O", and `urllib.request.Request` declared `net_send` though it is a constructor — are filed onto INV-nular, not deducted.

- **`collapsed_flow_count` is now reconcilable against the call sites it counts (INV-kakad).** Three independent refuters on three repositories reported the row-level denominator as uncheckable against the source. They were right, and the reason is disclosure rather than arithmetic: the record emitted the multiplier beside `source_primitives` / `sink_primitives` — *distinct names* — and nothing about site multiplicity, so two rows with identical primitives legitimately carried different counts with nothing to explain the difference. shellcheck showed three rows at 1 source name × 1 sink name → 1 and a fourth at 1 × 1 → **2**.
- **The rule, established by live repro on all three named repositories.** The count is the number of *(source call site, sink call site)* pairs where the sink site is reachable from the source site's caller. `striptests` reads env **once** and reaches `redirect.>` at **two** sites — the file node, and `sponge`, which it reaches — so one source site × two reachable sink sites = 2. Findings now carry `sink_call_sites` as `(caller, callee)` **pairs**, unioned across a collapsed group, and the multiplier is bounded by `len(source_primitives) * len(sink_call_sites)`.
- **The first cut of that fix was wrong and the live re-run caught it.** Recording the sink *caller* alone collapsed kamaraflow's four sinks — `open`, `file.write`, `os.makedirs`, `shutil.copyfile`, all called from one function — to a single site, leaving `collapsed_flow_count=8` against a bound of 2. Both sides multiply: one callee reached from two callers, and one caller reaching four callees, are both live shapes. Every unit test written for the shellcheck shape passed either way; only re-running the three repositories exposed it, and that case is now pinned as its own test. **Measured after the correction: 18 of 18 evidence rows across the three repositories reconcile, all exactly.**
- **No double-counting was found, and the item's "inflated in at least one direction" is not carried forward.** `source_callers` is built one entry per edge and edges are deduplicated on `(src, dst, edge_type)` (INV-vukiv), so a site cannot enter twice; every pair traced corresponds to a distinct (source edge, sink edge) with a real reachability path. The refuters' counts were low because they counted primitive *occurrences in source text* while the tool counts reachable *site pairs*.
- **A WI-tubij regression on `dev`, found by running the suite rather than by CI.** `test_bash_env_read_source.py::test_bash_now_carries_both_halves` froze the exact list of bash taint-source rows, and WI-tubij's clock additions (`$EPOCHREALTIME`, `$EPOCHSECONDS`, `$SECONDS` — `host_info_read` in every catalogue) broke it while changing nothing the test is about. It went red on `dev` because smart-test's hand-extended manifest for that PR did not select this file, so CI never ran it. The assertion now tests the **split** the docstring actually claims — both labels derive, `env.environ` is the `host_secret` half, and every `host_description` source stays in the `shell` module — instead of an inventory that decays on the next correct catalogue edit.
- **What this does not fix, stated because the number is a precision denominator.** For `analysis_method=structural` the pair test is pure call-graph reachability with no check that a value crosses — which is what ADR-0017 defines `structural` to be. So a rate computed over this count is a rate over reachability pairs rather than over value-flow claims, and it grows with how much a file reaches rather than with anything about taint: kamaraflow's source is a *file* node, which reaches everything the file calls. That is an independent, mechanical reason 0006 was right to make **situation** canonical, and it is stronger than "the row figure is less trustworthy" — the row figure is a rate over a different population than the claim is about. Tracked on the item, not fixed here.

### Fixed

- **A repository referring to itself by its PUBLISHED PACKAGE NAME is no longer reported as an unexamined third-party module (INV-vivok).** `_uncatalogued_external_modules` already excluded a module whose file the analysis read (`_analyzed_modules`, path-derived) and one the language marks internal (`is_definitionally_first_party` — `crate::`, `./`). Neither can see a repo that refers to itself by the name it *publishes*, because that name lives in a manifest and not in the directory layout. caddy is the sharp case: its relative spellings (`modules/caddyhttp`) were already suppressed while `github.com/caddyserver/caddy/v2/modules/caddyhttp` was reported — the **same package judged two ways** by how the importing file happened to spell it. A third mechanism, `supply_chain.collect_first_party_package_names`, reads the published name from `Cargo.toml` `[package].name`, `go.mod` `module`, and `package.json` `name`, and the CLI threads it to both coverage call sites the way `higher_fidelity_available` already is.
- **The item's own premise was refuted in the code before it was built on.** INV-vivok proposed discriminating first-party package nodes by `supply_chain_tier` and rejected the idea because the tier "is None on every package node in both repos". It is not: `ir.py` declares `supply_chain_tier: int = 1` and serialises it **nested** under `supply_chain.tier`, so only a *top-level* read returns `None` — the same null-extractor shape that has produced a null result three times now. Measured over all 42 cached surveys, 796 package nodes: **187 at tier 1, 609 at tier 3, none `None`**, and 10 of 19 repos carry both, so the field does discriminate. The conclusion survives for a **better** reason: a package node's tier is derived from its *declaring file's path*, so a genuinely third-party package declared in an in-repo manifest reads first_party — `cmake:CMakeLists.txt:204-204:"GnuTLS":package` sits at tier 1, and GnuTLS is not libzmq. Suppressing on tier would hand a false clean verdict to exactly the population this gate protects. The manifest is read directly instead, which is a fact rather than a shape heuristic.
- **`sketch._detect_project_binary_names` was checked for reuse and rejected on both halves.** It regex-scrapes *every* `name = "..."` in `Cargo.toml` — including `[dependencies.<x>]`, `[[bin]]` and `[lib]` — and keeps only the **last component** of a Go module path. Admitting a dependency's name would suppress a genuine third-party module, and `caddy` alone matches none of the callee slots that carry the full `github.com/caddyserver/caddy/v2`. "What is this repo's executable called" is a different question with a different failure direction.
- **Measured by recomputing the gate over each repo's own cached survey — the same instrument the item was filed with.** bellman **15 → 14** modules reported, suppressing exactly `bellman.VerificationError`; caddy **158 → 151**, suppressing the four entries the item named plus three it had not (`modules/caddypki`, `modules/caddytls`, `notify`). **Nothing was added in either arm.** The controls are the result: bellman still reports `blake2s_simd::Params`, `rand`, `rayon` and `ff.Field`, and caddy still reports the whole of Go's stdlib (`archive/tar`, `crypto/ecdsa`, `crypto/rand`, …). Because suppression here can only ever *hide* a real dependency, the match is component-bounded — `caddy` must not swallow `caddyserver`, `bellman` must not swallow `bellmanx` — and both bounds are pinned by tests.
- **Corpus-wide, and the safety check is sharper than "nothing was added".** Across the 42-survey corpus the change suppresses **154 module reports and adds 0**, in 10 of 42 repos. Because suppression is the false-clean direction, each suppressed entry was then re-checked against the names its own repo declares as *dependencies*: 90 collide, and all 90 are the workspace-sibling shape INV-nuzas/ADR-0041 D8a already rules first-party (package A declaring in-repo sibling B — `grep::pcre2` in ripgrep, `lib/algo` in lila, whose 118 suppressions are all its own `ui/lib` and `ui/bits` frontend modules). Narrowing further to names declared **only** as a registry version leaves **4**, in 2 repos: a monorepo that publishes a package to npm and has a sub-project depend on it by version rather than by link. The name is still the repo's own and its source is in the tree, so the suppression matches the invariant; whether that sub-project resolves to the in-repo copy or an installed one is **not** established, and is disclosed in the collector's docstring rather than asserted away.
- **As the item predicted, this flips no verdict.** `qualifying_only` is `not unknown`, and 14 and 151 entries respectively still withhold both repos. It is noise reduction in a disclosure whose remaining bulk is stdlib enumeration (WI-lutuh), and it makes that remainder readable rather than shrinking it.

- **`concept-audit-record` no longer advances the audit cadence for an audit that left no trail.** The 2026-08-20 record named suspect domain `call_construct`, produced tracker items, and wrote **no findings document and no Examples entry** — so the bookkeeping that drives the cadence reminder moved while the audit trail did not, and the 2026-08-27 re-run of the same domain had to re-derive the whole inventory. The recorder now refuses (exit 2, state untouched) unless a `*.md` under `docs/audits/` or the lab notebook names the suspect domain — separators and case stripped, so `identity vocabulary` matches `concept-audit-identity-vocabulary_07152026.md`. `--force` overrides and says so on stderr.
- **The first cut of that gate would have passed the exact case it was built for.** Matching on name alone was satisfied by *any* historical document, and the April-era `docs/audits/0012-evidence-type-cluster-d-call-construct.md` already existed on 2026-08-20 — the live run named it as the witness, which is how the flaw surfaced. A write-up only evidences *this* audit if it **postdates the previous record**, so the mtime check is the load-bearing half. mtime is imperfect on a fresh clone, but a fresh clone's files read as recent, so the failure direction is permissive rather than a false refusal.

- **A C++ type alias no longer reports as an attribute read (INV-sibij).** INV-pusin's ratified statement — *a module named solely by an import or a type annotation performs no I/O and must not withhold a boundary verdict* — held for cpp's `using std::cout;` and failed for `using S = std::string;`, whose scoped path sits under `type_descriptor`. The context was **not** added when the defect was filed, because in the cpp grammar `type_descriptor` also covers casts and template arguments and the filing recorded that "whether every member of that population is genuinely a type annotation ... is NOT established". It is now, by parsing each form and calling the production `_scoped_context_kind`: the context covers exactly three forms — type alias, `static_cast<std::string>(y)`, and `std::vector<std::string>` — and all three **name** a type and read no value.
- **The filed defect is the smallest member of the population it lives in.** Counted on real repositories, template arguments outnumber type aliases 10–25× (rocksdb 2359 vs 126, powerdns 611 vs 80), so the change is a far larger lever than the filing implies — and correspondingly riskier, since removing `module_attr_ref` removes *withholding*, the false-all-clear direction. The A/B therefore reports **module_attr_ref edge count → uncatalogued-module count → verdict**, in that order of sensitivity: a several-thousand-edge change would otherwise hide behind an unchanged `inconclusive`.
- **`call_expression` is deliberately NOT added**, though the filing measured it leaking too: it is where genuine calls live, `std::ofstream("f")` is one, and suppressing it would be the silent recall loss the item warns about. `std::cout << 1` (`binary_expression`) and `std::string::npos` (`init_declarator`) are value reads and keep emitting — both pinned as positive controls, because a skip tuple that swallows them is a mute button, not a fix.
- **The test harness was asserting against tuples the analyzers no longer ship, and now cannot.** `test_base.py` carried hand-copies of both skip tuples, and `RUST_SKIP` still listed `use_declaration` — which INV-pusin's *second* closure had moved out of the proximate tuple into `skip_ancestor_kinds`, because the grammar wraps every `use` form but the bare one. So every rust test in that class passed through the mechanism production had **abandoned**: the right answer for the wrong reason, and blind to a regression in the real path. A new parity test reads the tuples out of the shipped analyzers and fails if either copy drifts; the rust harness now passes the ancestor kinds too, so those tests exercise the configuration that actually runs.
- **Measured A/B over the five cpp repositories that carry a survey baseline, both arms, fresh cache each, arm-take proven by reading the tuple out of the shipped module: 12,556 → 8,566 `module_attr_ref` edges (**−3,990, −31.8%**) and **0 of 15 verdicts moved**.** Per repo: rocksdb −2494 (−59%), powerdns −1219, shaka-packager −251, libzmq −15, plasma-desktop −11. The zero verdict movement is the **safety** result rather than a disappointment — this change removes withholding, so a verdict flipping to clean would be a claim about the world needing per-repo defence, and none did. The two surviving `inconclusive` verdicts are held by entirely different rungs (plasma-desktop by `glsl`, a language with no I/O catalogue; shaka-packager by an opaque-launch disclosure at 14 call sites). A verdict-only A/B would have reported "no effect" while a third of cpp's attribute-read population disappeared, which is why the instrument reports edge count first.
- **A second leaking form was found by enumerating past the filed repro, and a third was found and deliberately NOT fixed.** `class A : public std::exception` (`base_class_clause`) names a base type and is now skipped. `for (std::string x : v)` (`for_range_loop`) still leaks, because that context covers **both halves of the loop** — the variable's type *and* the range expression, where `for (auto& l : std::cin)` is a genuine stream read. Skipping it would suppress real I/O; separating them needs field-level granularity that `skip_context_kinds` does not have, since it matches ancestor *kind* only. The residual over-withholds, which is the safe direction, and is pinned by a test asserting the range expression still emits — so anyone who later adds `for_range_loop` sees immediately what it costs.

### Added

- **Rust's `io_primitives` catalogue declares its first `module_completeness` grants — 20 of 33 audited modules (WI-lutuh).** A grant asserts that *every* I/O primitive a module exposes is catalogued, which is what lets a consumer read silence as an **examined negative** rather than "none I could see". Rust declared **zero**, so no Rust module could ever be an examined negative and every clean Rust boundary verdict was withheld. Rust is now the **second** of the 15 shipped catalogues to declare any (python has 76; the other 13 remain at zero). Method, probe, and every refusal with its reason: [`docs/surveys/rust-stdlib-module-io-enumeration.md`](docs/surveys/rust-stdlib-module-io-enumeration.md).
- **A DEFAULT TYPE PARAMETER CAN CARRY A BOUNDARY, and the probe could not have seen it.** `std::collections::HashMap` came back with no signal, yet `HashMap::new()` returns `HashMap<K, V, RandomState>` and `RandomState`'s initialiser calls `crate::sys::random::hashmap_random_keys` — an OS entropy read, invisible to a text scan of `map.rs` because `RandomState` is defined in another module. It did **not** cost the grant, on a cross-language consistency argument rather than a judgement call: the shipped python catalogue already grants `secrets`, `random` **and** `uuid` on the recorded reasoning that OS-RNG seeding belongs to the row of the module that *performs* it (`os.urandom`), and `os.getrandom` was removed from `env_read` outright with "a CSPRNG read is not an environment read under any reading" — so this vocabulary has **no kind for entropy at all**. Refusing rust's hash collections on a read python grants would make `module_completeness` mean different things per language. A test pins the dependency, so if python's grants go, rust's rationale is re-argued rather than silently outliving its basis.
- **Thirteen modules are deliberately refused**, `std` itself foremost — granting the whole standard library would turn every unmatched `std` call into an examined negative. The clock modules are withheld for the *opposite* reason to the rest: WI-pavob ruled a clock read **is** `host_info_read`, so `std::time` and `std::time::SystemTime` need rows, not a no-I/O declaration (WI-tubij). And `std::ptr` is granted **protectively** as well as correctly: it publicly exports `read`, `write`, `copy` and `read_volatile`, names colliding exactly with I/O verbs, so the grant makes those calls an examined negative instead of leaving the spelling to be guessed at.
- **Measured A/B over 13 rust repositories, both arms, fresh cache per arm: NO verdict moved — and the blocker census is the actual result.** Of 36 claims across the 12 repos that completed both arms (arti timed out symmetrically and is excluded), the surviving `inconclusive` verdicts are held by: **a language with no I/O catalogue at all — 8**; **method-starvation — 4**; and the `module_completeness` rung these grants feed — **2**. Only 2 of 14 reach the rung this work addresses, and **both improved**: `rage`'s unadjudicable-module count falls **33 → 25**, with the residual being third-party crates, which are out of catalogue scope by standing ruling. A verdict-only A/B reports "0 moved", which reads as *this work was useless*; the count and the census say *it works, and other rungs fire first*, and those call for opposite next actions. A gate-level positive control pins the mechanism directly, because no cohort measurement can distinguish a working grant blocked upstream from a grant that does nothing.
- **The dominant blocker for rust turns out to be a manifest artifact, filed separately.** `_call_production_coverage` withholds on "language(s) toml made calls but have no I/O catalog", and those `toml` calls are Cargo `[[bin]]` target declarations — `toml:Cargo.toml:15-19:mini-redis-cli:binary -> rust:src/bin/cli.rs:main:function`. A manifest declaring an entrypoint is typed as a **call** by a language that cannot make one, and `defines_target` (registry-backed, already used for this by `xml_config.py` and pyproject) is emitted over the *same* targets in the same run. `fish`/perl/powershell are deliberately NOT folded in: those edges are genuine calls in real scripts that can perform I/O, so withholding there is INV-javam working as designed.
- **Seven bugs were found in the audit probe itself, every one producing a plausible wrong answer and none reporting an error.** Two were new here: a module can be a **file and a directory at once** (`std/src/fs.rs` beside `std/src/fs/tests.rs`, and the `is_dir()` test fired first — so `std::fs` and `std::process` were each adjudicated **against their own test file** and nothing else), and `crate::` was resolved to `std` regardless of the importing file's crate, so core-resident modules were checked against std's tree and refused for reaches they cannot make. Three of the fixes *narrow* what is scanned, which is the false-all-clear direction, so each was applied alone and measured across all 33 modules against 15 must-refuse and 6 must-candidate controls — and **that control set is why two of the fixes exist**: scoping a type to its own items alone turned `std::time::SystemTime` (reaches the clock through a file-level `use`) and `std::path::PathBuf` (derefs to `Path`, so `pb.read_dir()` is a PathBuf call site) into grantable candidates. Both are I/O; both would have been granted.

### Documentation

- **[I/O primitive kind-conformance sweep](docs/surveys/io-primitive-kind-conformance-sweep.md)** across all 947 `method`-kind entries in 9 catalogues, in three tiers. **Verified:** rust (7 miskinds, corrected) and python (2 miskinds, 95 clean, 46 explicitly refused — `file.*` pseudo-modules, `multiprocessing` factories, uninstalled django/flask). **Flagged not changed:** javascript — `fs.createWriteStream` is the sole method among 33 `fs` function siblings, and `Deno.{readFile,readTextFile,readDir,stat,lstat}` are declared both kinds in adjacent entries; starvation abstains for JS (INV-gijis) so neither can withhold. **REFUSED: 726 of 947** — kotlin 181, scala 162, java 139, objc 122, swift 97, go 25, no toolchain on this platform. Those are unexamined, not clean, and INV-suril already records the shape in java.
- **A language-agnostic signal for the refused tier**, since a probe cannot reach it: "split modules" (one module carrying both kinds) is how the Rust defect would have been visible *without reading Rust* — and it is **empty for every refused language**, which is itself informative. A second signal found 97 cross-language name disagreements; most inspected are genuine language differences, but `flush` is function-kind for `sys.stdout`/`sys.stderr` where it takes a receiver — an over-matching error in the opposite direction, recorded and not pursued.

- **ADR-0017's implementation-status markers were re-measured against the tree, and five of them were wrong (INV-lataj).** They carried a single date — *measured 2026-08-02* — and were never revisited after #188, #190, #192 and #197 landed in the six days following. The top-of-file Status line claimed §3a, §3c–3d, §4 and §7a each had **no production consumer**: §3a has **two** call sites, §3c–3d is live, and §4b has been live since #197. The §3a banner described `propagate_taint_ddg` as building `ddg_forward` and `tainted_at` and "reading neither" — **neither identifier occurs anywhere in `taint.py` any more**, so the description was not merely stale but unfalsifiable as written. Meanwhile the Phase 2 table row three hundred lines below was accurate and current, so the document disagreed with itself.
- **"Not implemented" and "implemented but unwired" are now named separately, because they instruct a reader to do different jobs.** §4a (`infer_summary`) and §7a (`is_field_tainted`) are both fully written, both tested, and both have zero production callers — the same dead-code-at-100%-coverage shape this ADR already documents for `_sink_module_compatible`. Calling that "not implemented" tells the next reader to write code that already exists.
- **No count is pinned in the replacement text.** The catalogue's size moved 33 → 108 → 113 across two edits in two days, and `taint.py`'s own docstring already carries a stale "38" for exactly that reason.

- **`verify-claims` now publishes what it cannot see, in a document a user will actually reach (WI-bovor, WI-tizig).** [`docs/VERIFY-CLAIMS-SCOPE.md`](docs/VERIFY-CLAIMS-SCOPE.md), linked from README and from SECURITY.md's own limitations section. The filing said the untyped-receiver limitation was documented five times in `verify_claims.py` and zero times for users; re-derived at tip, **the gap is wider than filed** — the tool emits **eight** caveat kinds and exactly **one** (`unknown_receiver_scope`) was named in any user-facing document, so a consumer reading `caveats[].kind` out of the JSON envelope got seven values that appeared nowhere in the documentation. All eight are now defined for a reader.
- **What the page had to say out loud, because a passing exit code is an assertion someone gates a pipeline on.** The verdict ladder and the exit-code contract in precedence order (`1` violated → `2` inconclusive *or a claims file that failed validation* → `3` caveated → `0` clean), with the two traps named: a gate written `verify-claims … || exit 1` **fails on exit 3**, which is deliberate and fail-closed; and `2` is not a pass, it means the tool did not check your claim.
- **Four limits on a clean verdict, each stated with its mechanism rather than as a hedge.** The shipped catalogues are **stdlib-scoped and third-party libraries are not detected transitively** — `requests`, `httpx` and `urllib3` have zero rows in the Python catalogue, and the reason is structural (hypergumbo analyses your repository, not `site-packages`, so there are no edges into a client's internals to follow down to the stdlib call it eventually makes); the ready-made overlays in `docs/io-primitives-overlays/` are pointed at. A language with no taint catalogue is **not verified**, and the INV-javam disclosure that says so is quoted. A `module_completeness` grant in an overlay is a **closed-world claim that turns a gate off**, and every grant is disclosed by module name (INV-tabaf). And `analysis_method` is **not a confidence score** — `ddg_mixed` has now scored below `structural` on three separate populations.
- **The §3a intraprocedural limit is published with a measured share, which is the half WI-tizig asked for.** A flow whose source is read at MODULE TOP LEVEL is anchored on the `file` node, has no enclosing function to build a CFG for, and can never be adjudicated by data dependence — it comes out `structural` no matter how capable the language's extractor is. Re-derived on measurement 0005's census: **10 of 37 situations (27.0%) and 17 of 170 rows (10.0%)**, and **all 10 are labelled `structural`**, which is the mechanism confirming itself. Concentration is very uneven (express 3 of 3, caddy 0 of 16). `dataflow_coverage` publishes capability per LANGUAGE; this limit is per-FLOW and applies to every language equally.
- **WI-tizig's other half has closed on its own and the item did not know.** Its 2026-08-06 filing measured 31 of 189 pooled rows (16.4%) as structurally un-adjudicable, of which **18 were `external_symbol`-anchored**. On the 0005 census that kind is **zero** — INV-rozob's fix gave those anchors a real path — so what remains is the `file` half alone. The number to carry forward is the `file` share, not the 16.4%.
- **What a `violated` verdict is worth is now on the page too**, with the measured figure and the instruction to treat it as a place to look rather than a finding: measurement 0005's 21.6% per situation / 10.0% per row, the three dominant false-positive mechanisms in order, and the reminder that precision says nothing about recall.

### Added

- **Measurement `0008` — [the §4a cross-function composition ceiling](docs/measurements/0008-the-cross-function-composition-ceiling.md), and the payoff splits by direction.** Measurement 0007 sized the BLOCKER (`cross_function` is 76.3% of never-ran walks) and named ADR-0017 §4 function summaries as the remedy. This sizes the REMEDY: **18.3% of cross-function findings** (778 of 4,245) meet the necessary conditions for a single-hop composed walk — per-repository median 22.3%, range 0%–100%, and beads alone is 62.4% of the pool, so the pooled figure is a pooled census and not the estimator (INV-duvup). New instrument `scripts/measure-cross-function-reach.py`.
- **For REFUTATION the payoff is bounded at zero, and §4a does not move it.** Removal authority can act only on `unconfirmed` — the walk ran and established no dependence. Across the same 11 repositories, of the 28 walks that ran, **14 confirmed / 14 escaped / 0 unconfirmed**. Composition creates running walks; on the observed split those are confirmations and escapes, not the class removal needs. **§4a is therefore not the gate on WI-kabif's removal arm** that 0007's framing implied.
- **For CONFIRMATION the effect is real and it INFLATES the output — a cost nobody had priced.** `analysis_method` is part of the collapse key and adjudicated flows bypass `collapse_unadjudicated_flows` entirely, so a newly-confirmed flow becomes **its own row** while the `ddg_mixed` group it left behind survives one member lighter. A situation of 40 members with 10 confirmations becomes 11 rows, not 1 improved one. At the ceiling that is up to 778 new `precise` rows on a cohort emitting 298 in total.
- **Three independent reconciliations against 0007**, which is why the instrument is believed: cross-function evidence rows total **106** against 0007's independently-reported 106; the verdict census reproduces 0007's result table **row for row** (14 / 0 / 14 / 139 / 131, 298 rows); and per repository the blockers reconcile to 0007's `not_attempted` column.
- **Depth is not headroom.** Only 21.9% of cross-function findings are one hop. The 56.4% "whole chain covered" figure should NOT be pursued — jaeger's 5-hop route runs from a `_test.go` helper through `internal/tenancy` into `examples/hotrod`, and each extra hop multiplies exposure to call-graph false edges. Go is 94.2% of findings, so this cohort is substantially a Go measurement; rust and typescript return a structural zero.
- **Measurements `0006` and `0007` were never added to the [measurement index](docs/measurements/README.md).** Both are indexed now, along with `0008`.

- **Measurement `0007` — [the ADR-0017 §7a addressable domain is zero on this corpus](docs/measurements/0007-the-section-7a-addressable-domain.md).** Of 153 `ddg_mixed` evidence rows across 11 repositories, **zero** rest on a walk that ran and established no dependence. 90.8% rest on a walk that **never ran**; the remaining 9.2% on a walk that ran and **lost track of the value**. `unconfirmed` is zero in *every* repository, not merely in aggregate. Every removal §7a would authorise under the "drop every `ddg_mixed`" pricing is removal-on-ignorance.
- **WI-kabif's pre-registered tripwire was right, and the +6.3-point figure was priced on the wrong field** — not an upper bound in need of refinement. The item's own words: *"Addressable domain is therefore about 18 percent of flows. If a future measurement reports a much larger number, suspect the measurement."* It reported 26.8%; suspecting the measurement is what found this.
- **Why the walk did not run, measured rather than assumed:** `cross_function` 76.3%, `source_not_tracked` 17.3%, `sink_before_source` 6.5% of the 139 rows. These **partition** the population — a remedy for the top blocker promotes flows to the next one, not to the walk. §3a is intraprocedural by construction, and WI-kabif predicted this from a different cohort in advance (*"69 percent of flows have source and sink in different functions"*); 76.3% is that number measured a second way. The design call is answered **neither build nor retire**: §7a is blocked on §4 function summaries, its own discussion's blocker 5, not on labelling.

### Fixed

- **`auto-pr` silently overwrote a hand-extended test manifest, so CI ran one test file while the commit that shipped listed twenty.** During the push it regenerates `.ci/affected-tests.txt` from the slicer and, if the result differs from the committed file, `git add`s it into an amended commit. That is an unconditional overwrite. The slicer has no data-file→test edge (**INV-bigaz**), so a change to `io_primitives/*.yaml` — or any other pure-data input — slices to roughly **one** test file; an author who deliberately widens the manifest to the tests that actually exercise the changed data had it narrowed back during the push. **The overwrite is invisible from the author's side** — the manifest is extended, committed correctly, and replaced afterwards — which is why it survived. Found by reading a CI log that said `1 selected files` for a commit whose own manifest listed twenty, then confirmed across **three merged catalogue PRs: every one shipped a 1-file manifest** while its notes recorded a working 19-file extension.
- **This is WI-buhov's bug one directory over, and it takes WI-buhov's remedy.** `auto-pr` used to restore backed-up tracker `.ops` over the working tree unconditionally and silently ate concurrent writes; `_ops_union_restore_file` fixed it by making the restore a **union**. The manifest path had the identical shape and no such guard. New `_manifest_union` sits directly beside it in `scripts/lib/forgejo-api.sh` — pure (no git, no network), so it is testable by sourcing the library exactly as its sibling is. **The regen may still ADD** — catching a stale manifest is its real job and that is preserved — **but it can never DROP.** Paths that no longer exist on disk are still dropped, so a deleted test cannot be resurrected into CI, and auto-pr now prints how many committed entries it kept that the slicer did not select.
- **The same prefix mistake survived in the reporting path, and a diagnostic that cannot fire is worse than none.** The merge path was fixed to select by section, but auto-pr's "kept N entries" line still counted with `grep -c '^packages/'` — which is **0** for a manifest of root-level `tests/...` entries, the common shape. So the one line that makes a silent narrowing *visible* never printed, and its absence reads exactly like "nothing needed preserving". Counting now goes through `_manifest_test_count`, which reads the SELECTED_TESTS section like every other reader. Confirmed against the real manifest: `sliced=1 final=95` now prints **`kept 94 committed manifest entries the slicer did not select`**, where it previously printed nothing at all.

- **A filter that removes everything is not believed.** The existence check is cwd-relative, matching the caller's long-standing assumption that `auto-pr` runs from the repo root (the surrounding `grep` and `git add` on the same path already assume it). Were that ever to break, every path would fail `-f`, the manifest would empty, and **CI would run nothing while reporting success** — the silent-loss failure this function exists to prevent, reintroduced one level down. An empty filter result therefore falls back to the unfiltered union and lets CI complain about a missing path instead of passing vacuously.

### Added

- **`std::env` and `std::process` are audited, and unlike the rest of this arc these rows are LIVE (WI-dadog).** WI-gihos measured that the rust catalogue matches **zero** method-kind primitives across 11,691 call edges — receivers arrive unresolved, so `gate_named_entry` correctly refuses them (INV-linub L3/L4). **Function-kind rows are the ones that fire**, and `std::env` is almost entirely free functions. Measured A/B across the 42-repo cohort: **`std::env::temp_dir` newly classifies 3 edges** as `host_info_read` in 2 repositories (ripgrep, y-sweet). Small, and stated as small — but it is the first row in this arc to move an edge at all. The `std::process` **method** rows are inert today for exactly WI-gihos's reason, and that is said here rather than found later. Starvation delta: nothing freed, nothing stranded, despite four new method-only modules.
- **Every boundary is fixed by cross-language precedent rather than chosen.** `python.yaml` already rules `os.putenv` / `os.unsetenv` / `os.chdir` as `env_write`, `os.kill` as `ipc_send` ("signalling another process"), `os.wait` / `waitpid` as `ipc_recv` ("reaping a child collects its exit status — data received from another process"), and `tempfile.gettempdir` as `host_info_read`. **Added:** rust's first `env_write` section (`set_var`, `remove_var`, `set_current_dir`), `vars_os` under `env_read`, `temp_dir` / `home_dir` under `host_info_read`, `Child.{kill}` as `ipc_send` and `Child.{wait, try_wait, wait_with_output}` as `ipc_recv`, plus the `ChildStdin` / `ChildStdout` / `ChildStderr` pipe types carrying their trait methods on the concrete type per WI-gihos's rule. **`set_current_dir` was promised in advance**: rust.yaml's own note read *"chdir has no row here: python rules it env_write, and rust's std::env::set_current_dir is not yet audited."*
- **`std::thread` stays absent entirely, and the judgment is recorded with the argument that carries it because no cross-language answer exists.** `std::thread` reads `RUST_MIN_STACK` through `env::var` when it spawns; the item asked whether that makes `thread::spawn` an `env_read` boundary for a caller who never names a variable. **No.** The decisive reason is discrimination, not precedent: the read is **internal** — it sizes the new thread's stack and its value never reaches the caller — so if it counted, locale lookups, `TZ`, `RUST_BACKTRACE` and a long tail of stdlib machinery would all qualify, and `env_read` would stop distinguishing "this code reads configuration" from "this code runs on a stdlib that does". **A boundary that everything satisfies classifies nothing.** Supporting: rust.yaml already declared concurrency primitives absent, and the owner's boundary test (*"is it changing state outside of itself"*) put a thread inside the process, as WI-pavob applied to locks. **python is silent, not supportive** — `threading` appears only in its `stdlib_modules` name list, with no rows and no completeness entry — and that is stated plainly rather than dressed up as agreement.
- **A fourth probe bug, and like the first three it returned a plausible wrong answer rather than an error.** The receiver regex required a leading `&`, so `fn wait_with_output(mut self)` — and **46 other by-value receivers in `process.rs`** — read as associated *functions*. Kind is precisely what decides matchability, so that would have shipped `wait_with_output` as a function-kind row no method call could ever match (INV-nular). Re-checked against WI-gihos's 70 already-merged rows: **none was affected**, because the by-value names there (`by_ref`, `bytes`, `chain`, `take`) were all refused as adapters anyway.
- **Refusals, each with its reason and its test:** `std::process::id` and `Child::id` (python deliberately holds `os.getpid` out); `Command`'s builder setters `env` / `env_clear` / `env_remove` / `envs` / `current_dir` / `arg` / `stdin` / `stdout` / `stderr` (they mutate a builder in *this* process and `spawn` is already rowed — WI-bupor's `OpenOptionsExt` rule); `ExitStatus` accessors (reading an already-collected status); `Stdio` constructors; `join_paths` / `split_paths` (pure `OsString` manipulation); and `thread::available_parallelism` (python holds `os.cpu_count` out for the same reason it holds `getpid` out).

- **`std::io` is audited, and the rule the audit produced is about WHERE A ROW MAY BE KEYED (WI-gihos).** The item was filed to decide what a wrapper like `BufReader` inherits from the reader it wraps; enumerating the module — 556 items across 15 files — answered a structural question first. **A row's module must be a type receiver inference can produce, and three kinds cannot be.** A **trait** cannot: no Rust value has type `Read`, so `gate_named_entry`'s refusal of method-kind hits without a module hint can never be satisfied — measured, every edge whose slot is `std::io::Read` / `::Write` / `::BufRead` across the 42-repo cohort carries the name `module`, a `use` import, never a call. A **wrapper** cannot: its boundary is whatever it wraps, and in `rage` **8 of ~10 `BufReader::new` sites wrap an in-memory byte slice**, so an `fs_read` row would be *majority-wrong on the first real repository to exercise it*. A **generic helper** cannot: `io::copy`'s measured call site is `fn write_output<R: io::Read, W: io::Write>(input: R, output: W)`, whose boundary is unknowable rather than merely unknown. So `Read` / `Seek` / `BufRead`, the wrapper types, `copy`, `read_to_string`, and the `empty` / `repeat` / `sink` null devices all get **no rows**, each refusal pinned by a test rather than by prose. `std::io::pipe()` is absent as descriptor plumbing that moves no data — python.yaml's precedent for `os.pipe` — but `PipeReader` / `PipeWriter` are concrete types whose bytes genuinely cross, so their read and write families are rowed `ipc_recv` / `ipc_send`, the way python rows the matching `os.read` rather than leaving a pipe read invisible.
- **The trait methods go on the concrete type, which `std::net::TcpStream` already did and `std::fs::File` did not.** `File` implements `Read`, `Write` and `Seek` and carried **none** of their methods, while `TcpStream` carried `read` / `read_to_end` / `write` / `write_all` — one catalogue answering one question two ways. `python.yaml` settles it the same way, putting `read` / `write` on WI-fuvuj's synthetic `file` module rather than on `io.IOBase`. **Added:** `File.{read, read_vectored, read_to_end, read_to_string, read_exact}` as `fs_read` and `File.{write, write_vectored, write_all, write_fmt}` as `fs_write`, plus the four concrete stdio handles and their `Lock` variants (`Stdin`/`StdinLock` → `ipc_recv`, `Stdout`/`Stderr` and locks → `logging`), because `.lock()` moves no data and without those rows a read through `stdin().lock()` was the same gap one level down.
- **These 49 rows are correct and INERT, and both halves are stated rather than one discovered later.** Across **11,691 Rust call edges in 42 repositories the catalogue matches zero method-kind primitives** — every match it makes is function-kind. The 78 genuine trait-method call edges (46 `write_all`, 15 `read_to_end`, 10 `read_to_string`, 6 `read_line`) all arrive as `rust:external:0-0:<name>:external_symbol` with a null `dst_ref`, which `gate_named_entry` rightly refuses. **That is INV-linub L3/L4, and it makes every method row in `rust.yaml` dead today — `TcpStream`'s included, so a file read and a socket read are equally invisible.** Adding correct rows does not change that and is not claimed to: measured both ways, the change frees no module, strands none, and reclassifies no edge. What it buys is a catalogue that is true and self-consistent, live the moment receiver evidence is. The Rust row of INV-linub's per-language exposure table is now measured at findings level rather than edge level.
- **`std::fs::File`'s refusals carry their reasons:** `seek` / `rewind` / `seek_relative` / `stream_position` are absent because a syscall that moves no data changes nothing outside the process — the rule that already excluded `try_clone`; `flush` is absent by `python.yaml`'s measured precedent, where a `flush` row made every bare `.flush()` in runtime code read as a direct unwrapped fs-write; `by_ref` / `bytes` / `chain` / `take` build an adapter; and `read_buf` / `read_array` / `write_all_vectored` are unstable in rustc 1.94.0. **`is_terminal` is the one genuinely borderline call and is recorded as such rather than settled quietly:** it is `isatty(2)`, which moves no data and changes nothing outside the process — the same rule that already excludes `try_clone` — but `python.yaml` rows `os.isatty` and `os.ttyname` under `fs_read`, so the cross-language answer points the other way. The refusal wins only because the Rust spelling is a *trait* and the concrete-type spelling would have to assert `fs_read` on `Stdout`, whose boundary is `logging`. Both sides are written into the catalogue header so the next reader can overturn it without re-deriving it.

- **`std::os::*::fs` is in scope, and the exclusion that kept it out was flagging one way of creating a symlink while ignoring another (WI-bupor, owner ruling).** The catalogue header excluded `std::os::*` as *"raw-fd extensions"*. That noun is right for what it names — `AsRawFd` / `FromRawFd` / `IntoRawFd` hand over a descriptor number and reach no syscall, and they stay out — but the exclusion was being read as covering the whole namespace, and those modules also carry plain filesystem mutation. **`std::fs::soft_link` was catalogued while `std::os::unix::fs::symlink` was not**, and a Linux program calling `chroot` or `chown` through this module read as doing nothing at all there. **Added:** unix — `symlink`, `chown`, `fchown`, `lchown`, `chroot`, and `FileExt`'s `read_at` / `read_exact_at` / `write_at` / `write_all_at`; windows — `symlink_file`, `symlink_dir`, `FileExt::seek_read` / `seek_write`.
- **Every boundary is fixed by cross-language precedent rather than chosen here.** `python.yaml` already rules `os.chroot`, `os.chown` / `lchown` / `fchown`, `os.symlink` / `os.link` and `os.pwrite` as `fs_write`, and `os.chdir` as `env_write`. The rust rows follow python rather than re-litigating each name, because **a catalogue that disagrees with itself across languages is the defect INV-nular's sweep exists to find**. `chroot`'s primary effect is on the calling process, and it is `fs_write` on that precedent with the reasoning recorded rather than left implicit.
- **Most of `std::os::unix::fs` by method count is not I/O, and saying so is the point of a total-surface audit.** The `MetadataExt` / `PermissionsExt` / `FileTypeExt` / `DirEntryExt` accessors read an already-fetched `stat` struct; `OpenOptionsExt` and `DirBuilderExt` are builder setters. All are refused and pinned by tests, alongside the unstable names (`mkfifo`, `junction_point`, `seek_read_buf`, `read_buf_at`, the vectored family) — a module can look far larger than its I/O surface, and the refusals are what distinguishes the two.
- **Trait methods are catalogued under the trait, following the `std::io::Write` precedent, and deliberately not added to `ambiguous_names`.** The names are distinctive enough to match without a module hint, and rust method edges mostly carry an unresolved receiver slot (INV-linub), so requiring a hint would make them unmatchable in practice.
- **Measured, with the same instrument and the same controls:** 13 rows added, and across the 42 reusable surveys the starved-module set is unchanged in every repository — nothing freed, nothing newly starved. Instrument shown live: 9 of 42 surveys carry a non-empty starved set and `std::path::Path` starves in exactly 3 of 3 Rust repositories. The two free-function modules carry no methods, so they cannot enter the starvation population at all; the two trait modules are method-only, and every name they carry is a method, so any real call into them satisfies them.
- **Correction to the previous entry:** it said `fs_write` was *"the only one of the nine kinds"* that could carry the `lock` family. **There are eighteen boundary kinds, not nine.** The decision is unchanged and was re-checked against the full set — `ipc_send` implies a message, which a lock is not, and `env_write` is process-state mutation — but the stated reason was wrong.

### Added

- **`std::fs` was in the catalogue and catalogued at half, so it never appeared on any "unexamined module" list (WI-bupor).** The total-surface audit this item sets as its bar — every name the module exposes, not the ones a repository happens to call — found **20 missing stable I/O primitives** across six modules. `std::fs::File` carried three associated functions (`open` / `create` / `create_new`) and **not one of its eleven stable methods**; `std::fs::DirBuilder` and `std::fs::DirEntry` were absent entirely; `std::path` had no rows at all. **Catalogue presence is not catalogue coverage, and only a total-surface audit can tell them apart.**
- **Added:** `std::fs` — `exists`, `read_link`, `set_permissions`. `std::fs::File` — `metadata`, `set_len`, `set_permissions`, `set_times`, `sync_all`, `sync_data`, and the five-name `lock` family. `std::fs::DirBuilder` — `create`. `std::fs::DirEntry` — `metadata`, `file_type`. `std::path::Path` — `try_exists`, `is_symlink`. `std::path` — `absolute`.
- **Every name was read out of the installed toolchain with stability and receiver shape checked per entry, not recalled**, because the python stdlib audit's unaided first draft got 5 of 32 verdicts wrong and every error was on the false-all-clear side. Seven nightly-only APIs were excluded (`fs::set_times`, `fs::set_times_nofollow`, `fs::set_permissions_nofollow`, `File::open_buffered`, `File::create_buffered`, `Dir::open`, `Dir::open_file`). **The receiver check corrected a filed entry:** `DirBuilder::create` takes `&self` and is a METHOD, not the associated function it had been recorded as — and a kind is not cosmetic, since a receiverless callee declared a method is unmatchable by construction (INV-nular).
- **Two probe bugs were found producing this list, both returning a plausible wrong answer rather than an error.** An impl-tracking regex attributed sixteen module-level functions to `impl DirEntry`, re-derived using indentation as the discriminator. A signature regex required `(` immediately after the function name, so every generic `pub fn exists<P: AsRef<Path>>(..)` was silently skipped — which is why `DirBuilder::create` first appeared absent from the toolchain altogether.
- **The refusals are pinned by tests, not just recorded in prose.** `Path::is_absolute` resolves to `path.has_root()` and is purely lexical — it was a false positive of the I/O-reach regex, caught by disbelief rather than by any error. `Metadata` / `Permissions` / `FileType` accessors and `OpenOptions` builder setters are in-memory. **`File::try_clone` is `dup(2)`: a syscall, but nothing crosses and nothing outside the process changes, so it fails the same boundary test that ruled `std::sync::Mutex` out.** A test fails if a later auditor "fixes" any of them.
- **The `lock` family is `fs_write`, chosen deliberately rather than conveniently.** `flock` / `LockFileEx` changes no byte of the file, so `fs_write` overstates it — but a lock is observable to another *process*, which is the boundary test `std::sync::Mutex` failed, and it is the one case in that discussion which passes. Omitting it is a false all-clear; `fs_write` is the only one of the nine kinds that carries it without a new heavyweight axis, and the residual over-claim can only mislead a program that opens read-only and locks. `std::path::absolute` is `fs_read` because it delegates to `env::current_dir()` — `getcwd(2)` — matching the boundary already assigned to `std::env::current_dir`.
- **Measured before shipping, because a correct row is exactly what used to regress a verdict.** Across the 42 reusable surveys, both arms driving the production loader and the production predicate: **20 rows added, and the starved-module set is unchanged in every repository — nothing freed, nothing newly starved.** The instrument is shown live rather than assumed: 9 of the 42 surveys carry a non-empty starved set, and `std::path::Path` starves in exactly 3 of 3 Rust repositories, which reproduces the independently recorded figure. `std::fs::File` becomes mixed-kind here, and it is INV-soval that keeps a `File::open` call satisfying it — a test fails if that fix is ever reverted underneath these rows.

### Fixed

- **The blind-language starvation gate PENALISED CORRECT CATALOGUING, so the incentive it created was to under-catalogue (INV-soval).** `method_starved_modules` is MODULE-granular but tests a PER-KIND property: it collected every module carrying any `method`-kind primitive and marked one satisfied only on a `call_construct == "method"` edge. A module may legitimately declare both kinds — `std::fs::File` carries the associated functions `open` / `create` alongside the methods `metadata` / `sync_all` / the `lock` family — and such a module could never be satisfied by a function-construct call the catalogue matched *exactly*. Measured on the shipped catalogue: adding ONE correct `std::fs::File` method row flips a repo that merely opens a file from a clean verdict to a withheld one. The predicate's own docstring states the intent the code did not implement — *"the catalogue was never given anything it could match"* — and for a mixed module it **was** given something it could match. PR #552 created three such modules itself, by splitting rust's miskinded associated functions out of `methods:`: that fixed the MATCH and left the COVERAGE broken.
- **A construct test alone cannot see the case, because `call_construct` IS NOT STAMPED ON AN ASSOCIATED-FUNCTION CALL TO AN EXTERNAL TYPE.** Measured across three real Rust repositories: every edge into `std::fs::File`, `std::process::Command` and `std::path::Path` carries `call_construct: None`, while the same repositories stamp it on thousands of others (ripgrep: 917 `method` and 12 `function` among 1,156 external call edges). The language therefore clears the abstention check on its other edges and arrives at this one with nothing to test. So satisfaction now has a **second route — the called NAME matching a function-kind row** — which is the evidence that survives an unstamped edge, and it discriminates precisely where construct cannot: `Command::new` matches a function-kind row, while `Path::new` does not, because `Path::new` performs no I/O and is correctly absent.
- **Loosening a withholding gate is the false-all-clear direction, so it was measured across the corpus before it shipped, not argued.** On 42 real repositories spanning the surveyed languages: **6 modules freed today, 7 once WI-bupor lands**, `new=[]` in every repository — the change frees modules and strands none — and **every freed module is mixed-kind**, so no method-only module moved. The blind-language population the gate exists for is entirely method-only in both the kotlin and java catalogues, asserted by a test rather than assumed, so this change cannot reach it. `std::path::Path` still starves in 3 of 3 rust repos and `java.io.File`'s Kotlin constructor still starves — both correctly, the former because it is INV-linub's slot-`external` L3 defect surfacing at the coverage gate rather than anything this fix addresses.
- **The name route is bounded by tests that must still starve, and the bound is the point.** An uncatalogued name on an unstamped edge starves; a name catalogued as a METHOD on an unstamped edge starves, because the name index is built from function-kind rows only and an unstamped edge naming a method is exactly the blind-analyzer shape (a receiver existed and the analyzer could not see it); and the per-language abstention still outranks both routes, so no JavaScript or TypeScript repo starts being reported. The route was added after the first fix proved insufficient, and its tests were added after the **coverage gate caught that not one existing test executed it** — every test in the mixed-kind class stamps `call_construct`, so the route whose entire justification is unstamped edges had never run.

- **A `use` statement stopped counting as an attribute read — for every spelling this time, not just the one the repro used (INV-pusin, second closure).** The item was marked `satisfied` in PR #504 against a fixture spelled `use a::b::c;`, and that is precisely the one form the fix handled. `skip_context_kinds` matches the **proximate** syntactic context, but tree-sitter-rust wraps the braced, wildcard and aliased forms in `scoped_use_list` / `use_wildcard` / `use_as_clause`, so the context reported for `std::io` in `use std::io::{self, Write};` is the *wrapper* — never `use_declaration`. **Six of the fourteen `use` spellings still emitted `module_attr_ref` edges**, and the coverage gate counts those as calls into a module whose I/O "was never examined". A closure satisfied the repro and not the statement.
- **`use std::io::*;` put a BARE `std` on the uncatalogued-module list, which is unclearable by construction.** Enumeration cannot answer it: `module_completeness: complete` asserts that *every* I/O primitive a module exposes is catalogued, and nobody can audit the whole Rust standard library. So every Rust repository carried a withholding blocker that no amount of `module_completeness` work could ever remove — which is why this had to land before the stdlib enumeration campaign (WI-lutuh) rather than beside it.
- **Matched by ANCESTRY rather than by three more strings in the tuple.** New `skip_ancestor_kinds` on `emit_module_attribute_refs` tests every ancestor instead of the proximate one, and rust passes `("use_declaration",)`.
