The three wrappers are today's spelling of the invariant, not the invariant; the invariant the item ratified is *a path anywhere inside an import declaration is an import*, and stating it directly is closed over grammar shapes nobody has enumerated. `use_declaration` moved out of `skip_context_kinds`, so the fact keeps one home. The parameter is opt-in and empty by default, so cpp, javascript, java and go are byte-unchanged.
- **A form enumeration, with negative controls and a grammar guard.** `_USE_FORMS` covers fourteen spellings; two negative controls assert a genuine value read (`std::env::consts::OS`) still emits, including in a file that also carries the suppressed shapes — without them the enumeration is satisfied by an analyzer that stopped emitting attribute reads altogether. A third test fails CI if the grammar declares a `use_*` node kind no row exercises, which is exactly how the first closure missed five forms. It already earned its keep: it caught `use_bounds` (Rust 1.82 precise-capture, `impl Sized + use<T>`), which shares the keyword and is not an import.

- **`merge-pr` printed a `codeberg.org` PR link on a GitHub remote — wrong in both host and path.** `origin` is `git@github.com:iterabloom/hypergumbo.git` and the API layer already routed correctly (`detect_forge_backend` → `api.github.com`); only the display strings were never told. This was ONE FACT IN TWO HOMES with the wrong one winning: `auto-pr` carried a correct private `_autopr_pr_web_url` branching on `FORGE_BACKEND`, while `merge-pr` hardcoded `https://codeberg.org/$REPO_SLUG/pulls/$n` in two places. GitHub's web path is `/pull/N` **singular**, so the emitted links were wrong twice over.
- **`pr_web_url()` now lives in `scripts/lib/forgejo-api.sh`**, which `auto-pr` and `merge-pr` both already source; the private copy is deleted and all five call sites repointed. The non-GitHub branch **derives its host from `API_BASE`** instead of naming `codeberg.org` — previously a self-hosted Forgejo user was handed a link to somebody else's server. Verified live: `github → /pull/552`, `codeberg → /pulls/552`, `git.example.org → /pulls/552`.
- **Why the silence lasted: nothing checks a printed link.** The API worked, CI was green, and the only symptom was a human clicking through to the wrong forge. Pinned executably by `test_no_script_hardcodes_a_forge_host_in_a_pr_link`, which fails if either script reintroduces a literal host.

- **The WI-bunag closure guard now tells you not to narrate its own rejection in the corrected message.** Earned twice in one sitting: a PR was refused for a bare `Fixes <ID>` on a still-open item, and the corrected commit *explained the rejection and quoted the offending phrase while calling it a mistake* — which re-matched the same regex verbatim and burned a second CI cycle. `\b(?:closes|fixes|resolves)\b[\s:]+<ID>` has no notion of quotation marks or of a sentence disapproving of itself.
- **The load-bearing half is a pre-push check**, not the prose: the guard fires at MERGE time, after CI has already run, so a message defect costs a full cycle every time. The rejection now ends with `git log -1 --format=%B | grep -inE '(closes|fixes|resolves)[[:space:]:]+(WI|INV|META)-'`, which costs nothing and runs before the push. Pinned by `test_rejection_tells_you_not_to_narrate_it_in_the_rewrite`.

- **A Rust associated function catalogued as a method withheld every verdict on any repo that opens a file (INV-nular).** rust.yaml declared seven receiverless callees as `methods:` — `std::fs::File.open/create/create_new`, `std::net::TcpStream.connect`, `std::net::TcpListener.bind`, `std::net::UdpSocket.bind`, `std::process::Command.new`. `File::open(path)` takes no receiver, so its call site can only ever produce a function-construct edge, and the consequence was not a mislabelled finding: (a) `io_boundary`'s method-kind gate could never match it, so the whole `std::fs::File` half of the fs catalogue was unreachable **by construction**, and (b) `verify_claims.method_starved_modules` saw a resolved call into a method-keyed module with no method-construct edge, concluded the analysis "did not look", and withheld the verdict.
- **The tool was resolving the call site perfectly and withholding anyway.** Measured live on `encrypted-dns-server` (rust+toml only): the edge is `rust:std::fs::File:0-0:open:external_symbol` — module and name both correct — and all 7 generic claims returned `inconclusive` naming `std::fs::File` structurally invisible. Re-kinding `std::fs::File` alone dropped the starved list from **two modules to one** on the same binary.
- **The fix is a SPLIT, not a move**, and a blanket correction would have broken it: `TcpStream::connect` is an associated function while `TcpStream::write_all` is a method under the same module, and `OpenOptions` legitimately carries both (`::new()` associated, `.open()` method) so it is deliberately untouched. Controls asserting the method half ship beside the subjects.
- **python.yaml carried the same error twice** — `pathlib.Path.cwd` and `.home` are **classmethods**, verified with `inspect.getattr_static` rather than from recall. Milder: `pathlib.Path` also carries genuine instance methods, so a repo calling any of them marks the module satisfied and nothing is withheld.
- **A Rust typed receiver whose type is EXTERNAL now keeps its type (INV-linub L3).** The typed-receiver strategies resolve `Type::method` against first-party symbols only, so an external receiver type missed, `resolved` stayed False, and the unresolved path rebuilt the module slot from `use_aliases` alone — where a method name never appears. The receiver type computed moments earlier was dropped on the floor. Measured on the same repo: `external` module slots on method-construct edges fall 203 → 194 of 219, and stdlib slots rise 0 → 2. **Deliberately narrow** — it changes the module slot only, never which edges are emitted.
- **Measured through L4 on findings, as INV-linub requires, and the honest result is a null**: zero findings and zero verdicts move on that repo. The `File`/`Path` call sites take associated-function and call-expression receiver shapes the identifier-only branch does not cover.

- **The coverage gate counts REFERENCES, not PARSE CANDIDATES (INV-hukuf).** `emit_module_attribute_refs(scoped_path=True)` walks a scoped path left-recursively and emits one edge per nesting depth, because the module/attribute split is syntactically ambiguous and the emitter cannot consult the catalogue — `std::env::consts::OS` is module `std::env` with attribute `consts::OS` OR module `std::env::consts` with attribute `OS`. That is right for MATCHING and a category error for coverage accounting: at most one proposal per read can ever match, so every genuine N-deep read was guaranteed to contribute **N-1 entries naming modules nothing ever called into**. Measured on a zero-dependency crate whose only scoped read is `let os = std::env::consts::OS;`: **`inconclusive` (rc 2) → `confirmed_with_caveats` (rc 3)**, with `std` and `std::env::consts` — the collateral of the read that matched — no longer reported.
- **A prefix test, not a line key, and that is what keeps it from over-suppressing.** Two distinct scoped reads can share a line (`f(std::env::consts::OS, std::fs::File::open)`), and a rule keyed on `(src, line)` alone would let one matching vouch for the other, silently removing a real uncatalogued module. The proposals of one read are not merely co-located, they are NESTED, so the sibling test is a component-wise prefix over MODULE slots — `std::envy` is not a prefix of `std::env::consts`, and `std::fs` does not vouch for `std::env`.
- **The module slot, not the name slot, and it was measured the wrong way round first.** On the analyzer's own edges the name slot carries the whole path (`std::env::consts::OS`); by the time the coverage gate runs, WI-pubiv's boundary-id remap has rewritten it to the bare leaf (`OS` / `consts` / `env`) — exactly the shape where a prefix test finds nothing. Written against the name slot, reproduced against a live survey, read as inert, corrected.

- **A disjunctive module slot is now expanded on BOTH consumer paths, so C++ coverage stops being *impossible* (INV-zimud).** `cpp.py` sets an unresolved call's module slot to the comma-joined list of every `#include` in the calling file and documents the contract in its own comment — *"downstream consumers may split the module_hint on commas"*. Exactly one of the two consumers honoured it: `io_boundary` splits the slot to CLASSIFY, while the coverage gate handed the whole joined string to `module_io_is_enumerated`, where it is a synthetic pseudo-module that **no `module_completeness` entry can ever match**. Measured over the WI-lutuh sweep on unmodified upstream repos: 19,273 of 81,711 C/C++ external dsts carry a comma (libzmq 21.7%, plasma-desktop 40.3%, shaka-packager 29.5%; all three C repos 0.0%). A contract stated in a producer comment is not enforced.
- **ALL, not ANY, and the two paths differ on purpose.** Classification asks *does any spelling name a primitive*, where an ANY answer is a positive claim. The gate asks *was the surface this call could have come from enumerated*, where a non-match is informative only if EVERY possible home was enumerated — one unenumerated disjunct leaves the call genuinely unexamined, and ANY would let a single enumerated header vouch for a file that also includes twenty that are not. Both callers derive from one `module_hint_disjuncts`, so they cannot drift about what a disjunct *is* — which is the failure this contract already suffered once.
- **The unknown is reported per disjunct.** `string,sys/socket.h,ws2tcpip.h` names nothing a reader can act on; `sys/socket` does.
- **What it does not do, stated because the number is zero:** no C or C++ module is enumerated today — both catalogues mention `module_completeness` only in a comment — so on the shipped catalogues this changes no verdict. What changes is that C++ coverage stops being **impossible** and becomes merely **unmet**, which is the state WI-lutuh's enumeration campaign can act on. An overlay test demonstrates the difference in both directions: enumerate all three includes and the call is covered; enumerate two and it is still withheld, naming the third.

- **An I/O primitive's return no longer inherits its argument's taint (INV-fumod).** The 0001 rubric's own tie-break made executable — *taint flows through in-program computation, not through an external resource selected by the tainted value*. An I/O primitive's return comes from the OTHER SIDE of the boundary: a handle on a resource the argument merely NAMED, or bytes the argument merely ADDRESSED. Derived from the catalogue, not curated: every I/O primitive is already enumerated per language, so a hand-written list of "opening" calls would be a second home for that fact.
- **Measured with a control that discriminates.** `out = open(args.outfile, "w"); out.write("a constant banner")` reported **two** findings, `builtins.open` and `file.write`, where only the first is earned — nothing tainted is written, and the tool was already internally inconsistent about it, naming `open` correctly and *then* crediting the handle as well. The write's confirmation is now withdrawn. The control `out = open("/tmp/fixed.txt", "w"); out.write(args.payload)` keeps its confirmation, because there the tainted value reaches the write's own argument.
- **And it is INERT on the 0006 cohort — byte-identical across all 11 repositories — which is the result that matters more than the fix.** §3a is confirm-only, so what the rule withdraws is the false claim of PRECISION (INV-sadah's concern), never the finding; inclusion still rests on call-graph reachability, which this does not touch. And the cohort's instances are not reachable by any catalogue rule: jaeger's refuted situation is `with open(args.output,'w') as f: f.write(summary)` where `summary` came from `parse_diff_file(args.diff)`, so the external read happens **inside a first-party callee**. Three independent lines of work — §7a's addressable domain, the walk's reach, and this precision rule — now terminate at the same place, ADR-0017 **§4 function summaries**.

- **`analysis_method` no longer has to answer two questions at once (INV-zidur).** It says *which analysis produced this finding*; it was also being asked *what did the walk conclude*, and it cannot — the call site collapses the walk's three-valued return with `is True`, and the label is then chosen on `fn_has_ddg`, *did the DDG cover the source function*. `TaintFlowFinding.walk_verdict` now records the walk's own result (`confirmed` / `unconfirmed` / `escaped` / `not_attempted` / `unavailable`), and `walk_blocked_by` names the first guard that stopped it. Both reach the `verify-claims` JSON evidence rows.
- **The filing named two facts under `ddg_mixed`; there are three, and the third is the one that matters.** Every guard above the walk — same-function, a recorded sink call line, a tracked source def, sink lexically after source — can fail with `fn_has_ddg` still true, and the finding is stamped `ddg_mixed` anyway. So the label means *the DDG covered the function*, not *the walk looked and found nothing*.
- **Consuming the walk's `False` required INV-lupav's forfeit gate, and the guard that says so fired on the first cut.** `test_taint_refutation_gate_contract` asserts a structural rule: a site that CONSUMES `_ddg_taint_reaches`'s `False` must pass `forfeit_refutation`; a site that COLLAPSES it with `is True` need not, because `False` is then indistinguishable from `None`. This site used to collapse and now consumes — `walk_verdict` distinguishes `unconfirmed` from `escaped` — so an unearned `False` (the walk exhausted only because a later use sits in a construct the extractor does not model) would have been published as *"the walk looked everywhere and found nothing"*, exactly the claim INV-lupav says is not earned. The gate is now wired to the §3a arm; measured, it moves nothing on this corpus, which is the expected result given `unconfirmed` is zero.
- **A separate field, not a fourth `analysis_method` value.** The precedent INV-zidur cites (splitting `structural` out of `ddg_mixed`) separated two different *analyses*; this separates one analysis's *results*, a different axis, and folding a verdict into a method name would repeat the one-name-two-facts shape being fixed. `analysis_method`'s published vocabulary is unchanged, so every `ddg_mixed` in measurement 0006 and in `docs/VERIFY-CLAIMS-SCOPE.md` still says what it said. **Nothing is acted on:** §3a stays confirm-only and no verdict moves.

- **Writing to the bit bucket is no longer a filesystem write, and a bash-maintained variable is no longer a host secret (INV-nular, bash half).** Measured on the shipped CLI with a control in the same run: a script whose only redirect is `echo "$API_KEY" > /dev/null` returned **`violated` (rc 1)** against `{boundary: fs_write, must_not_exist: true}` and now returns **`confirmed` (rc 0)**, while the control writing to `/etc/cron.d/pwned` stays `violated` in both arms. Nothing is written to any filesystem — the kernel discards the bytes and no observation anywhere differs because the redirect ran, which is what makes the finding *vacuous* rather than merely imprecise, and is the mechanism behind 0006's headline being an upper bound on useful precision.
- **The discriminator could not live in the catalogue and now does not have to.** `redirect.>` is ONE row whose boundary depends on the TARGET at the call site, exactly as `builtins.open`'s depends on the mode, so the analyzer stamps `io_target_kind` (`host_path` / `null_device` / `std_stream` / `unresolved`) and `io_boundary` reads it. `> "$OUT"` stays classified: "wrote somewhere I cannot name" is a real write. `/dev/stderr` is **marked and deliberately not reclassified** — that write really does leave the process and can publish a secret, so it is a `logging` crossing, and settling `logging`-vs-`fs_write` is the decision the Haskell `hPutStrLn` rows are already waiting on (INV-vaduk shape 4).
- **EXAMINED and CROSSES are different questions, and the first cut at this conflated them.** Refusing the match in `classify_call_in_catalog` — where the coverage gate also reads it — turned the false `violated` into an **`inconclusive` (rc 2)**: *"calls into 1 module(s) that the I/O catalog could not classify (redirect)"*, trading a wrong finding for a withheld verdict. We know exactly what `> /dev/null` is; that is *why* we can say it crosses nothing. The check moved to the tagger, and a regression test pins that a discarding call is still classified.
- **Bash variables are split by WHO SET THEM, not by whether the name looks sensitive.** A sensitivity list is precisely the curated enumeration the `env_read` row exists to refuse — wrong the moment a repo invents a name, and wrong silently. This is INV-jurif's own discriminator one level out: a name the *script* assigns is not an environment read (shipped), and neither is a name *bash* assigns. The 50 documented shell variables (`BASH_SOURCE`, `RANDOM`, `LINENO`, `PIPESTATUS`, …) now cross no boundary at all; the 8 that describe the host or user (`HOSTNAME`, `OSTYPE`, `MACHTYPE`, `HOSTTYPE`, `PWD`, `OLDPWD`, `UID`, `EUID`) route to `host_info_read` on INV-tutar's precedent — Python's catalogue already files their syscall equivalents (`getcwd`, `getuid`, `uname`) there. `$HOME` stays an environment read: bash does not set it, it inherits it. Both arms measured — pre-fix, `$HOSTNAME` and `$BASH_SOURCE` both landed on `env.environ` and derived `host_secret`; post-fix, `$HOSTNAME` derives `host_description` and `$BASH_SOURCE` derives nothing, with `$API_KEY → /tmp/out.txt` still `violated` as the control.
- **Two of the six reported bash claims did not reproduce and are recorded as such** rather than quietly fixed or quietly dropped: `>&2` emits no edge at all (a recall gap in the opposite direction from the over-report that was filed), and `>>` is distinguished from `>` by dst id and by `io_mode` end to end. Both are now pinned by tests so they cannot start to.
- **A collapsed edge no longer reports one call site's fact as the whole relationship's, and the case that made it urgent is a real filesystem write being deleted from the boundary map (INV-vukiv).** `deduplicate_edges` keeps one edge per `(src, dst, edge_type)` and unioned only `line` into `call_lines`; every other site-varying key on the survivor was the FIRST edge's value, presented with nothing to mark it partial. Measured end to end on the shipped CLI with a control in the same run: `def truncate_logs(p): open(p,'w').close()` reports `fs_write`, and **adding `open(p,'r')` above it reported `fs_read` only** — the truncating write vanished, because the two `builtins.open` sites collapsed to one edge carrying `io_mode='r'` and the mode gate then eliminated the `fs_write` row. A `must_not_exist: fs_write` claim confirms on that function. This is the INV-nufob / ADR-0033 false-confirm class reached through the edge collapse rather than through a catalogue hole, and it was live in Python.
- **The rule is a generalization, not an invention.** `call_arg_shape` has carried exactly this discipline since INV-fubag — a "every argument here is a literal" proof may survive a collapse only if it holds of EVERY site, because asserting it from one line silences real flows at another — hardcoded for that one key. What was missing was any way to declare a second, so `MetaKeySpec` gains `per_call_site` and `ir` reads the list from the registry rather than owning a copy. Declared today: `io_mode`, `call_arg_shape`, and bash's `redirect_target`, `redirect_target_resolved` and `env_var` (the last three were emitted while unregistered, so nothing could have known to union them).
- **The collapse contract mirrors `call_lines`.** The singular key survives only while every collapsed site agrees, so its presence means "true of the whole relationship"; the moment two sites disagree it is REMOVED and the distinct values move to `<key>_values`. A site that OMITS the key is itself a disagreement — adopting the one site that has a value would state it of sites that never claimed it. Two further per-site erasures are closed by the same change: bash `redirect_target` reported `echo a > /dev/null` followed by `echo "$API_KEY" > /etc/cron.d/pwned` as a write to `/dev/null`, and `env_var` reported `$HOME` then `$API_KEY` as `HOME`, naming the harmless read and losing the secret one.
- **Both fs boundaries are reported when the sites span both, rather than trading the lost write for a lost read.** `resolve_mode_boundary_across_sites` resolves to `fs_write` if ANY collapsed site writes — the reverse of the direction `resolve_mode_boundary` takes for ABSENCE, and deliberately so: an absent or computed mode is ignorance and licenses nothing, but disagreeing sites are not ignorance, the `'w'` was read off the source. On its own that would delete the read, so `mode_spanned_boundaries` additionally stamps the plural `io_boundaries` INV-zumin already minted for "several facts about one call are true at once" — here the several facts belong to several call SITES rather than to several catalogue rows. Uniform edges gain no key.

### Added

- **Measurement `0006` — the first measurement under the ratified frame, and the series restarts at N=1: [33.9% per situation, 31.5% per row](docs/measurements/0006-taint-precision-under-the-ratified-frame.md).** 112 situations over 16 repositories in 10 languages, seed 20260825. It is **not** comparable to 0001/0004/0005 and must not be plotted against them — that is the whole point of INV-duvup, which established that the earlier series could not show whether the tool was improving because pooling alone swung 0005's row rate 5.9–16.7% and the estimator changed silently between measurements. **The frame did what it was built to do:** the largest single repository contributes **14.6%** of rows, against 77% (pretix, 0001), 75% (caddy, 0005) and 50% (rabbitmq) in earlier cohorts, and a per-repository precision spread from 0% to 85.7% that concentration had been hiding is now visible.
- **Both labelling passes were independent agent panels; the orchestrator held none.** A deviation from 0005 (where pass 1 was the orchestrator's own ledger) with the reason recorded rather than glossed: panel A was launched before the orchestrator's pass existed, and agents return their labels into the orchestrator's context, so any pass written afterwards would not have been independent. Two panels of 16, one repository each, 112/112 coverage both, **94.6% agreement**. Panel B's standalone total coincides with pass 3's by a *different* assignment of the six disagreements — a coincidence, not corroboration, and the tables say so. **Neither panel used UNADJUDICABLE once**, which is left as an open question about the label rather than as evidence the sample was clean.
- **Two sensitivities were pre-registered with their arithmetic before pass 4 and withheld from the refuters; the larger one fired exactly as priced.** `open-handle-as-receiver` (ArkLib#0, ArkLib#1, jaeger#0) was declared as 39.3% → 36.6% if rejected — all three were independently refuted, and the refuters drew a sharper line than either panel: a receiver built by in-program computation survives (`path.with_name(path.name + '.bak')`), a file handle returned by `open()` does not. `sink-designation` was declared at 39.3% → 41.1% and no refuter raised it. Pass 4 also refuted three situations outside both declared levers, and **refuted 7 of 44 TPs (15.9%)** against 0005's 1 of 9 — a cheaper protocol would have shipped 39.3%.
- **One refutation is reported separately and deliberately not folded in.** tmux#6 was refuted on the ground that the chain holds in a different function than the anchor — a criterion **not in 0001's rubric**, which the adjudicator had introduced in the refutation brief. Both panels had independently seen the wrong anchor and scored the real chain. Folding it would be the adjudicator rewriting the cited rubric mid-measurement, which frame rule F6 forbids; the headline is stated without it (33.9%) and with it (33.0%).
- **The refuters' incidental findings name a single dominant defect family: a primitive classified by its NAME rather than by what it does**, across five languages — Haskell `IORef`/`STRef` as `db_read` (and `newIORef` is a constructor that *writes*, reading nothing), C `socket` as `net_recv` (it returns a descriptor and receives nothing), Elixir `Application.get_env` as `env_read` (config, not the process environment), Python `parse_args` as `env_read` (argv, not environ), bash `>` as `fs_write` with no redirect-target check so `>/dev/null` counts, and `BASH_SOURCE` as `env_read`. (Two further bash claims in the refuters' reports **do not reproduce** and are struck here rather than carried: `>&2` counts as nothing at all — `_redirect_edge` returns `None` for fd duplication, which is a *recall* gap in the opposite direction — and `>>` is distinguished from `>` by both its dst id and its `io_mode`, end to end into the boundary map, pinned by a test since INV-vavup.) The consequence is stated plainly: a finding can be **true value-flow and vacuous as a claim** — all five shellcheck true positives are real dataflow filed under `untrusted-input-no-database` against in-process mutable cells — which makes 33.9% an **upper bound on useful precision**, not a measure of it.
- **`collapsed_flow_count` could not be reconciled by three independent refuters**, including totals padded with cross-module pairs that carry no value. That field is the row-level denominator, so the row figure is the less trustworthy of the two — a second, independent reason the ratified frame was right to make **situation** the canonical unit.
- **Measurement `0005` — taint precision after phases 1 and 2 of the taint-root campaign: [21.6% per situation, 10.0% per row](docs/measurements/0005-taint-precision-after-vocabulary-split.md).** Census of `0004`'s five-repo cohort at `ba01153095`; 8 TP of 37 situations, 17 TP of 170 rows. Per-situation is **2.16x** per-row, the third population in a row where the units differ by roughly that factor. The instrument's positive control leads the report: **0 confirmed verdicts** on any of the five repositories — 13 violated, 22 inconclusive — so none of this speaks to whether the tool ever says "clean".
- **The pre-registered adversarial control ran this time, and the honest finding is that the blind pass alone would not have been enough.** `WI-sivuz` fixed the method in advance — a second pass by adjudicators that did not produce the first, given the flow and the source but not the label, with the disagreement RATE published beside precision — and `0004` could not reproduce it, which it named as its weakest point. Four independent adjudicators over disjoint chunks reached the **identical verdict on all 37 (0.0% disagreement)**, including both contested cases. **Then the two passes that followed changed the headline twice, in opposite directions.** `0001`'s caveat is not a formality: a low disagreement rate shows the rubric was applied consistently; it is not evidence the rubric is right, because both passes share it.
- **The primary pass was wrong about the one label it had flagged as contested, and a commissioned adjudication corrected it (row rate 7.9% -> 10.6%).** Five pairs under `caddy:untrusted-input-no-host-fs:2` end where the sink's path argument is a **config field** reached from stdin through `json.Unmarshal` into a module caddy selects at run time; they were labelled UNADJUDICABLE on `0001`'s *"the hop is real dynamic dispatch or reflection"*. The clause reads *"the path cannot be followed from source alone: … or the hop is …"*, which makes reflection an INSTANCE of unfollowability rather than an independent trigger — and this path is followable from in-repo declarations (`RegisterModule(FileWriter{})`, the module ID, the `json:"filename"` tag, `ctx.LoadModule(cl,"WriterRaw")`, and `os.Chmod(fw.Filename, configuredMode)` taking the field itself). Every citation was re-read before the label changed. The accepted consequence is stated rather than patched: under this rubric **every configured path is a true positive for any config-driven server** — uninformative, not false — and that is filed on its own terms.
- **An adversarial pass refuted 1 of the 9 true positives (situation rate 24.3% -> 21.6%, row rate 10.6% -> 10.0%).** `Fernet(os.environ["CI_BUILD_KEY"].encode())` then `outfile.write(f.decrypt(infile.read()))` was called TP on `0001`'s pretix precedent. The refutation is better: the written bytes come from `infile.read()`, an external resource, and `Fernet.decrypt` **authenticates before decrypting**, so a wrong key raises `InvalidToken` rather than producing different output — the key gates whether the write happens, which is control dependence. The refuter drew the line by contrast with a flow it did **not** refute: `url.Values.Encode` is also a library hop, but its output literally contains the source strings. It also disclosed that it had strayed into a ledger-building script the four blind chunks were forbidden but its own instructions did not name; that is an error in the instructions, and it is recorded rather than quietly fixed.
- **The 297 -> 170 row drop is one scope decision, not a precision fix, and the instrument can say so exactly.** `host_secret` has three claims (`network`, `logging`, `host_fs`); `host_description`, created by the INV-tutar split, deliberately has one. Re-running the identical census with two scope-probe claims added restores `0004`'s population **repo for repo** — caddy 219, mitmproxy 60, poetry 13, express 3, apollo-server 2, total 297 — and the 170 rows are a strict subset by flow identity with all 127 extras carrying a probe claim id. So on this cohort phase 1's fail-open fixes removed nothing, which is consistent with what they do (they turn false *cleans* into unknowns).
- **Phase 2 did not raise `host_secret` precision, and the honest reason is that it moved a true positive out.** Compared on its NEW membership: 5 TP of 22 situations (22.7%) against `0004`'s 17 of 51 (33.3%) for the then-undivided family. The split removed 29 situations — including caddy's `upgradeBuild`, which sends `runtime.GOOS`/`GOARCH` to a remote build service and is now the single true positive of `host-description-no-network`. What INV-tutar bought is a change in what the claims MEAN, and it should not be defended with a precision number it did not produce.
- **`reachability-only` has overtaken control-dependence as the largest false-positive class** (12 of 29 at the situation unit, 9 of them caddy), and the mechanism is structural: caddy's call graph is fully connected through `caddy.Load -> changeConfig -> unsyncedDecodeAndRun`, so the *same* ten or eleven terminal I/O calls appear under four different sources in four different packages. One source read buys a fixed tax of a dozen rows regardless of what it is. One new mechanism is named: **`route-disables-sink`** — `cmdRespond` really does carry stdin into `cfgJSON`, and the same config sets `Admin.Config.Persist` false, which is the guard on the autosave write.
- **`ddg_mixed` scores below `structural` again, now in a third population and at both units** (7.0% vs 18.4% per row). The label is not a calibrated confidence. `ddg` is no longer clearly the best label either — the refuted flow was one of its three situations — but its shape survives: it is far too small to filter on, and reporting only `ddg` would discard **7 of the 8** true positives, which is `0001`'s *do not filter on confidence* conclusion reproduced at both units.
- **Three producer defects were found in passing and are recorded with their measured size.** A prefix primitive and its extension are counted as two source primitives for one read (`os.environ` alongside `os.environ.get` in seven situations; in five of them the bare form does not occur anywhere in the file, so **9 of the 170 rows are that artefact**). A sink primitive can name something that is not a call (`sys.stderr` as the `file=` keyword of a `print`, making the rubric's own test unsatisfiable against the named primitive). And a route can be fictitious in a diagnosable way — the claimed `StoragePool.Provision -> caddy.Load` hop is a **name collision** with `ca.storage.Load`, an interface method on `certmagic.Storage`.
- **No band movement is claimed.** `WI-sivuz`'s bands were named before any interval was computed. This is input to the owner's re-rank, which is what gates the recall class (`INV-linub`); it is not a self-certification that recall may resume.

### Changed

- **`measure-taint-precision.py` reports BOTH units, always, each labelled with its unit — and refuses rather than guessing.** The instrument predates `INV-karud`'s situation collapse, so it was reporting one unit while calling it the other: an evidence record has been a SITUATION since the collapse, standing for `collapsed_flow_count` source->sink pairs, and the script kept only the witness scalars. It now carries `collapsed_flow_count` / `source_primitives` / `sink_primitives` / `sink_symbols` onto every flow row (absent -> 1, so `0001` and `0003` flow files still score correctly — there, one record *was* one pair), and prints a per-situation and a per-row rate side by side in every table.
- **A situation label cannot tell you how many of its pairs are real, so `score` will not pretend it can.** A situation is TRUE POSITIVE iff *at least one* of its pairs is; assuming "all" inflates the row rate and assuming "one" deflates it. A multi-pair `TP` that carries no `tp_pairs` in the ledger makes `score` **exit 1 with the offending ids** rather than print a number — the same refusal `collect` already makes about a zero it cannot distinguish from a run that never happened. `unadj_pairs` exists alongside it so a situation-unit run reconciles with a pair-unit run instead of folding those rows into FP.
- **`collect --no-collapse` measures the row unit instead of apportioning it.** It rebinds `taint.collapse_unadjudicated_flows` to the identity for that process only, so each record is one pair and can be adjudicated on its own terms; `0004` had to hand-block that. Production is untouched and `collect` prints which arm it ran. The two arms were run separately for `0005` and reconcile exactly — 37 situations summing to 170 pairs against 170 pair records, every member set recovered (37/37), both ledgers agreeing on TP=13, FP=152, UNADJ=5.

### Fixed

- **`measure-call-escape-cause.py` filtered call sites on the Python node type alone, so every non-Python repository reported zero call-node escape sites under a live positive control (WI-nukut).** The filter was `cache[sym].get(line) != "call"` — `call` is the *Python* CFG statement node; Go's are `call_expression` / `deferred_call` / `go_statement`. On caddy the instrument printed `CALL-node escape sites: 0` while its own control reported 78,600 edges offered and 236 escape events captured, so the zero read as a finding about the *language* rather than as an instrument that never looked. It is now a declared per-language `_CALL_NODE_TYPES` map grounded in observed CFG output, and **a language with no entry refuses the table (exit 2) naming the language and the number of sites dropped** rather than silently emptying the population — the same fail-loud contract `measure-escape-closing-ceiling.py` holds for its own monkeypatch. Re-run on caddy: **0 → 8 sites** (`INV-mumov` 50.0%, `4a` catalogue gap 37.5%, `INV-linub` 12.5%), a cause distribution that inverts Python's (`INV-linub` 76.5% on pretix). Mixed-language repositories are where this was least visible: kserve is 34 Python sites and 14 Go, pretix 15 Python and 5 JavaScript, so the dropped sites hid behind the Python ones that still came through.

- **An overlay's completeness grant now names WHAT it vouched for, not just the file it came in (INV-tabaf).** A `module_completeness` entry is the most powerful line a user can write and was the least visible one. Everything else an overlay supplies *adds* knowledge; this converts the **absence** of knowledge into evidence — it is a closed-world claim that an unmatched call into that module is an *examined negative*. The measured hazard on record: a six-line overlay with zero primitive rows and one entry for `telnetlib` turned the INV-buzab exfiltration fixture (opens `telnetlib.Telnet`, writes `os.environ["API_KEY"]` into it) from `inconclusive` rc 2 to **`confirmed` rc 0**, disclosed by nothing but a stderr line naming the overlay's path.
- **INV-zosun's provenance disclosure was the right frame at the wrong resolution.** It records *which* catalogue files a verdict was computed against and *who* supplied them, in text and JSON alike — but a reader holding `io_primitives: overlays/deps.yaml [claims-file extra_catalogs]` cannot tell whether that file added a `requests.post` row or closed the world over `telnetlib`, and those are not comparable claims. `catalog_provenance` now emits a `completeness_grants` list keyed by module, and the text renderer prints it as its own block rather than folding it into the path list, because "this file was used" and "this file closed the world over telnetlib" are not the same size of fact.
- **The population is exactly the entries that can do the damage, and every name is printed.** Only `completeness: complete` entries count — an entry without it records no closed-world claim and `_from_dict` does not even keep it, so disclosing it would name a grant nobody made. The list is **not capped at five** the way the third-party name list is (WI-fosir found that cap evicting the name a reader needed): a completeness grant is a small, deliberate, hand-written list, and if it is long enough to want summarising, that *is* the thing the reader should see. The overlay is loaded a second time purely to describe it, and every failure there is swallowed — a describe-step that can fail a run it is only reporting on turns a disclosure into an outage.

- **A catalogue that does not exist no longer reports declared provenance (WI-gofah) — and the change that shipped is not the one the item asked for.** WI-gofah proposed flipping `load_catalog`'s missing-file default from `provenance_declared` to `in_progress`, and named the consequence it expected: *"the flip would newly mark every catalogue-less language's `external_potential` chains unreliable"*. Measured at tip, that consequence cannot occur and the flip would make the output **worse**: `_external_potential_chains` skips any source language whose catalogue is `not is_supported`, and `cmd_io_boundaries` never puts one into the `catalogs` dict at all — so a catalogue-less language produces **no chains of any kind**, and there is nothing for the flag to mark. Verified end to end on a lua+ruby fixture, which already gets the better-worded INV-javam disclosure: *"no I/O primitive catalog for language(s): lua, ruby. Zero boundaries reported … does NOT mean the code is I/O-free"*. Flipping the status would have added a **second** disclosure saying something false — that a catalogue exists and is partial.
- **What was actually wrong is that the field lied to anyone who read it.** `status` is a claim about a catalogue's provenance, and on the missing-file fallback it answered `provenance_declared` — for a catalogue that does not exist. Nothing reads it that way today only because both readers ask `== "in_progress"`, which is a property of today's two call sites rather than of the field; the next consumer to ask `== "provenance_declared"` gets a citation-backed answer for a language nobody catalogued, failing **open**. The fallback now carries `CATALOG_STATUS_UNSUPPORTED`, a third value **a catalogue file may not declare** (the YAML validator's existing two-value check refuses it), because it describes the absence of the file rather than anything the file says. Behaviour-preserving by construction — both readers test for `"in_progress"` — with each reader asserted directly rather than inferred.

- **`env_read` split into `env_read` + `host_info_read`; a boundary that auto-derives a taint label now means what the label means (INV-tutar, owner-ratified 2026-08-25).** `AUTO_SOURCE_LABEL_MAP` derived `host_secret` from `env_read`, and `env_read` catalogued two different things: ambient CONFIGURATION reads whose values may carry a credential (`os.getenv`, `System.getProperty`, `std::env::var`, `process.env`), and host DESCRIPTION or identity reads that are not secrets in any ordinary sense (`runtime.GOOS`, `os.uname`, `navigator.platform`, `platform.system`, `pwd.getpwnam`). Census over the shipped catalogues: **134 of 195 rows were the second kind** — 114 pure platform description, 16 user/process identity. Every `host-secret-*` claim therefore counted description reads as secret flows: 48 of 85 adjudicated flows at **22.9% precision**, the weakest family in measurement `0001`, and 51 of 59 situations in `0004`.
- **The catalogue was already distorting itself to cope, which is what made this a vocabulary defect rather than an imprecision.** `io_primitives/python.yaml` deliberately withheld `getpid` / `cpu_count` / `times` — *"env_read rows auto-derive host_secret TAINT SOURCES … and a pid is not a secret"* — while `go.yaml` rowed `GOOS`, `Getwd` and `Executable`. One boundary value, two membership rules, two shipped files: the apex/peer hit in the fundamental-concept audit, which alone is grounds to deprecate. All four leakage tests fired; the census and the audit are in [`docs/surveys/env-read-boundary-census.md`](docs/surveys/env-read-boundary-census.md), the decision in ADR-0016's boundary table.
- **The split is of the BOUNDARY, not the label, and not a per-row override.** A rename leaves one boundary meaning both things and breaks three shipped example claims for nothing; a per-row `taint_label` would let the row *and* the boundary each decide, which is one fact in two homes. The boundary vocabulary (`CATALOG_BOUNDARY_TYPES`) is the registry-backed thing, so it is where the distinction belongs. `host_info_read → host_description`; **`host_description` is not a weaker `host_secret`** — a claim naming one does not match flows carrying the other, and `generic-taint-claims.yaml` now ships `host-description-no-network` as its own question (telemetry and fingerprinting, not credential exfiltration).
- **130 rows moved across 13 catalogues; one was removed rather than moved.** `os.getrandom` was filed under `env_read` and derived a `host_secret` source — it is a CSPRNG read, not an environment read under any reading, and the same file already keeps `os.urandom` out for its own (still live) reason. After the split the auto-derived source population is **87 `host_secret` / 183 `host_description` / 579 `untrusted_input`** across 17 languages.
- **Family D (user and process identity, 16 rows) folds into `host_info_read` rather than getting a third boundary** — owner's call. *"This app sends the username to a third party"* is a genuinely distinct claim, but 16 rows with no measured consumer demand is a speculative vocabulary entry; declaring it later is cheap.
- **The two vocabularies are now pinned to each other**, so a boundary cannot be added without deciding what it derives, and a catalogue cannot use a boundary nobody declared — which would otherwise be silently inert (no auto-source, no auto-sink, no display grouping, no error). Both arms of the split are asserted at the derived-source layer with a control population, because "everything is `host_description` now" would pass a one-arm test.
- **No precision target is promised.** The band ruling stands until the owner re-ranks on measurement `0005`, which is what prices this.

- **One predicate now answers "does this file announce itself as generated" (INV-bokim + INV-pogob).** Two lived in `supply_chain.py` and disagreed, and the **more destructive consumer held the weaker rule**: `is_likely_minified` heuristic 3 was an unanchored case-insensitive `(Generated by|@generated|DO NOT EDIT)` over five lines, and it decides `Tier.DERIVED`, which drops **every symbol in the file**; the comment-anchored `_GENERATED_HEADER_PATTERN` only set the `is_generated` flag. `_classify_file_core` consults the loose one at step 2 and returns immediately, so the loose one won. Measured cost: two hand-written first-party test files in *this* repository contributed **zero nodes** to a default survey because their docstrings mention some *other* file being regenerated (`"regenerated by"` contains `"generated by"`); and a Go probe reported 0 of 210 primitives reachable because its own fixture carried `// generated by <script>`.
- **The union is the rule, not the strict set.** Collapsing onto `_GENERATED_HEADER_PATTERN` as it stood would have *lost* a real detection — the Python protobuf compiler writes `# Generated by the protocol buffer compiler.  DO NOT EDIT!`, which is not `Code generated by`. The shared rule is comment-anchored, and keeps the weak phrase `generated by` **only when `DO NOT EDIT` co-occurs on the same line**, which is what separates a generator's declaration from a sentence about one.
- **The scope is the leading COMMENT BLOCK, and a first cut got that wrong in a way only measurement caught.** Scanning the whole 4 KiB head for an anchored marker passed every unit test and, on a five-repository sweep, newly classified three first-party files as generated: a comment in `test_max_tier.py` *describing* a `"DO NOT EDIT"` header, Eclipse's per-method `// TODO Auto-generated method stub`, and a bare `// autogenerated` field comment. A generated file declares itself in its *header*, so the header is the scope — and it has to be the block rather than the first five lines, because a real declaration routinely sits under a licence notice (http4s's `MimeDB.scala` carries `// Autogenerated file, don't edit` at line 17). All three are pinned as regressions, along with the licence-header control.
- **A/B over six repositories (14,114 scanned files), after the scope fix:** **4 files stop being `Tier.DERIVED`** — hypergumbo's `test_spec_linker_count.py` and `test_scip_generated.py` (INV-bokim's own two, now `FIRST_PARTY` alongside the `test_ir.py` control), `SECURITY.md`, and clover's `GlobalCoverageRecordingTranscript.java` whose javadoc says a class was *"previously generated by an instrumenter"*. **1 file newly becomes `Tier.DERIVED`** — http4s's `MimeDB.scala`, which is genuinely generated. caddy, VernissageServer and keycloak (8,896 files): **no change at all**. The two consumers now agree on every file, which is the property that was missing.

- **A directory whose name collides with a dependency's first component no longer vouches for that dependency (INV-lakom).** `_is_analyzed_module` shortens the callee module because the slot may carry a trailing TYPE name (`app.config.Loader` for a callee defined in `app/config.py`). The shortening was **unbounded** — it walked every dotted prefix down to a single component — so it equally licensed `os.path` → `os` and `crypto.tls` → `crypto`. A repo that owns a directory named `os` therefore reported boundary coverage **complete** over the standard library's `os.path`, with nothing about `os.path` examined, in the direction that produces a clean verdict over an unexamined module. Same shape as INV-juvul's finding one layer up, which was fixed by testing the *folded* spelling whole; the dotted loop was left unbounded.
- **Two bounds, each for its own reason.** ONE step, because that is the length of a type name — a nested class (`app.config.Loader.Inner`) would need two, is not licensed, and reports a gap, which is the safe direction. NEVER to a single component, because a bare `os` / `crypto` / `json` / `time` is precisely the spelling that collides with an ordinary directory name, and the analyzed set is matched as a component-bounded **infix** (it must be, to tolerate packaging prefixes), which makes a one-component needle match almost anywhere. **Not** "match the shortened form as a suffix instead": that was the first design and INV-liloh's own fixture refutes it — `hypergumbo_core.scip._generated` shortens to `hypergumbo_core.scip`, which sits *inside* `packages.hypergumbo-core.src.hypergumbo_core.scip.loader` and is not a suffix of it.
- **A/B over five real repo-language pairs, both arms in one process because the predicate is pure, with a hard assertion that the bound can only ever refuse (it never vouched where the old one did not).** caddy/go: **0 change** — the separator-fold path already handled it. **hypergumbo/python: 7 newly disclosed, every one `starlette.*`** — a real declared dependency (`starlette>=1.3.1` in `hypergumbo-tracker/pyproject.toml`) that was being vouched for by a **test fixture directory** named `starlette`, on hypergumbo's own self-claims. **clover/java: 754 vouched → 250, so 504 newly disclosed**, all shaded third-party (`clover.antlr.*`, `clover.com.google.common.*` = guava, `clover.com.lowagie.text.*` = iText) vouched for by the `clover` component in the repo's own `com.atlassian.clover` package path.

- **java now stamps an explicit `this` receiver too, which was the last arm of INV-pirot and the one whose rationale was wrong.** The previous cut excluded `this` on the grounds that its receiver type *is* known (the enclosing class), so there is no want of receiver evidence. That reasoning does not survive contact with the emit site: an edge reaches it **because the call did not resolve**, and a `this.m()` that does not resolve is one whose `m` is *not* on the enclosing class. The analyzer knows which object; it does not know which type declares the method — exactly what the two taint gates read the key for.
- **Measured harm, with a control in the same run.** `this.doFinal(plain)` emits `java:external:0-0:doFinal:unresolved` — bare short name, `external` in the module slot. `_build_sanitizer_index_multi` indexes the shipped `javax.crypto.Cipher.doFinal` under its bare last component too, so with no `call_construct` the edge fell to the registrar's permit branch and **registered a phantom barrier**; the same edge stamped `method` registers nothing. Both arms, plus a correctly-typed receiver that must still bind and a wrongly-typed one that must not, are pinned in `test_this_receiver_phantom_barrier.py` — filed in hypergumbo-core rather than beside the analyzer, because a stamp test that passes while the guard it feeds is inert is the exact shape that made WI-sajis necessary.
- **Parity, not a new convention, and the recall cost is zero rather than assumed to be small.** python, go, objc, rust, scala and swift all stamp an explicit `this`/`self`; java alone did not. `gate_named_entry` returns `None` immediately for `call_construct == "method"`, so the stamp can only refuse a *function*-kind catalogue hit — and `io_primitives/java.yaml` declares 139 method-kind primitives, 3 attribute-kind and **no function-kind entry at all**. **A/B on 218,049 java call edges (clover, confluent-examples, gradle): +85 edges gained the stamp, 0 changed dst, 0 changed resolution.** Small, and stated as small: the argument for it is the phantom barrier and the parity, not the volume.
- **An IMPLICIT `this` — a bare `m(p)` with no receiver token — stays unstamped, and that is a different omission rather than the same one half-done.** `call_construct` names the *syntactic* construct (ADR-0024, audit-findings 0012); a bare call is not syntactically a method call, and stamping it would file a resolution fact under a construct name — the leak the concept audits exist to prevent. java has no free functions, so that shape *is* a live phantom-barrier surface; it needs a signal of its own and is filed on its own terms.

- **A receiver the analyzer cannot NAME is still a receiver — `call_construct` now records the construct for every receiver shape in java, scala and swift (INV-pirot).** `call_construct` was derived from the receiver's *name*, and the name is `None` for every receiver EXPRESSION — `new File(x).mkdirs()`, `get().mkdirs()`, `((File) o).mkdirs()`, `f.getParentFile().mkdirs()`. One variable was answering two questions: *"what identifier can I look this receiver's type up under"* and *"is there a receiver at all"*. **Measured at tip before the fix, control in the same run:** java stamps `f.mkdirs()` and leaves all four expression shapes bare; scala and swift do the same; objc, rust and go were already correct for every shape.
- **This is the fail-open direction, not a disclosure nicety.** `taint._register_sanitizer_callers` refuses to bind an unresolved bare-name sanitizer match **only** when `call_construct == "method"`. With the key absent the edge reaches the permit branch and registers a **phantom barrier** — and since PR #214 a barrier earns `sanitized`, which drops the flow from the claim's violation set. A missing stamp therefore **deletes findings**.
- **The parity gate grew the arm that would have caught it.** `test_disclosure_parity.py` shipped with plain `receiver.method()` fixtures and its own docstring recorded a cast-receiver residual as "real but separate and much narrower". Re-measured, it was neither: it is *every* non-identifier receiver, in *three* languages. A second registry-driven arm (`COMPLEX_RECEIVER_FIXTURES`) now requires an object-creation / cast / call-result fixture per language, with the same measured-exemption discipline as the first — python is exempt because it emits **no edge at all** for a call-result receiver, which is an emission gap already filed as WI-makij, cited rather than re-filed.
- **In scala and swift the same conflated predicate also gated the INV-fahub magnet guard**, so a complex-receiver call fell through to the bare short-name binds that guard exists to prevent. Both now route through the honest-unresolved branch. **A/B on six real repositories (82,001 call edges; clover, http4s, gatling, Kingfisher, VernissageServer, confluent-examples), arms differing in one hunk with the arm proved by both module path and behaviour:** edge count **identical in every repo** (nothing gained or lost); **+15,896 edges** gained the stamp (19.4% of all call edges were missing it); **2,314 edges** changed dst. Those 2,314 are removals of confidently-wrong bindings, and the shape says so without sampling — **1,200 (51.9%) pointed at a `field`, `variable` or `property`, which is not callable at all**, and **75% crossed a file boundary on nothing but a short name** (`.map()` → an unrelated `package.map`, `.tearDown()` → a different test class's, `.field` → a data field).
- **`this` stays excluded, narrowly and on purpose:** its receiver type *is* known (the enclosing class), so there is no want of receiver evidence to record. The residual — `this.m()` unresolved because `m` is inherited from an external supertype — is filed separately, because stamping it would also refuse function-kind catalogue hits in `gate_named_entry`, and that is a recall change needing its own measurement.

### Added

- **A verdict now says at what analysis fidelity it was reached — the second half of the owner's 2026-08-23 bar (WI-lagod, envelope 2.2).** The ruling rewrote INV-fibis / INV-nuhun to *"a clean verdict must name what it could not examine, **and at what analysis fidelity**"*. The first half shipped across three PRs; this is the second, and until it existed neither item could close. `Verdict` carried eleven fields and **none** named the analyzer behind its edges, while `BoundaryCoverage`'s blind spots are every one a property of the *edge set*. Measured consequence: two runs over one Rust crate differing **only** in backend produced a **byte-identical verdict block** while one carried 10 `origin=scip` nodes and the other zero — a reader holding the output could not tell which analyzer had spoken. The raw material was always there and unread: `Edge.origin` is a list of pass IDs and `Edge.__post_init__` *hard-raises* on an empty one; `verify_claims` read `src` / `dst` / `type` and never `origin`.
- **Two different facts, kept apart on purpose.** `analysis_fidelity` (per-verdict, language → pass IDs) is **what ran** — a statement about this run, scoped to *call* edges because a `contains` edge from the containment linker says nothing about the fidelity of the call structure a boundary verdict rests on. The new `higher_fidelity_available` caveat is **what could have run** — a statement about the *machine*, and the one that makes "rust-analyzer installed but not enabled" distinguishable from "no such backend exists", which the ruling explicitly requires because a reader would act differently on them.
- **Not `analysis_methods`, which already exists.** That records how the taint walk *reasoned* (`ddg` / `ddg_mixed` / `structural`); a `structural` verdict and a tree-sitter verdict are different facts, and widening that key into this one would put two concepts under one name.
- **A false sentence in the first cut was caught by measuring the second arm, not by reasoning.** Keyed on the call-edge fidelity map, the caveat fired in a run where rust-analyzer *was* enabled and had printed its own "backend ACTIVE" banner — because on that crate the SCIP pass emitted `references` and `contains` and no external `calls`. It told the reader to enable something already on. "Did it run" is now answered from a different population (`passes_that_ran`, every edge) than "what produced the calls", and a test pins the case.
- **One home for the stamp, because the count is the argument.** This module builds a `ClaimVerdict` at **fifteen** return sites; a field set at each is a field the sixteenth will not set — and WI-lagod exists *because* a verdict could not say which analyzer produced its edges. Both public entry points are now thin wrappers over `_verify_claim_uncredited` / `_verify_taint_claim_uncredited` that stamp once. The taint arm gets the same treatment for the reason INV-nuhun exists: crediting fidelity on one arm and staying silent on the other is the asymmetry that item names.
- **Availability is derived at the CLI and passed in.** "Is the binary on this machine" is a fact about the machine; `verify_claims` reasons about an edge set, and putting a `shutil.which` inside it would make a pure function of the graph depend on the host. The Rust check is also gated on the language being present first — probing runs the binary, and doing that for a repository with no Rust in it is work no answer depends on.
- **Measured end to end, and the arms now differ where they used to be identical:** same crate, backend **installed but off** → `analysis_fidelity {"rust": ["rust"]}` **plus** the `higher_fidelity_available` caveat; backend **enabled** → same fidelity map, **caveat gone**. The fidelity map is unchanged between them and that is correct: on that crate SCIP contributed no call edges, and the map reports what produced the call structure, not what was switched on.
- **The mypy strict ratchet moved down, not up.** A duplicated bare `findings: list` in the new wrapper tripped the shrink-only gate at 671; both occurrences (the new one and the pre-existing one it copied) are now `list[Any]`, and the baseline is re-locked at **669**.


- **A clean verdict on a language whose analyzer cannot see external instance-method calls now says so (owner ruling 2026-08-23, "declare the blindness").** The untyped-receiver disclosure can only disclose an edge that *exists*. kotlin and javascript emit **no external instance-method call edge at all** (WI-nasuf), so there was nothing to disclose and the verdict came out a bare `confirmed` — silence reading as safety over 232 catalogued method-kind sinks (kotlin **181 of its 186** primitives, javascript 51 of 187). **Measured end to end:** a kotlin file whose only I/O is `f.writeText(payload)` on a parameter-supplied `java.io.File` moved from bare `confirmed` (rc 0) to `confirmed_with_caveats` (rc 3) naming the language and the scale; a python control in the same shape keeps its bare `confirmed` (rc 0).
- **The fact is declared, not inferred, because the edge set cannot express it.** "This repository contains no external method calls" and "this analyzer never emits them" produce the *same empty set* and opposite verdicts. `analyzer_disclosure.py` therefore holds a **dated per-language declaration** with the measurement behind it, in the same positive-declaration shape already ruled for stdlib `module_completeness` — and it is **fail-closed**: an undeclared language answers "does not emit", so a language arriving with method-kind sinks is treated as blind until someone measures it. A test turns that default into a hard gate, driven from the shipped catalogues, so the omission is caught at test time instead of becoming an unintended caveat in production.
- **Not filed in `io_primitives/*.yaml`, deliberately.** Those catalogue what a *library* does; this records what an *analyzer* can see. Putting an analyzer capability in a library catalogue would place one concept in the wrong home and invite the next reader to add library facts to it.
- **A caveat, not a withhold, and the distinction is load-bearing.** Those analyzers *do* emit call edges, just not this shape, so "I examined everything I could see, except this whole construct" is the true sentence — a qualified opinion (ADR-0016 §4). `inconclusive` would claim the analysis formed no view at all. The caveat rides **both arms**, boundary and taint, because a run that discloses on one and stays silent on the other about the same unseen call is precisely the asymmetry INV-nuhun names.
- **It disappears on its own when the gap closes.** When WI-nasuf teaches those analyzers to emit the edges, the declaration flips and no invariant changes colour. Under the *capability*-phrased bar the rewrite replaced, that same commit would have read as a mass **new** violation — the anti-correlation (LIVE.md rule 19) the bar rewrite exists to kill.

- **A changed `build.rs` now revokes a backend trust grant; a changed `Cargo.toml` does not (owner ruling 2026-08-23, resolving ADR-0045 OQ1).** The shipped behaviour was advisory for everything — a change was reported but the grant stood, and the next run executed the new build script. The split is the substance: `build.rs` is the file that actually **executes** during indexing and it changes rarely, so a prompt about it carries information, while `Cargo.toml` churns with routine dependency bumps and re-prompting on that is how a person learns to click through the prompt that matters (this project's own reasoning, already written down in `TestRustAnalyzerDisclosureRespectsTheGate`). **Measured end to end:** granted repo → 12 `origin=scip` nodes; `Cargo.toml` bumped → still granted, still 12, change surfaced on `--show`; `build.rs` edited → **0 scip nodes**, and the modified script never ran.
- **Revocation is applied at the single read point, not at each caller.** `read_decision` returns the *effective* answer, so a consumer cannot forget to check — and forgetting would fail **open**, executing a build script the user never approved. `recorded_grant` keeps what was actually written down, so `--show` can distinguish "you declined" from "you granted and the build script then changed"; conflating those would misreport what happened. The gate's own behaviour is asserted through the resolver it really calls, not through the store alone.
- **The limit is stated rather than implied, and was on the table when the ruling was made:** only *top-level* manifests are hashed, so no form of this protects against a **dependency's** build script. It catches tampering with this project's own build script, and nothing else.
- **The four tests that pinned the old behaviour named themselves as the ones to change, and were changed rather than deleted.** Their docstrings said they were the contract for OQ1; the rewrite keeps that role and adds the **discriminating** case the split needs — a `Cargo.toml` change that must *not* revoke. Without it, "revoke on any change" would pass every other assertion and the ruling would be silently over-implemented.

- **A durable, per-repository backend trust grant — `hypergumbo trust-backend` (ADR-0045 rulings 5–8).** Enabling the SCIP Rust backend makes indexing execute the analysed crate's `build.rs` and proc macros as the invoking user, and until now the only way to make that choice *stick* was exporting `HYPERGUMBO_RUST_ANALYZER=1` from a shell profile — which is **global**, so it opted in every Rust repository the user would ever analyse, including ones cloned specifically to audit (WI-sobig). The trust decision is per-repository; the only persistence available had the wrong scope for it. Grants now live in `$XDG_STATE_HOME/hypergumbo/trust.d`, mode `0600`, **outside `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME`** — a config directory is designed to be synced between machines, and a synced grant of code execution is the same footgun by a nicer route.
- **Keyed by resolved absolute path, deliberately not by ADR-0013's repo-fingerprint.** That key (remote URL + first commit SHA) exists so multiple checkouts *share* a cache, which is right for a cache and exactly wrong here: two clones of one remote can have different working trees, and it is the tree whose `build.rs` runs. Paths resolve through symlinks so one tree cannot acquire two divergent decisions.
- **A decline is a decision, so the nudge stops asking.** `read_decision` returns `None` for "never asked" and `granted=False` for "asked and said no" — the same three-valued discipline the opt-in resolver uses. The "installed but NOT enabled" disclosure now goes quiet for any repository with a decision on file, either way, and still fires for undecided ones. **Measured:** granted repo → backend runs with no flag and no env var (10 `origin=scip` nodes); revoked → 0; granted-but-`--backend tree-sitter` → 0, so the flag still outranks the grant.
- **`executes_analysed_code` now gates from both sides (ruling 5).** Config refuses an *executing* backend; the trust store refuses a *non-executing* one (`trust-backend pyright` exits 2 pointing at the config file). One setting cannot acquire two homes.
- **The advisory-hash decision is pinned, including which test to change if it is reversed.** A grant records a digest of `build.rs` + `Cargo.toml`; a later change surfaces on `--show` but does **not** revoke (ADR-0045 ruling 7). Revoking would re-prompt on an ordinary dependency bump, and by this project's own reasoning an alarm that fires when moot trains skimming. OQ1 is open; the two tests that hold this say so in their docstrings.
- **Ruling 6 is partially landed and the gap is stated.** The store is `0600` and unsynced, but "human owns it, the agent cannot write it" is enforceable only by the OS and only when the agent runs as a different user — hypergumbo cannot tell an agent from a person sharing a uid. An operator wanting that property must `chown` the store, exactly as `tracker init` does for its config.

- **User and project configuration files, and one setting they are forbidden to hold (ADR-0045 rulings 1–3).** hypergumbo had no user configuration of any kind: every preference was per-invocation or environmental, so a project with authored I/O overlays retyped `--io-primitives` on every run. Two TOML tiers now exist — `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/hypergumbo/config.toml` and `<repo>/.hypergumbo.toml` — with `io_primitives` as the first setting. The project file is a root dotfile rather than `.hypergumbo/config.toml` because `.hypergumbo` is already an *output artifact* name in `discovery.py`'s ignore list, and config inside an ignored output directory invites a cleanup step to delete it.
- **A trust grant is refused from every config tier, loudly.** Enabling the SCIP Rust backend makes indexing **execute the analysed crate's `build.rs` and proc macros as the invoking user**, so it is not a preference — and `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` is a directory people deliberately sync between machines and commit to dotfiles repos. A `rust_analyzer = true` line there would opt every Rust repository on every machine into running build scripts: the WI-sobig footgun reached by a nicer route. `backends.rust_analyzer` therefore raises in **both** tiers, with an error that says where the setting does belong; the *disable* direction is refused too, deliberately, because a key that works one way looks supported both ways. Verified end-to-end: a repo shipping `[backends] rust_analyzer = true` exits 2 naming the file and the reason.
- **Two rules that currently forbid the same key are kept as two rules.** `TRUST_ONLY_SETTINGS` (ruling 2) is refused everywhere because the fact does not belong in config at all; `PROJECT_FORBIDDEN_SETTINGS` (ruling 3) is refused from the project tier because a repository must not make a security decision *about itself* — the deliberate inversion of ADR-0016 §35 / ADR-0017 §370, where project-local overlays outrank built-ins (right for catalogs, backwards for a gate). Collapsing them would silently widen whichever outlived the other.
- **Overlay precedence extends the existing chain rather than starting a second one.** `_resolve_io_overlays` remains the single ordering site: user config < project config < claims-file `extra_catalogs` < `--io-primitives` flag, ascending, because `load_catalog` treats a later path as the winner on qualified-name collision. Relative paths resolve against **their own config file**, not the working directory — with two tiers in play a single cwd-relative rule silently resolves a project path against wherever the user was standing. **Measured end-to-end:** a `net_send` claim over a `boto3.upload_file` call returns `inconclusive` (rc 2) with no config file and **`violated` (rc 1)** with an overlay declared only in `.hypergumbo.toml`.
- **Unknown keys and wrong types raise instead of being ignored**, naming the file, the setting, and the known settings. A silently-dropped key is how someone spends an afternoon believing a preference is in effect. The cost is stated rather than hidden: an older hypergumbo reading a newer config refuses it rather than degrading, which is ADR-0045 OQ4 (schema versioning) and is open. A rejected setting exits 2 with a sentence, not a traceback.
- **`tomllib` is now a declared-complete stdlib module, because one call into it moved all eighteen of hypergumbo's self-claims.** Adding `import tomllib` to the new config loader took every self-claim from `confirmed_with_caveats` to `inconclusive`. That leverage is the coverage gate working exactly as designed, not a bug: `BoundaryCoverage.qualifying_only` is `not unknown`, so the uncatalogued-module list must be **exactly empty** for an opaque launch to qualify a verdict rather than withhold it — the self-proof sits on a knife edge by construction, and one unaudited import takes it off. INV-buzab's distinction is what makes the refusal correct: `is_stdlib_module` recognises the *name* (`tomllib` was already listed), while `module_io_is_enumerated` is the predicate that vouches for the silence, and 283 of Python's 300 enumerated stdlib modules carry no rows. **The fix is a positive dated declaration, not a widening of the gate.** Verdict and evidence: `tomllib.load(fp)` reads from a binary file *object* the caller opened and `loads(s)` is pure parsing — the same shape as `json` — and the survey probe re-run on 2026-08-23 found the sole `open(` match in `tomllib._parser` inside the `TypeError` text *"File must be opened in binary mode…"*, the docstring false-positive class the survey already documents. Recorded in `docs/surveys/python-stdlib-module-io-enumeration.md`, whose 2026-08-15 line "the 18 claims remain inconclusive" is flagged as historical — later work drove the count to zero, which is why one call could move everything.
- **Deferred with a trigger, not silently skipped:** ruling 4's *config* tiers inside `resolve_optin` are reachable only by a backend whose opt-in is allowed in config — i.e. one that does not execute analysed-repo code — and none exists. Wiring them now would add a layer with no consumer, the same reasoning ADR-0022 used to defer its own runtime-aggregation half. Trigger: the first non-executing backend (pyright, WI-nanom).

### Fixed

- **An Objective-C caveat that could not name what it had failed to examine — and the missing home behind it (INV-divuf / WI-nakut, ADR-0036 Ruling 1).** The shipped CLI rendered `distinct method(s): .` on a file whose only `fs_write` reach was an untypable receiver: the disclosure fired, counted the site correctly, and could not say WHICH method — most of its value. The obvious explanation (a five-slot id split truncating a selector) was checked and ruled out by the item that filed it, correctly; the real cause was one layer further out. **An Objective-C selector ENDS in a colon** (`writeToFile:atomically:`), so the id's second-to-last token is the EMPTY STRING, the boundary node synthesised from that id got `name=''`, and every such selector in a run collapsed onto one node — **80 distinct selectors became 17 on Mantle, one node absorbing 49 edges from 134 call sites**.
- **The fix is the one ADR-0036 already ruled, not a new design.** Ruling 1 makes the id's name slot deliberately lossy — *"the ID is a location-addressed key, not a fidelity surface — full-fidelity names live in `Symbol.name`"* — and instructs consumers needing the exact name to **"read `Symbol.name`, never re-derive it from the ID."** Measured on the repro, the designated home was **never filled**: `node.name` was `''`, `qualified_name` `null`, `dst_ref` `null`, and `writeToFile:atomically:` existed **nowhere** in the output. The colon was not the defect; it was what made the missing home visible.
- **One home, stamped at the one factory.** `make_unresolved_edge` — which all 57 unresolved-external call sites across 36 language analyzers route through — now stamps `meta['callee_name']` with the full-fidelity name, registered on the edge-meta axis per ADR-0024 as `single_writer`. It is stamped **unconditionally**, including the cell where WI-huzuv correctly withholds `dst_ref` because the module is unknown (precisely the objc cell); carrying the name in `dst_ref` sometimes and in `meta` otherwise would give one fact two homes and make every consumer ask which cell it was in first. `create_boundary_nodes` prefers that home over the lossy slot, which is what keeps two selectors that both parse to `''` distinct.
- **Both disclosure sites now read the lossless home through ONE function.** `unknown_receiver_scope` and `_call_site_label` are a pair — `_site_method` parses the label back out — so a fix to one that skipped the other would re-open the drift INV-divuf names. The id remains an explicit fallback: not every producer routes through the factory yet, and a disclosure path is the wrong place to raise. **The discriminating test feeds an edge whose id name slot is useless but whose lossless home is intact**, which is both the shape today and the shape after Ruling 1's deferred sanitisation lands — a consumer that still parses the id fails it under the second spelling.
- **The first cut of this fix broke Ruling 1's OTHER half, and the validator caught it.** Feeding the full-fidelity name into the dedupe key also fed it into the minted boundary id — `rust:external:0-0:Vec::with_capacity:external_symbol` — which `id_format` correctly rejects as `double_colon_separator` (INV-sadiv); **bellman's `id_format` violations went 1 → 16**. Getting the lossless half right by breaking the lossy half is not getting Ruling 1 right. `_canonical_external_id` now folds `:` → `.` for the **id** while `Symbol.name` keeps full fidelity — the exact split the ruling prescribes — and bellman's violations land at **0**, clearing the one that pre-dated the change. The sanitizer moved to `ir` (which mints boundary ids) with an explicit re-export from `analyze.base`, because a copy on each side would be one rule with two homes.
- **A second language turned out to be affected, on a repo that was only meant to be a non-destruction control.** `parts[-2]` had been discarding the receiver type on every Rust `Type::method` call, so distinct callees merged: bellman's **13 truncated boundary nodes became 16 qualified ones** — `batch_normalize` split back into `E::G1::batch_normalize` and `E::G2::batch_normalize`, `with_capacity` into `Vec::with_capacity`, `now` into `Instant::now`. Objective-C is simply where the same defect produced an **empty** name rather than a merely truncated one. Verdicts, edge counts and `cross_field` violations are unchanged on both repos; **no verdict flipped** — this is identity and disclosure quality, not coverage.
- **Schema 0.20.2 → 0.20.3.** `Edge.meta` gains `callee_name` (INV-divuf). An addition, hence a patch bump. Every artifact written before this version stays valid: consumers fall back to the id's lossy name slot when the key is absent, which is exactly what they did before it existed. Unlike `call_arg_shape` it is **not** sparse or opt-in — Ruling 1 tells consumers never to re-derive the exact name from the id, so a key present only sometimes would leave them re-deriving it the rest of the time. (`docs/hypergumbo-spec.md` also carried two stale `0.20.1` references, already stale before this change; corrected to 0.20.3.)
- **Measured at three layers**, unit / survey / published CLI: `node.name` `''` → `'writeToFile:atomically:'`, and the caveat `distinct method(s): .` → `distinct method(s): writeToFile:atomically:`.

- **Colon-bearing PATH slots are now read through the chokepoint, and the untyped-receiver caveat names the whole callee (INV-divuf, reduced).** Per ADR-0036 D1a the path slot is the one colon-tolerant slot — `dart:io`, `std::io` — so `parts[1]` is a truncation wherever it is used on one. Four such reads in `verify_claims` and `io_boundary` now go through `ir.symbol_path_slot`. The name-slot reads behind the disclosure a reader actually sees go through `ir.symbol_name_slot`: the caveat had been naming `writeToFile` for the Objective-C selector `writeToFile:atomically:` — a method that does not exist, which is worse than naming nothing because a reader can act on it and find no such call.
- **THIS CHANGE WAS REDUCED MID-FLIGHT AFTER ITS PREMISE WAS REFUTED, and the refutation came from the suite rather than from review.** The first cut asserted that "only `parts[0]` and `parts[-1]` are colon-free by the grammar" and added a shrink-only gate over ~13 positional name-slot reads. That premise is **false**: ADR-0036 Ruling 1 makes the NAME slot colon-free *by rule* — `sanitize_id_name_segment` folds `:` to `.` and `make_symbol_id` applies it to every name slot — so `parts[-2]` is correct on a well-formed id and those reads are the grammar being relied on, not defects. `test_id_format_validator_names_the_name_slot_colon_not_the_span` failed and said so. The gate and its baseline are gone.
- **One part of that first cut was an active regression and was reverted.** Span-anchoring `validate_symbol_id_format` made it locate the span *correctly on a malformed id*, so an id with a colon in its name slot parsed clean and the `colon_in_name_slot` diagnosis fell silent — a diagnosis INV-dulah paid a real investigation to get right, and silence in a validator is the false-clean direction. Both validators keep `parts[-3]` deliberately, now with a comment saying why.
- **The root cause of the Objective-C symptom is a PRODUCER defect at one line, not a parsing defect at fifteen.** `make_unresolved_edge` builds its dst with a raw f-string and never applies the sanitizer — exactly what `spec_validator`'s own error message warns against ("Use make_symbol_id(...) rather than constructing IDs with f-strings").
