A selector therefore reaches the name slot verbatim, in violation of Ruling 1. Measured consequence on Mantle (31 `.m` files): the empty name feeds `_dedupe_key`, so **80 distinct selectors collapse to 17**, 268 of 319 external dsts are nameless, and one node absorbs 49 edges across 134 call sites.
- **The obvious fix is not obviously right, and that is filed rather than decided.** Sanitizing at the producer restores the grammar and reproduces all 80 distinct names — but **107 of the 128 primitives in `io_primitives/objc.yaml` carry a colon in their name** (`fileExistsAtPath:`, `writeToFile:atomically:`), so it would break catalogue matching unless the qualified index registers the folded form too. Restore the grammar at the producer, or amend the grammar to admit the colon: a genuine fork, recorded on INV-divuf with both measurements.
- `_span_token_index` is extracted as a plain DRY refactor — the two chokepoints carried an identical copy of the span-locating loop — and changes no behaviour.

- **A Rust or C++ module reached both by an import and by a scoped attribute read is now ONE node instead of two (INV-rilit).** `imports` and `calls` spelled it `std::io`; `module_attr_ref` spelled it `std.io`. Measured on bellman by folding every Rust node's path slot: **ten entities existed under two spellings in a single run** — `std::io`, `bls12_381::Scalar`, `ff::Field`, `std::cmp::Ordering`, and six more. Every per-dependency question — who imports it, its centrality, its supply-chain tier, whether it is dead — was answered over a split population. Now **zero**, and the coverage gate's own count drops 18 → 16 with no duplicate spellings left.
- **The item's cost figure was too high, and so was an earlier correction to it.** It filed "4 of 23 withhold entries are one module under two spellings" and a later note repeated the 4. Measured directly, **two** of the gate's entries are a second spelling of one already listed. The other two counted pairs — `std.cmp.Ordering`/`std::cmp` and `std.sync`/`std::sync::Arc` — are a **type** path beside a **module** path, which is a different fact. The node-level cost (ten) is the real one; the gate-level cost is two.
- **The normalisation was a workaround that outlived its defect.** It was documented as dot-normalising "so the resulting edge ID survives downstream `:`-split parsing" — but ADR-0036 (D1a) made the path slot the one colon-**tolerant** slot in the grammar, and `symbol_path_slot` carries `rust:std::fs:0-0:write:external_symbol` as a worked example in its own docstring; `symbol_name_slot` is span-anchored for the same reason. It was also only **half** applied, which is the tell: C++ emitted `cpp:std:0-0:std.numbers::pi:attribute`, one id carrying **both** separators, because only the path taken from the imports map was rewritten.
- **This is conformance, not a new shape** — the risk argument in one line. A colon-bearing Rust path slot has always been in production for ordinary `calls` edges (`rust:std::cmp:0-0:min:external_symbol`), as has a colon-bearing name slot (`Error::new`). `module_attr_ref` was the outlier; it now matches its siblings.
- **A positive control caught a false premise before the producer changed, and it was the whole reason this shipped intact.** The code said the catalogue "registers both `::` and `.` forms", so re-spelling looked free. It was not: `IoPrimitive.qualified_name` joins module and name with a **dot** unconditionally, so a `module: std::env` row was indexed as the mixed `std::env.consts` plus a dotted alias and **never** as `std::env::consts`. Emitting the language's own spelling would have silently lost every Rust and C++ attribute classification. The qualified index now folds separators through one shared helper on the read side — and that helper exists because the same gap reappeared mid-fix: folding in `lookup` but not in `lookup_with_module` left `classify_call` still returning `None`.
- **Non-destruction asserted on both controls at the population level, not just the verdict:** caddy unchanged at **158** uncatalogued modules and 55/76/3 chains `violated`; httpx unchanged; the id-format and cross-field validators report the identical 1 and 119 violations before and after, so no new id was made malformed. bellman's verdicts stay `inconclusive` — **no verdict flipped**, for the same reason as INV-juvul: what remains is stdlib enumeration (WI-lutuh).
- **Nine tests that pinned the dotted spelling were rewritten rather than deleted**, including one that named itself `test_basic_scoped_path_emits_dot_normalized_edge` and repeated the stale justification in its docstring.

- **The uncatalogued-module disclosure no longer reports a repository's own code as an unexamined third-party dependency (INV-juvul).** Measured on two unmodified upstream repos by recomputing the gate over each run's own cached survey: **express** reported 17 modules "the I/O catalog could not classify" of which **7 were first-party** (`..`, `../`, `../../db`, `./post`, `./site`, `./user`, `lib/utils`) and only 4 genuine; **bellman** reported 23 of which **6 were first-party** (`crate::`, `super::`, and its own crate name). Because `BoundaryCoverage.qualifying_only` is `not unknown`, **one** noise entry withholds a verdict — so on both repos the first-party entries **alone** were sufficient to withhold every claim, and the genuine dependencies were buried: express's rendered `details` truncates at five and opened with `..`, `../`, `../../db`, making all four real ones invisible. Now 17 → **10** and 23 → **18**, with the reason opening on `assert, body-parser, document, mime-types, node:http`.
- **Three mechanisms, and each rule is exact rather than a heuristic.** `crate::` / `super::` / `self::` are Rust path qualifiers that resolve within the current crate, and all three are reserved words Cargo cannot publish under, so no dependency set can contain one. A JavaScript specifier beginning `./` or `../` is resolved against the importing file and never looked up in `node_modules`. Third and language-independent: `_analyzed_modules` folded `/` into `.` on the **src** side while the callee side was compared raw, so express's own `lib/utils` could never match its own analyzed `lib.utils` — a one-sided implementation of a contract `_module_matches` already documented. The fold now has **one home** (`normalize_module_separators`) instead of three.
- **A registry plus a gate, not a better literal.** The defect was a python-shaped rule applied to two languages whose module grammar differs from python's, and the way it repeats is a sixteenth language arriving with nobody having asked. Every language the boundary gate can reach now declares a `FirstPartyModuleGrammar` **with its basis**, an empty one being a valid answer that has to be written down, and a parity test drives the denominator from the shipped catalogues. It **fails open** — the opposite of `analyzer_disclosure`'s direction, because a missing grammar means "no reason to suppress this", and suppressing on a rule nobody wrote down is what would hide a real dependency.
- **The first cut was refuted by its own control run, on caddy, before it shipped.** Folding `/` and *then* running the existing prefix loop let `os/exec` shorten to `os` and match caddy's own `internal/filesystems/os.go`: **13 modules suppressed, ten of them Go standard library** — `crypto/tls`, `crypto/x509`, `os/exec` and seven more — because the repo owns a file called `crypto.go`. `os/exec` is the subprocess boundary. Shortening exists to strip a trailing **type** off a dotted callee slot and is not licensed by a change of separator, so the folded spelling is now tested **whole and never shortened**, pinned by a test carrying the caddy shape. express and bellman looked clean under the broken cut; only the Go repo showed it.
- **Non-destruction asserted on both controls in the same runs:** caddy unchanged (55 / 76 / 3 chains, all `violated`), httpx unchanged (0 modules moved). **No verdict flipped** on express or bellman either, and that is the point rather than a shortfall — what remains is stdlib enumeration, and only `python.yaml` declares any `module_completeness` while 14 of 15 catalogued languages declare zero (WI-lutuh). This makes that work's output readable; it does not substitute for it.
- **Two residuals found while measuring, filed rather than glossed.** The prefix-shortening hole above is **pre-existing for dotted languages** and still reproduces — a repo owning `myapp/os/helpers.py` and calling `os.path.join` reports coverage **complete** over stdlib `os` (INV-lakom, P1); this change declines to widen it but does not close it. And a repo referring to itself by its **published package name** (`bellman.VerificationError`, `github.com/caddyserver/caddy/v2/...`) is still reported, because the graph carries the fact as `package`-kind nodes that dependency packages share, and `supply_chain_tier` is `None` on every one of them (INV-vivok, P3).

- **A method name the Rust analyzer deliberately declines to resolve is now disclosed when the I/O catalogue calls it a sink (INV-polad).** `rust.py` carries a 77-name denylist of ubiquitous method names so that a name-only resolution cannot bind `x.load()` to a project `JoltDevice::load` — WI-bakak measured 22 of 29 false callers from `AtomicU64.load(Ordering::Relaxed)`, and that policy is correct and untouched. But **nine of those names are methods `rust.yaml` itself declares as I/O sinks** (`send`, `write`, `read`, `flush`, `recv`, `new`, `spawn`, `status`, `output` — ten rows, `write` appearing for both `io::Write` and `TcpStream`), so `sock.send(payload)` emitted **no edge at all** and a clean `net_send` verdict had nothing to disclose. Measured on an authored crate whose `exfiltrate()` does exactly that: a bare `confirmed`.
- **The denylist was doing two jobs and only one of them was ever justified.** It skips *resolution* — that is WI-bakak's fix — and it also skipped emitting the honest *unresolved-external* edge, which points at `external` and therefore could never have produced one of those false callers. The disclosure cost was invisible because the suppressed call leaves nothing behind to notice.
- **The set moved rather than being copied.** It now lives in `analyzer_disclosure` and `rust.py` imports it, so there is one object and not two — and here the two readers want *opposite* things from it (the analyzer asks "may I resolve this name?", `verify_claims` asks "what did I therefore never look at?"), which is exactly the condition under which two homes diverge unnoticed. The overlap with the catalogue is computed from the shipped catalogue at render time, so adding a row for a denylisted name extends the disclosure with nobody remembering to.
- **Emitting the suppressed calls was measured and rejected, not assumed away.** As ordinary unresolved-external edges they take total edges **+33%** (bellman 2867 → 3819) and **+67%** (tiktoken-rs 375 → 627), and the *external* edge population **+115%** and **+207%**, because the same denylist holds `clone` / `unwrap` / `map`. Paying that in centrality, dead-code, slice and supply-chain output on every Rust repository to make nine names disclosable is the wrong trade; declaring the gap says the same true thing for nothing.
- **A first reading of the overlap claimed all four `Command` rows were unreachable and a live control refuted it.** A crate calling `Command::new("sh").arg(..).status()` returns `violated` with the launch site named: the denylist governs the *instance-method* path, and `Command::new` is an associated-function call on a different path that catches the chain first. The helper is restricted to method-kind rows for that reason, with the worked example in its docstring.
- **Measured end to end with two controls.** A Rust crate whose only boundary reach is a denylisted name → `confirmed_with_caveats` (rc 3) naming all nine; a Go crate → **bare `confirmed`** (rc 0), so it is not a blanket downgrade; a Rust crate whose catalogued sink is *not* a denylisted name → bare `confirmed`, so the disclosure is not just reprinting the denylist. The caveat rides the taint arm too, because a run that discloses on one arm and stays silent on the other about the same unseen call is the asymmetry INV-nuhun names.
- **The remaining imprecision is stated, not hidden:** the caveat is language-level, so a Rust crate calling none of the nine still receives it. Scoping it needs a channel from the analyzer (which knows) to `verify_claims` (which does not), and that means schema surface — filed separately rather than bolted on here.


- **A clean boundary verdict disclosed nothing on languages holding 55% of the catalogued method sinks, because the disclosure channel selects on a key three analyzers never stamped (INV-fibis parity).** `verify_claims.untyped_receiver_sites` — the channel that makes a clean verdict name the sink call sites whose receiver could not be typed — selects on `meta.call_construct == "method"`. java, objc and rust emit the external call edge and never stamped that key, so each reached a bare `confirmed` and named nothing; kotlin and javascript emit no external instance-method call edge at all (WI-nasuf). **Measured across the nine catalogued languages that declare method-kind sinks**, one fixture apiece calling a sink from that language's *own* catalogue on an untypable receiver: 295 sinks inert-but-stampable (java 139, objc 122, rust 34), 232 with no edge to stamp (kotlin 181, javascript 51), 427 already firing (scala 162, python 143, swift 97, go 25). Method-kind is nearly the whole catalogue for several — java 98%, kotlin 97%, objc 95%, swift 93%, scala 89%.
- **java's stamp moved out of the branch that made the key mean something narrower than its name.** It was set only where the receiver's type resolved *and* was imported, so `call_construct` silently meant "a method call whose receiver I typed" rather than "a method call". It is now set once, for every receiver-bearing call, in the one place they all pass. The extensive reasoning already recorded there about the **phantom barrier** (naming the type shortens `w.doFinal` to `doFinal`, and `_register_sanitizer_callers` matches sanitizers on the short name) is unchanged and still load-bearing — the flag must not be dropped from either place. objc stamps unconditionally because a message send *is* a method call by construction; rust stamps on its existing `is_method_call`.
- **The stamp costs nothing on the recall side, and the gate it feeds is where that is visible.** With no module hint `io_boundary.gate_named_entry` refuses a method-kind hit **either way** — through the `call_construct == "method"` arm with the stamp, and through the `non_method` filter without it. What the stamp buys is that the verdict can *say so*. **Measured end to end:** a java file whose only fs_write reach is `u.mkdirs()` on an untypable receiver moved from a bare `confirmed` (rc 0) to `confirmed_with_caveats` (rc 3) naming the sites, while the **control arm** — same claim, a typed `java.io.File` receiver — stays `violated` (rc 1), so this is not a blanket downgrade.
- **The parity gate, not the three stamps, is the durable part.** `test_disclosure_parity.py` is driven from the shipped `io_primitives` catalogues rather than a hand-written list: any language declaring a method-kind sink must have a fixture whose untypable receiver produces the stamp, so a language arriving *later* fails there instead of reaching a silent clean verdict while every existing test stays green. The two no-edge languages are an explicit, bounded exemption pinned by its own test — the set is a measurement, not a place to put inconvenient languages.
- **A first draft of that measurement was wrong and the correction is recorded rather than quietly fixed.** Written with a *cast* receiver (`f.asInstanceOf[java.io.File].createNewFile()`), scala and swift both read as inert and the headline came out at 82% rather than 55%; a cast takes a different, bare-call branch in both analyzers, and the plain `receiver.method()` shape real code uses was already stamped correctly. The residual is real but far narrower: neither language stamps for a cast or otherwise complex receiver expression.

- **`--backend tree-sitter` did not disable the rust-analyzer backend, so the opt-out the tool advertises in its own security warning was inert (ADR-0045 ruling 4).** `check_rust_analyzer_disclosure` prints *"Disable per run: --backend tree-sitter"* alongside the disclosure that indexing **executes the analysed repository's `build.rs` and proc macros as the invoking user**. It did not disable anything. `cli.py`'s argv preprocessing translated `--backend` into the `HYPERGUMBO_RUST_ANALYZER` environment variable but implemented **only the positive arm** — the `tree-sitter` arm stripped the flag and set nothing — and because the flag was stripped, `should_use_rust_analyzer_backend` never received `backend_flag` in production at all and read only the variable, which the opt-out never cleared. The gate was not at fault: it takes flag and environment as separate injectable parameters and would have honoured a flag that reached it.
- **The failure direction is arbitrary code execution, and it bit exactly the users who followed the only durable opt-in available.** Exporting `HYPERGUMBO_RUST_ANALYZER=1` from a shell profile is currently the *only* way to make the choice persist (WI-sobig), so the affected user is one who enabled the backend globally and then deliberately opted out for a single repository they did not trust — and who executed its build scripts anyway, having been told by the tool that they had turned that off. **Reproduced end-to-end** on an authored Rust fixture before the fix: `HYPERGUMBO_RUST_ANALYZER=1 hypergumbo verify-claims <fixture> --backend tree-sitter` printed *"rust-analyzer backend ACTIVE"* and produced **10 nodes carrying `origin=['scip']`** out of 26. After the fix the same command prints *"installed but NOT enabled"* and produces **0 scip nodes** out of 16 — identical to the pure tree-sitter baseline — while the `--backend rust-analyzer` positive control still yields 10 of 26.
- **Fixed as a precedence rule with one owner, not a second special case.** New `hypergumbo_core.backend_selection.resolve_optin` owns the ADR-0045 ordering (**flag > environment > project config > user config > default**) and is consulted by both the CLI and the gate, so the two cannot come to disagree about what `--backend tree-sitter` means. Its return is **three-valued** — `True` / `False` / `None`, where `None` is *no tier has an opinion* and `False` is *a tier said no*. Both currently produce the same behaviour, so a two-valued version would pass identical tests; it would also be a latent bug, because the moment a config tier lands an explicit `HYPERGUMBO_RUST_ANALYZER=0` collapsed into "no opinion" starts **losing** to a config file that says on, silently re-enabling code execution the user turned off. Unobservable through any current caller, and therefore pinned by tests rather than left for the config work to rediscover.
- **Two homes for one fact were closed rather than added to.** The rust-analyzer selection vocabulary (env var name, on-spellings, off-spellings) now lives in `hypergumbo-core` — which the CLI can import and the optional `hypergumbo-lang-rust-analyzer` package cannot be assumed present for — and `gate.py` imports it instead of redeclaring it; `gate._is_env_enabled` / `_is_flag_enabled` were left in place for their existing tests but now **delegate**, so truthiness has exactly one definition. The two duplicated `--backend` arms in the argv loop were merged into one helper rather than a third copy being added. The environment variable survives as the **transport** to a gate that runs deep in the analyzer registry, under a documented rule its callers must keep: only ever write it with a decision sourced from a tier that *outranks* it, never from a config tier it is supposed to beat.
- **Two tests that pinned the old behaviour were updated, not deleted.** `test_backend_tree_sitter_does_not_set_env` and its `=`-form sibling asserted that the flag set nothing; setting nothing is what made the opt-out inert. Their premise (the flag is consumed, `rc == 0`) is kept and only the conclusion inverts. Separately noted rather than folded in: the preprocessing strips `--backend <anything>` before argparse runs, so the parser's `choices=` constraint never sees the global flag and an unrecognised value is **silently swallowed** — a real, smaller usability defect recorded on the tracker item instead of widened into this fix.

- **An UNANNOTATED `__init__` parameter is now typed from the repository's own constructor call sites (INV-fibis, interprocedural half).** The half above needs `__init__` itself to carry the evidence; this covers the shape none of those reach — `def __init__(self, sock)` with the type living only at `Client(socket.socket())`, usually in another file. `_collect_call_site_param_types` previously skipped every non-`function` callee, so constructor calls were never collected at all.
- **The off-by-one is the entire hazard, and it is why this was split out of #486 rather than folded in.** A caller writes `Client(sock)`, so the index is keyed on the CLASS symbol with the first *argument* at position 0, while `__init__`'s positional list starts at `self` and puts the same parameter at index 1. Getting this wrong does not fail loudly — a minted hint is **trusted downstream by design** (it bypasses both `gate_named_entry` and the `ambiguous_names` net), so an off-by-one types the *wrong parameter, confidently*. The correction is applied **once**, in the consumer, via an explicit `position_offset`; normalising at collection time would require resolving a cross-file class to its `__init__` symbol from a table that function does not have. **Verified by mutation, not by inspection:** with the offset set to 0, the two-parameter fixture emits `python:pathlib.Path:0-0:sendall:unresolved` — `sendall` attributed to `pathlib.Path` — and `test_two_params_of_different_types` is the test that catches it while every other test in the class still passes.
- **CORPUS EFFECT: ZERO, ON ALL FOUR REPOS, AND THE EXPLANATION IS MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** django/db, mitmproxy, httpx and poetry produce byte-identical edge populations. The first guess — "these codebases are annotated, so there is nothing to infer" — is **refuted**: django/db has **241** unannotated `__init__` parameters that become own fields and mitmproxy has **236** (httpx and poetry genuinely have 0, being fully annotated). The population is large; the pass simply does not reach it. Instrumenting the collector shows why: across 123 django/db files it produces **3** call-site hint keys in total (2 of them constructor keys), and across 250 mitmproxy files **16** (13 constructor keys). **The binding constraint is argument-type inference, not this wiring** — an argument only supplies a type when it is a binding-checked external constructor or resolves to a PascalCase class, deliberately narrow since the first cut of that inference read `json.dumps` as a type. On mitmproxy the constructor half nonetheless supplies **13 of the 16** keys in that index, so it is not inert; the funnel from "constructor argument typed" to "field used as a receiver whose method is catalogued" is simply long.
- **What the zero does establish is the precision side:** no edge changed anywhere in the cohort, so this cannot have added a false positive — the relevant guarantee for recall work landing inside measurement `0001`'s pre-registered `<50%` band. The agreement rule (a position two call sites disagree about is dropped, never tie-broken) and annotation-precedence are inherited unchanged, and both are pinned by tests.

- **An own instance field of EXTERNAL type now carries its module to the call site (INV-fibis, recall half).** `class_field_types` is `dict[str, dict[str, Symbol]]` — **Symbol-valued, so by construction it can only ever hold an in-repo class**. Locals have had both halves since WI-fuvuj (`var_types` for in-repo, `external_var_types` for catalogued external types); fields had only the first. So `s = socket.socket(); s.sendall(x)` reached the catalogue and `self.sock = socket.socket(); self.sock.sendall(x)` did not — two spellings of one fact, only one of them visible. `class_external_field_types` is the missing counterpart, populated from the three shapes an `__init__` can establish a field type from (annotated parameter, external constructor, annotated field declaration), each reusing that shape's existing resolver so the INV-kipor binding check is inherited rather than re-decided.
- **THIS SUPPLIES EVIDENCE; IT DOES NOT WIDEN A MATCHER, and that distinction is the whole design.** INV-nuhun established that the sink/boundary lookup refuses an untyped method receiver **twice** over, and that both refusals ARE the deliberate closure of INV-tapat and INV-maluk (both `satisfied`; INV-maluk was reopened over precisely this consumer). Nothing here touches that gate — it hands the gate the module hint it has always asked for, which is the only sound way past it. The key is namespaced `self.<field>` rather than the bare field name, unlike the in-repo merge: `external_var_types` is **mutated as the body is walked**, so a bare-keyed field type would be silently overwritten by a same-named local assigned later and would thereafter answer for the field. Namespacing makes the collision structurally impossible instead of relying on an ordering that happens to hold.
- **Measured decisively on the isolating fixture, and honestly on the corpus — the two disagree, and the corpus number is the headline.** On a fixture whose `Client.__init__` takes `sock: socket.socket` and whose `send()` reads `API_KEY` and calls `self.sock.sendall(...)`, arms differing in exactly this hunk move **both** the boundary and taint claims from `confirmed_with_caveats` (rc 3, each naming `svc.py:17 sendall()`) to **`violated`** (rc 1). The disclosure INV-nuhun shipped converts into a finding, which is the whole thesis: every receiver typed is one that need not be caveated. **On four real repositories it changes no verdict at all.** django/db and httpx: 0 sites retyped. mitmproxy: 6. poetry: 42. **48 call sites newly named across the cohort, 0 lost, 0 module changes of any other kind** — but only **2 of the 48 reach a catalogued primitive** (`pathlib.Path.exists`, `fs_read`); the rest name `cleo.io.io.IO` (31), poetry-core packages, and `typing.BinaryIO`, none of which the catalogue knows (INV-fotav's third-party gap). Poetry's verify-claims output is **byte-identical** across arms.
- **And the corpus null is WEAKER EVIDENCE THAN IT LOOKS, which is stated rather than netted out.** Poetry has no clean verdict to improve — its four claims are 2 `inconclusive` (uncovered-module gate) and 2 `violated` — so this is a **zero-against-zero** comparison, the pre-arm screening failure L15 records, not a demonstration of inertness. The verdict-level win is shown on the fixture and **not** on real code, and no claim is made that it was. What the corpus does establish is the precision side: **zero new findings means this cannot have added a false one**, which is the strongest available answer for recall work landing inside measurement `0001`'s pre-registered `<50%` band.
- **A test that pinned the old behaviour was UPDATED, not deleted or weakened.** `test_external_ctor_field_emits_placeholder` asserted the bare placeholder, and its own comment recorded why that was unsatisfying — *"the type IS known to the receiver-constructor table, which is why this is an EMIT gap and not a type-inference gap; the identical LOCAL already emits."* Its premise (INV-jujoh's guarantee that an edge must exist at all) is kept and asserted first; only its conclusion moves, and it now also asserts the edge is no longer anonymous.

- **A clean TAINT verdict certified absence of a flow it had already told the reader, in the same invocation, that it could not adjudicate (INV-nuhun).** INV-fibis's defect in the other arm, and the two arms disagreeing is what makes it its own item rather than a residue. Running one boundary claim and one taint claim over a three-line function that reads `API_KEY` and `sendall`s it through an unannotated parameter printed these adjacent: `! [NET-SEND] confirmed_with_caveats … a method the net_send catalogue declares is called on a receiver whose type could not be determined` and then `✓ [host-secret-no-network] confirmed — No unsanitized host_secret data reaches network zone.` The **silent** verdict carried the tick and the **disclosing** one carried the warning, so the arm that had looked less read as the more confident. A clean taint verdict now carries the same two disclosures the boundary arm carries: the site-naming `untyped_receiver` caveat, scoped by the claim's prohibited **sink zone**, and the unscoped `unknown_receiver_scope`.
- **THE FIX IS NOT "WIDEN RECALL", and the item's own `root_cause` field invites exactly that error.** It names one suppression — `_lookup_named_entry` dropping method-kind rows that carry no module hint — and there are **two**, both firing: `gate_named_entry` refuses an untyped method call outright at `call_construct == "method"`, and even with that removed its `non_method` filter would still refuse it, because the index bucket for `sendall` holds only method-kind rows. Verified at the unit: `_lookup_named_entry(hits, "sendall", None, …)` and `(…, "external", …)` both return `None`, while `(…, "socket.socket", …)` returns the `network` sink. **Both refusals are the deliberate closure of INV-tapat and INV-maluk** (both `satisfied`; INV-maluk was REOPENED in June precisely because taint's sink matcher was the third consumer that ignored the rule, a ~5541-violation false-positive cascade), and matching a method-kind sink on a bare short name is verbatim the "unfiltered short-name fallback" INV-maluk's statement forbids. So the flow can only be built by **typing the receiver** — INV-fibis's recall half — and where the receiver cannot be typed the verdict must disclose rather than assert. `test_a_typed_receiver_is_matched_not_caveated` is the positive control that the gate opens the instant real evidence arrives.
- **Zone-scoped, and asked of the SINK catalogue rather than mapped from boundaries.** Every python sink hypergumbo ships is auto-derived from an I/O primitive through `AUTO_SINK_ZONE_MAP` (measured: all 214, every zone reachable), so inverting that map would return an identical answer today and would have been less code. It is not what this does: the equality holds by construction and is enforced nowhere, so a repository declaring its own sink via `--taint-sinks` names a method the I/O catalogue has never heard of, and the inversion would silently disclose nothing about it. Under-reporting a caveat is quieter than a false `confirmed` but is the same species — asserting a completeness never established — which is the species this item exists to close. The two arms share **one** site walk (`_untyped_receiver_call_sites`), extracted when the second consumer appeared rather than after they drifted; an arm-disagreement item fixed with two hand-maintained copies of the walk would be the defect reappearing inside its own fix.
- **Measured on the filed repro, both signs, plus a falsifiability control.** Arm 1 (unannotated receiver): `host-secret-no-network` moves from bare `confirmed` to `confirmed_with_caveats` naming `leak.py:12 sendall()` under the *network sink catalogue*, while the `host-secret-no-host-fs` control still fires `violated` in the same run. Arm 2 (`sock: socket.socket`, otherwise identical): **still `violated`** — a typed receiver is SEEN, not caveated. Zone scoping holds on real code: of six generic taint claims, only the `network` one acquires the site-naming caveat; `sendall` does not leak into the subprocess, host-fs, database or logging verdicts. Falsifiability control — a fixture with no untyped method call at all — returns **rc 0 with all six claims bare `confirmed` and zero caveats**, so this is not a blanket downgrade.
- **hypergumbo's own security self-proof was silent about this, and now is not — 18 of 18 self-claims changed.** Every claim in `docs/hypergumbo.claims.yaml` is a taint claim, which is why the boundary-arm half of this work (PR #481) left the self-proof untouched and this half moved all of it. The `check-self-claims` gate caught the drift and was re-recorded only after the diff was reviewed against exactly what it warns about: **0 verdicts moved, 0 `credited_flows` moved, 18 caveat-only additions, 0 caveat removals.** The disclosure's noise is nameable rather than hidden: of the 119 sites raised against each `host_fs` claim, **63 are `.replace()`** — overwhelmingly `str.replace`, the literal INV-maluk collision. The remainder is not noise (`mkdir` 19, `write_text` 12, `write` 11, `unlink` 9, `rename`/`truncate`/`touch`), and the `network` claim raises `write` 11, `close` 6, `send_json` 1. This is what `_MAX_REPORTED_UNTYPED_SITES` was for: above five sites the sentence names distinct METHODS rather than locations, so a reader discounts `replace` and acts on `mkdir`. SECURITY.md's generator now says so, and states the bound the self-proof had never quantified: **6,541 of 9,267 method call sites (70.6%) in hypergumbo's own tree have a receiver the analysis could not type.**
- **Consumer impact, stated plainly: taint claims that previously exited 0 may now exit 3**, the same fail-closed direction the boundary arm moved in, and no envelope bump (a new caveat key is additive). The unscoped caveat is claim-independent, so on a repository with any untyped receiver it attaches to *every* clean taint verdict — five of six on the repro fixture — which is the same volume trade already accepted on the boundary arm, and is why the figures are reused rather than recomputed: one invocation must not print two different "N of M method call sites" denominators for one repository.

- **A clean boundary verdict now discloses that it is CLOSED-WORLD over the receivers the analysis could type — the unscoped half of INV-fibis, and the half that was demonstrably false on real code.** The shipped `untyped_receiver` caveat matches the called method's NAME against primitives catalogued for the claimed boundary, and **a name lookup is precisely what an untyped receiver makes meaningless**. Measured, not argued: a hunt across **32,593 non-test files** found **90 real scopes** whose untyped receiver calls a genuine I/O verb outside the 120 catalogued method-kind names (`post` 56, `upload_file` 6, `upload` 6, `put_item`/`get_item` 6, `download_file` 5, `execute_command` 3) — and the existing caveat fired on **zero** of them. Every one was held back instead by a **coverage gap** (68 uncatalogued-module, 13 opaque-launch, 5 unsupported-language), each of which the project intends to close, so the protection was **anti-correlated with the tool improving**.
- **The failure was reproduced end-to-end on unmodified real code, not on a fixture.** polis's `deploy/deploy-static-assets.py` is a CLI whose purpose is uploading static assets to S3: `def upload_file(s3_client, ...)` takes an unannotated parameter and calls `s3_client.upload_file(...)`. With a realistic project overlay that audits `boto3` but omits `upload_file` from its rows, the shipped CLI returned **rc 0 and "never sends data over the network: confirmed"**. The documented remedy for the *other* half of INV-fibis — project-local overlays (INV-fotav) — is exactly what removes the accidental protection for this half. It now returns **rc 3**, naming `upload_file` among the methods it could not adjudicate.
- **New caveat kind `unknown_receiver_scope`, carrying a count, a denominator and the distinct method names.** A bare "some receivers were unknown" is unactionable; 1-of-3 and 1-of-3000 are different verdicts, and `append`/`items` reads very differently from `upload_file`/`post` (34.7% of untyped-receiver sites across the corpus are builtin container/string method names, which is the calibration a countless disclosure would deny the reader). **This qualifies a verdict rather than withholding one** — the unscoped *downgrade* to `inconclusive` was measured on 2026-08-11 and remains refused — and it is **capable of not firing**: an analysis whose receivers are all typed still returns a bare `confirmed` at exit 0, pinned by `test_all_typed_receivers_stays_bare_confirmed`, because a caveat that is always present is one its reader learns to discount.
- **Consumer impact, stated plainly: claims that previously exited 0 now exit 3.** No envelope bump — a new caveat kind is additive and consumers already had to tolerate kinds they do not know — but *which* claims are caveated changes, and a gate written `verify-claims … || exit 1` fails on 3 where it previously passed. That is the documented fail-closed direction. The four clean-verdict paths (`must_not_exist` fully-clean and opaque-qualified, `max_chains` likewise) now build their caveat list through **one** builder, so a future disclosure cannot be attached to three of them and forgotten on the fourth.

- **A method call on an own instance field of external or unknown type emitted NO EDGE AT ALL, so every honesty mechanism was structurally blind to it and a boundary claim returned a bare `confirmed` at exit 0 (INV-jujoh).** The demonstration is two classes in one file whose calls are character-identical and differ only in where the field is assigned: with `self.sock = sock` in `__init__`, a `net_send must_not_exist` claim over `self.sock.sendall(payload)` returned **`confirmed` / "No net_send chains found." / rc 0**; with the same assignment in a non-`__init__` method, it returned `confirmed_with_caveats` and the caveat NAMED the site. `sendall` is a CATALOGUED stdlib primitive — no third-party module, no overlay, nothing exotic. The cause was an emit gap, not a type-inference gap: `class_field_types` is `dict[str, dict[str, Symbol]]`, **Symbol-valued, so by construction it can hold only an IN-REPO class**, and there is no external counterpart (unlike locals, which have both `var_types` and `external_var_types`). An own field of external type therefore fell between two sites — Case 2f could not resolve it, and py.py's Site-3 placeholder emit excluded it via `field_name not in own_field_names`. Silence is upstream of every mechanism: no edge for the `untyped_receiver` caveat to name, none for the uncovered-module gate to disclose, none for taint to walk.
- **The guards now gate the STAMP, not the EMIT — and withholding the stamp is the whole safety argument.** Site-3's own-field exclusion was correct for what it was written for: the stamp routes to the `inherited_calls` linker, whose walk is over PARENT classes, a premise that is false for an own field, and stamping one invites the shadow FP where a child re-declares a parent field name with a different type. So the fix is the item's route (b), not route (a): an own field takes the **same placeholder edge without `inherited_field_receiver`**. Every pre-existing shadow-FP lock passes unchanged, because each asserts the absence of the *stamp* (or of a resolved `ast_call_inherited_field` edge), never the absence of an *edge* — `test_self_field_untyped_own_field_no_hint` was passing VACUOUSLY (there was no edge to carry a stamp) and is now non-vacuous.
- **Measured, in both signs, arms differing in exactly this hunk.** django `db/backends/utils.py` goes from **0 of 10 to 10 of 10** `self.<attr>.<method>()` sites carrying an edge that names the method, including `self.cursor.execute()` at lines 103 and 105 — Django's SQL execution path. Across django/db, mitmproxy, meson and httpx: **+1,370 call edges, 0 relationships lost, 0 new edges carrying the stamp, 0 minted resolved.** `resolved_total` moved only up (+8, all from `method-call-recovery-linker` at confidence 0.4; two spot-checked against source are true positives — `self.addons` is a dotted-constructor field Case 2f cannot type, and `self.whitespaces` is genuinely a `WhitespaceNode`). **The one loss, stated rather than netted out:** 5 stamped edges across django/db and meson disappear, because `edge_key` is `(src, dst, edge_type)` and deliberately line-insensitive, so a new own-field edge at an earlier line collapses with a stamped edge to the same target and wins on encounter order. It costs nothing measurable — `ast_call_inherited_field`, the resolved output those stamps feed, is **unchanged in all four repos (0/7/11/0 in both arms)** — and the collapsed site is preserved in `meta["call_lines"]`, not discarded.
- **What this did NOT move, with the denominator.** Across 11 real scopes x 4 boundary claims = **44 verdict pairs (poetry x9 sub-packages, httpx, django/db), ZERO verdicts moved.** The reason is not that the fix is inert: **arm A produced no bare `confirmed` anywhere in that population** — every verdict was `inconclusive` (the uncovered-module gate firing first) or `violated`. That is the anti-correlation this item was filed on, visible in the data: bare `confirmed` is rare *today*, so honesty currently rests on the uncovered-module gate rather than on the caveats, and as catalogue and overlay coverage grow — a stated goal — `inconclusive` converts to `confirmed` and this blind spot would surface at scale. The verdict-level movement is therefore demonstrated on the isolating fixture (bare `confirmed` -> `confirmed_with_caveats` naming the site), **not on real code**, and no claim is made that it was.

- **`verify-claims` crashed with `RecursionError` on a real repository, exiting 1 with an empty stdout — the same exit code it returns for VIOLATED (INV-gotir).** `ddg_build._walk_functions` was a pure pre-order traversal that recursed once per AST child, so the Python stack depth WAS the tree-sitter AST depth, one frame per level, against CPython's default limit of 1000. Machine-generated code exceeds that without being pathological: keda's `vendor/go.temporal.io/api/workflowservice/v1/request_response.pb.go` (725,832 bytes of generated protobuf) measures an **AST depth of 1171**, because one `const` is a string built as `"..." + "..." + "..."` **1,165 times** and `+` is left-associative, giving a 1,165-deep left spine. **A CI gate written `verify-claims ... || exit 1` could not tell "claims violated" from "the analysis crashed and examined nothing"**, and a JSON consumer got a zero-byte file instead of a verdict list — the certifying-what-it-did-not-establish failure INV-javam and INV-fibis were filed for, arriving through the error path instead of the verdict path. The walk is now an explicit stack. **Measured: keda goes from rc=1 / 0 bytes / `RecursionError` to rc=1 / 9,700 bytes / six verdicts** — the exit code is unchanged in both, which is precisely why the fix was needed.
- **The transformation is exactly equivalent, and both halves of that are checked.** There is no work after the child loop, so the recursion carried no state a worklist cannot hold and no post-order step; children are pushed reversed so `pop()` yields them left-to-right and visit order — which `out` accumulates in — is preserved. Asserted rather than argued: an A/B on poetry with arms differing only in this hunk is **byte-identical**. `sys.setrecursionlimit` was rejected because it trades a catchable `RecursionError` for a C-stack segfault, strictly worse for a failure already indistinguishable from a verdict.
- **It is a class, and the class is sized rather than assumed.** An AST sweep finds **72** self-recursive child-loop walks across core and all three language packages. A corpus AST-depth census (files ≥60KB, measured iteratively so the instrument cannot hit the bug it measures) puts the realized exposure narrowly: keda has 8 files at depth ≥500 and **7 at ≥900**, all generated Go under `vendor/`, six of them `golang.org/x/text/unicode/norm/tables*.go` at **947–948 — within 5% of the limit**, so this is not one pathological file; dash.js, caddy and mitmproxy top out at 74, 37 and 79. `survey` over the same input does not crash and the Go analyzer parses the offending file fine (3,551 symbols), so the DDG walk was the single live offender. The remaining 71 are filed on the item rather than rewritten wholesale, since none has a measured failure. Note `_SKIP_DIRS` excludes `node_modules` and `.venv` but **not** `vendor/`, which is why the vendored Go tree is walked at all.

- **A receiver that arrives as a bare parameter is now typed from the call sites that pass it (INV-fibis residual).** WI-zilag routed an ANNOTATED parameter's type into the module slot, so `def h(p: pathlib.Path, x): p.write_text(x)` reaches the catalogue. The residual INV-fibis pinned is the same shape without the annotation — `def send(sock, payload): sock.sendall(payload)` — where the receiver names no module, the edge carries the `external` placeholder, and every consumer refuses it as an untyped method call. A parameter's type is not inferable from its body; what IS available is every argument the repository passes at that position, collected across all files before edge extraction and admitted only where EVERY observed call site agrees. Measured on the item's own two-arm fixture: the unannotated arm moves from `inconclusive` to **`violated`, 1 net_send chain — identical to the annotated arm**, while a third arm with no call site to learn from correctly stays `confirmed_with_caveats`, because there is genuinely nothing to infer.
- **THE CORPUS MEASURED IT AT ZERO, AND THAT IS THE HEADLINE, NOT A FOOTNOTE.** A/B on poetry, mitmproxy and pretix — arms differing only in this pass, two claim files each (ten boundary claims plus the six generic taint claims) — produced **byte-identical output in every one of the twelve runs**. Not "no regressions": no change at all. The pass is not inert — mitmproxy's unresolved method-call edges go `external` 6541 → 6526 with typed 2237 → **2252**, +15 edges strictly better typed and the total unchanged at 8778 — but **not one of the newly-typed modules is in the io_primitives catalogue** (`mitmproxy.http.Headers`, `argparse.ArgumentParser`, `django.core.mail.SafeMIMEMultipart`, `collections.Counter`, `rest_framework.test.APIClient`), so no boundary and no verdict moves. For a recall widening landing into the pre-registered `<50%` precision band this is the strongest available answer to the band's concern — it adds **zero** reported findings, so it cannot have added a false one — and it is equally a statement that the payoff is latent rather than demonstrated on these three repositories.
- **Why the reach is small, measured rather than guessed.** Of the hints the pass agrees on: poetry 18, of which **18 are on parameters that already carry an annotation** (a fully annotated modern codebase has almost no residual); mitmproxy 13 (8 annotated); pretix 51 (22 annotated, 17 on a parameter that is never a method receiver). The annotation always wins, so a well-typed repository leaves this pass nothing to do — which is the correct behaviour and also why the corpus zero is not evidence of a defect.
- **The corpus also caught a defect review did not, and the first cut shipped it wrong.** Resolving an argument expression through import bindings alone asserts that `x = json.dumps(y)` names a TYPE — it has the identical AST shape to `s = socket.socket()`. Measured on poetry: **7 of 19 agreed hints (37%) were function results** (`json.dumps`, `typing.cast`, `tomlkit.inline_table`, `parse_constraint`, two `get_*` helpers). Not merely useless: `io_boundary._module_matches` does bidirectional SUBSTRING matching, so `json.dumps` in a module slot can match a catalogue module `json` and mint a confident false boundary plus a false taint sink — the exact class WI-zilag documents for the raw-annotation-text shortcut it refused. An argument must now be shown to name a type by one of two routes: the receiver-type catalogue already knows the constructor (WI-fuvuj's binding-checked `_external_constructor_type`, which is what admits lowercase `socket.socket`), or the resolved path's final segment is PascalCase (PEP 8's class convention, which is what admits `requests.Session` — requests has zero catalogue rows per INV-fotav, so route one cannot). A convention is a heuristic and the code says so; what makes it acceptable is the direction of its errors, since a lowercase-named class is refused into silence while the thing it prevents is asserted with confidence.
- **Scope, stated as scope.** Bare-name callees only (a call through a receiver would need receiver typing to resolve its callee — circular); positional arguments only; callee kind `function` (a bare-name call cannot target a bound method, so positions would be off by one against `self`); ONE HOP, so a parameter typed by this pass is not itself evidence for a further parameter. An annotation the resolver cannot turn into a module still blocks the call-site route: the author wrote a type there, and that the resolver failed is not permission to substitute a guess.

- **Per-situation taint precision measured, and the row denominator was hiding a factor of 2.9 (`docs/measurements/0004`).** `INV-karud`'s collapse raised a question it could not answer from where it stood — *which way per-situation precision moves* — because it depends on whether the large groups adjudicate true or false. Measured on measurement `0001`'s own census cohort (caddy, mitmproxy, poetry, express, apollo-server), one adjudication, two units: **11.1% per row (33 TP of 297) versus 32.2% per situation (19 TP of 59)**. It moves UP, and the mechanism is that false positives concentrate in the largest groups — caddy's `cmdRun` emits 76 rows across three claims, every one of them false (its `os.Getenv` reads are all compared to `""`, its `runtime.GOOS` is a `switch` subject), and collapses to 3 situations, while 12 of the 19 true situations stand for a single pair each. **Zero unadjudicable, against 19 of 104 in `0001`** — every situation named a file a reader could open; that is not offered as `INV-rozob` closure evidence, since those anchors are in-repo file nodes the collapse never touched. `ddg` scores 80.0% and is still too small to filter on (5 of 59 situations holding 4 of 19 true positives), and `ddg_mixed` again scores BELOW `structural` — `0001`'s reversal reproduced in a second population, so the label remains uncalibrated. **The method is weaker than `0001`'s and says so first:** single-pass and non-blind, where `0001` used a blind second agent plus a third on disagreement; ties were pre-committed to FALSE POSITIVE, so both rates are floors. It does not move `WI-sivuz`'s `<50%` band, which was named before any interval existed — it changes what the number measures, not the ranking rule.

- **A taint verdict counted `|sources| x |sinks|` rows where the analysis had established one fact, so its evidence scaled with the CATALOGUE rather than with the code (INV-karud).** Both propagators emitted one `TaintFlowFinding` per (source call site, sink call site) pair reachable in the call graph, and each such record asserts "primitive P1 reached primitive P2". For a flow the ADR-0017 §3a walk adjudicated (`analysis_method == "ddg"`) that claim is earned. For every other flow inclusion rested on call-graph reachability alone, and n x m records assert n x m data dependences the walk established none of. Censused over six repositories: **359 reported flows describing 78 situations, 4.60x**, with 281 of 359 rows (78%) restating a situation already reported and caddy's `cmd/commandfuncs.go cmdRun` alone emitting 76. **Two multipliers, where the item had named one:** 2.87x the source/sink product inside a symbol, and 1.60x distinct call-graph ROUTES to the same primitive pair — distinct rows only because the consumer's flow identity keys on `path`, a field documented as one witness route rather than the route set. A fix keyed on the pair would have left the second, so the grouping keys on neither primitive. An unadjudicated finding is now collapsed to one per (taint label, source symbol, sink zone, `sanitized`, `source_boundary`, `analysis_method`), carrying `source_primitives` / `sink_primitives` / `sink_symbols` as module-qualified sets: *symbol S reads {P..Pn} and reaches zone Z via {Q..Qm}*. Names are qualified because `Do` is not checkable against a catalogue and `net/http.Client.Do` is (clause a1).
- **Adjudicated flows are deliberately NOT collapsed, and the exemption was priced before it was taken.** `ddg` means the walk confirmed a reaching-def chain from the variable the source defines to a use at the sink call site — that *is* a pair claim. It is **6 of the 359 census flows (1.7%)**, so collapsing them would have traded precision the analysis earned for almost none of the noise.
- **Two fields are in the grouping key that the proposal did not name.** `sanitized`, because a sanitized flow is excluded from the violation set and a mixed group would have to be counted both ways; and `source_boundary`, because WI-vazal split `net_recv` / `ipc_recv` / `db_read` inside the single `untrusted_input` label precisely so "a request body reached the database" stays separable from "a row read from the database reached the database". Their presence is also what makes collapsing BEFORE the consumer's filters sound: every filter `verify_taint_claim` applies — label, zone, `sanitized`, and `_source_scope`'s production test, which reads `source_symbol` and nothing else — tests a field that is in the key, so no group can be half-included. Asserted by a test rather than left as reasoning.
- **The pair count is disclosed, not traded away.** `details` gains a `spanning N source->sink pair(s)` clause (silent when it equals the situation count, so the common single-pair verdict stays byte-identical), and every evidence row carries `collapsed_flow_count`. Summing that across the evidence list would be wrong above `_MAX_EVIDENCE_ROWS`, which is why the total is computed over all violations and printed.
- **A/B on findings in BOTH signs before shipping, per the INV-nizom precedent** (an obviously-correct consumer rule once removed 51 io-boundary matches, 49 of them TRUE). Arms differing only in this change, same six repositories at the same checkouts, same claims file, per-arm `XDG_CACHE_HOME`, arm A a detached worktree at the parent commit and asserted to lack the collapse before any measurement. **359 rows -> 80 situations (4.49x); 0 verdicts moved; 0 (source symbol, source primitive, sink primitive, sink symbol) tuples from arm A missing from arm B.** Per repo: caddy 219->23, sops 39->11, poetry 13->5, mitmproxy 60->26, knex 25->12, express 3->3. The 80 exceeds the census's 78 because this key is FINER than the census's (source_symbol, claim) — the extra two are boundary/method splits, and the 4.49x is correspondingly conservative. Evidence completeness was asserted first (`evidence_count == len(evidence)` on every verdict in both arms), so the coverage check ran over the whole population rather than a first-hundred slice.
- **What this does NOT close, stated so it is not re-derived.** Clause (a2) remains unmet for every unadjudicated flow. Removal is UNSOUND on this substrate — the call graph emits one edge per (caller, callee), so an edge's `line` is *a* line and not every line (`printEnvironment` calls `fmt.Printf` twelve times and the edge records the first) — which is why the DDG arm is confirm-only by design and why (a2) cannot be met by dropping flows without inventing false negatives. This makes the CLAIM honest; it does not make the analysis precise.
- **`verify-claims --json` envelope 2.0 -> 2.1.** Not purely additive, and like 1.5 that is the point: `evidence_count` and the `details` count now report SITUATIONS, so a 2.0 consumer tracking a row total sees it fall without any verdict moving and without any (source symbol, source primitive, sink primitive, sink symbol) tuple being dropped. The old quantity stays reachable via the `spanning N source->sink pair(s)` clause and the per-row `collapsed_flow_count`, and the witness scalars keep their names and stay valid — each is a member of its own set. The behaviour-map `SCHEMA_VERSION` is untouched: `TaintFlowFinding.to_dict` has no production consumer and taint findings do not appear in `docs/schema.json`.
- **A hand-written test mock re-declared the dataclass and drifted the moment it gained a field (`test_cfg.py`).** `_MockTaintFinding` restated nine of `TaintFlowFinding`'s fields; adding `sink_symbols` made every DDG-target-selector test raise `AttributeError` on a stand-in that could not have been wrong about anything else. It now constructs the real dataclass. `cfg.select_ddg_targets` itself read the witness `sink_symbol` and now reads the set — honest scope: that function has no production caller today, so this is a latent reader corrected, not an observed regression.

- **The commit-msg brand scrub removed the half of a trailer that implies a vendor and kept the half that names one (INV-kunot).** The trailer loop tested only the VALUE against the brand regex and reassembled `${key}${ws}${val}`, so `Claude-Session:` passed through verbatim on **13 commits** — an accruing leak, not a historical one, since the harness appends that trailer to every agent commit. A branded key now becomes `Racial-Capitalism-Is-Bad:` and **forces the value** through the absurd-phrase rewriter even when the value carries no brand of its own: a vendor-named key means the whole trailer is vendor provenance, and a scrubbed key over an intact value would still publish the session id it points at. Three git-semantic keys (`Signed-off-by`, `Co-authored-by`, `Co-committed-by`) are never renamed, because `git interpret-trailers`, `%(trailers:key=…)` and DCO validation all key off the exact token. **The body sweep is now bounded** to the first and last 10 body lines: a body hit deletes the whole line with no marker, and the pattern list contains ordinary English words — `falcon` is a framework this project supports and ships `falcon.yaml` for, so an ordinary commit about the falcon analyzer had its subject mangled and a body line silently deleted. Subject and trailers are still scanned unconditionally; only the body sweep is narrowed, which trades coverage in the middle of a long body for precision. Seven scenarios added to `.githooks/test_hooks.sh` (42 passing), including idempotence under `--amend` — the design rests on the replacement key not matching the brand regex, an invariant spanning two files.

- **Schema 0.20.1 → 0.20.2.** `Edge.meta` gains `call_arg_shape` (INV-fubag). An addition, hence a patch bump: the key is sparse and opt-in, so its absence is the conservative reading and every artifact written before this version stays valid and unchanged under it.

- **A taint sink that provably cannot receive the tainted value no longer reports a flow (INV-fubag).** `docs/measurements/0003` adjudicated 34 false positives and found 24 of them on sink calls taking **no arguments at all** — `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory()`, `TemporaryFile()`. Taint models a flow as the tainted value being an argument to the sink call or its receiver, so such a call cannot carry one: this is a proof, not a heuristic, and it costs no recall. Producers stamp `meta['call_arg_shape']='literal_only'` only when they can prove BOTH halves — every argument is a literal constant, AND the receiver is absent (a bare-name call) or an imported module. Absence of the key means "cannot prove" and keeps flowing, so every behavior map written before the key existed is unaffected. Deduplication merges the marker conservatively: `TemporaryDirectory()` and `TemporaryDirectory(dir=tainted)` in one function collapse to a single edge, and without that the survivor's marker would silence a real flow on the strength of a different call site. **Measured end-to-end on the same three repositories:** of the 35 flows the construction-edge widening added, 29 are removed and 6 survive — marginal precision **2.9% → 16.7%** — and the one true positive (`expanduser` → `ZipFile(path,"w")`) survives, which was the acceptance criterion filed with the item. 5 pre-existing false positives go too (`logging.error("literal")`; the f-string call four lines below it still flows). **The receiver half was found by measurement, not review:** an argument-only first cut stamped `Path(DATA_DIR).mkdir(parents=False, exist_ok=True)` — both arguments literal, while the receiver decides which directory is created — silencing 9 real findings before the verification run caught it.

- **Measured what the construction-edge taint widening actually cost, and it is not good (`docs/measurements/0003`).** INV-lalad made `instantiates` traversable by the taint walk, which is a recall widening landing into the pre-registered `<50%` precision band. A delta census — baseline clone at the fix's parent commit, same instrument both arms, isolated analysis caches so neither arm could read the other's behaviour map — finds **35 flows added across six repositories, 0 removed, 1 true positive: marginal precision 2.9%** against a ≈41% baseline. The one true positive is the thesis confirmed on a second sink (mitmproxy's `expanduser` → `ZipFile(path,"w")`, invisible before for the same PascalCase reason as `subprocess.Popen`). Of the 34 false positives, **24 land on constructor calls that take no arguments at all** — `TemporaryDirectory()`, `TemporaryFile()` — which cannot carry taint by argument, a proof rather than a heuristic; filed as INV-fubag. Not a new defect: `content-not-argument` and `co-location-function` were already 29 of measurement 0001's 57 false positives, and this change fed a large new class of sinks into them. Also recorded: 0001's census population has drifted 60 → 298 flows in nine days, so a straight re-run would have attributed other work's drift to this change.

- **Object creation had no agreed edge representation, and construction edges were invisible to dead-code reachability (INV-kahig).** Ruby resolved `Klass.new` to `Klass#initialize` and emitted `calls`; the other eight analyzers that emit `instantiates` (dart, csharp, java, py, js_ts, php, cpp, verilog) emitted that — so `survey` filtered on `type == "instantiates"` silently omitted every Ruby object creation, and a linker held `frozenset({"calls", "instantiates"})` to paper over the split. Ruby now emits `instantiates`. Its **dst is deliberately unchanged** at the initializer symbol: WI-fagit independently converged C# onto that same choice (PR #689) because anchoring on the class conflates "referenced as a type" with "instantiated" and breaks reverse-slice. Making that change safe first required a defect it exposed: `cli.py`'s dead-code BFS keyed on `{calls, dispatches_to, wraps}`, so **construction edges carried no reachability at all** — measured directly against `_bfs_reachable`, on `main -[instantiates]-> init -[calls]-> polish` with `seeds={main}` the walk reaches only `main`. **The realized effect is narrower than that mechanism suggests, and the difference was measured rather than assumed (INV-rolok).** The hole was real for every emitting language, but false dead code only results where the edge's dst is a *callable* — Ruby's initializer, or C#'s constructor symbol post-WI-fagit. Where the dst is the **class** the fix is inert, because the only edges leaving a class node are `contains` / `extends` / `decorated_by`, none of which confer reachability (correctly — containing is not calling). An A/B of the old and new code over one fixed 44,842-node survey of hypergumbo itself moved nothing: 2152 dead candidates / 41.4% on both sides, because all 11,260 of that graph's `instantiates` edges point at a class or external symbol and none at a callable. That set now derives the call family from the registry, the third consumer to stop keeping a private copy that omitted `instantiates` after taint and verify-claims. The constant is now **module-level rather than a function local**: its own closure gate had been keeping a fourth copy and pinning it to production by scraping the function's source text for the literal, so the drift the scrape existed to detect was reproduced inside the test meant to prevent it. Consumers import the object; the gate now asserts each dispatch family's edge type is present rather than matching a literal.

- **Four `call_construct` values named a different axis than the field does (INV-tadup).** INV-vakaf drained one instance of this field's axis-mixing — `cross_file`, split out to `call_locality` — and left four. `remote_external` (erlang) and `application_external` (haskell) differ from their unsuffixed siblings only by whether the resolver found the callee, which is already recoverable from `dst`: a synthetic external node id versus an in-repo symbol id. The AST shape is identical, so the suffix was resolution status wearing the construct field's name. `chained_return_type` (go) names how the receiver's type was *resolved* and moves to `resolution_quality`. `interface_dispatch` (go) was the sharpest case — the very same edge already carried `evidence_type="interface_dispatch"`, so one value was being stated twice on two different axes; the construct is simply `method`. A live-tree AST guard pins all four against reintroduction, and ships with a positive control asserting the same walk finds `method` — a guard that cannot be shown to fire is indistinguishable from one whose search matches nothing.

- **`subprocess.Popen(tainted)` verified CLEAN while `subprocess.run(tainted)` verified VIOLATED — one capital letter decided whether a command-injection flow was visible.** `py.py` types `module.Attr()` as `instantiates` when the attribute is PascalCase and `calls` otherwise; `TAINT_CALL_EDGE_TYPES` did not contain `instantiates` and `taint.py` did not contain the string anywhere, so the taint walk could not traverse a construction edge at all. Two fixtures differing only in the sink's spelling produced `violated`/exit 1/2 DDG findings and `confirmed_with_caveats`/exit 3/0 findings respectively — the second stating "No unsanitized untrusted_input data reaches subprocess zone" about a two-line function in which it does, with an `opaque_boundary` caveat that named the wrong hazard (what the launched program does, rather than that attacker-controlled data chose the command line). The call-family half of the set is now **derived** from the registry declaration rather than restated — appending the literal would have made a fourth hand-maintained copy of "what counts as a call", which is the disagreeing-copies defect this cycle has been chasing. `verify_claims._CALL_SITE_EDGE_TYPES` adopts the same resolver, so the two sets now agree by construction instead of by coincidence; the taint-specific additions (`module_attr_ref` per WI-lokuv, `dispatches_to` per INV-zuhig) stay explicit, with regression tests pinning them, because deriving the set *wholesale* would silently delete both.
- **Two of hypergumbo's own fs-writes were outside the safety-zone discipline its claims assert, and were invisible for the same reason.** With construction edges traversable, `runtime-cli-no-host-fs` and `install-gitleaks-no-host-fs` immediately flipped to `violated`: `gitleaks.py` and the rust-analyzer probe in `graceful_degrade.py` called `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory()` directly, while the claims state that all writes go through the `safety_zones` wrappers. INV-zudak had enumerated the fs-write primitives as write_text / mkdir / unlink / chmod / copy2 / rmtree / replace — `TemporaryDirectory` is none of those, being constructor-shaped, so its drain looked complete only because nothing could see the call. A `tmp_artifact_dir()` context-manager wrapper joins the tmp_artifact zone and both sites route through it; the self-claims snapshot returns to 18 unchanged verdicts — restored by fixing the code, not by re-recording the baseline.

- **A C# method group was filed as a call construct on a `references` edge.** `csharp.py` stamped `meta['call_construct'] = 'method_group'` on the two `references` edges it emits for a method group (`Action h = Handle;`, `items.ForEach(Process)`) — but a method group *references* a method without invoking it, and `call_construct` is scoped to the call family. These were the only two sites in the tree where the two sibling keys actually overlapped, against a declaration that says they have distinct edge families. The label moves to `ref_construct`, the reference-family key that already lists `references` among its edge types, and `method_group` joins its documented value space. No consumer is affected: every reader of `call_construct` tests it against `"method"`, and `method_group` had no reader at all.

- **A meta key's edge scope lived only in a sentence, and the sentence was wrong on arrival.** `axis_meta_keys` described `call_construct` as the call construct on `calls` edges, and its sibling `ref_construct` asserted the two keys had "distinct edge families, zero overlap". That wording landed 2026-06-30 (INV-lajov) from a corpus measurement in which every observed `call_construct` sat on a `calls` edge — it described the sample, not the field, and was already false by the five `constructor`-on-`instantiates` sites that audit-findings 0012 §4 had deliberately created eight weeks earlier. The scope now lives on `applicable_edge_types`, the typed field ADR-0038 ruling 2 already built for per-edge-type applicability, so it is machine-readable instead of prose that can drift; `instantiates` is in scope deliberately, because `edge_type` is not a function of the construct — ruby emits `calls` for the same source construct dart and csharp emit `instantiates` for, which makes `call_construct='constructor'` the only cross-language invariant for object creation rather than a redundant restatement of the relationship. `method_call_recovery`'s two-edge-type set is re-commented to say why it holds two values (the analyzers disagree about the dst) instead of naming, stalely, which languages take which arm — java and py both emit `instantiates` today and ruby is the language that never does.

- **`contribute` failed instead of falling back when `gh` was installed but non-functional.** The arm-selection gate was `command -v gh` — "is the binary installed" — where the question it needed answered was "can gh complete a request". Ubuntu's packaged build (2.45.0-1ubuntu0.3) emits a malformed `User-Agent` (`GitHub CLI ` with a trailing space, the version never injected) that api.github.com rejects as an HTTP/2 `PROTOCOL_ERROR`, so every gh API call failed while `command -v gh` succeeded. `contribute` took the gh arm and exited 1 with `Failed to create PR`, never reaching the curl/`api_post` arm a few lines below — the same REST path `auto-pr` drives against that API every day, and already covered by `test_github_without_gh_falls_back_to_api_post`. A working fallback that is never reached is not a fallback. The gate now probes function (`gh auth status`, evaluated once and cached since it costs a round trip) and says why it fell back, so an installed-but-broken gh degrades to a working path instead of failing the contributor's PR. Third instance of the probe-asks-the-wrong-question class after INV-rupid ("is the library importable" ≠ "are the weights on disk") and WI-dojud ("is the binary present" ≠ "is the backend on"). `contribute` also gains `--help`, whose absence meant the script had no invocation that did not attempt to create a PR.

- **Eight sites answered "is this an inheritance edge?" their own way and disagreed; Solidity and Ruby lost dispatch to the disagreement (INV-nosoz).** `edge_types.INHERITANCE_EDGE_TYPES` existed and two consumers used it. `cli.py` kept a complete hand-rolled copy, a second `cli.py` site and `_transitive_bases.py` read `("extends", "implements")`, `inherited_calls.py` read `("extends", "implements", "includes")`, and `type_hierarchy.build_inheritance_maps` branched on two string literals and consulted no registry at all — the instruction to resolve the family from the registry has been in ADR-0023 §3 the whole time. Every consumer now routes through one predicate, `is_inheritance_edge()`, with `inheritance_dispatch_role()` for the consumers that need the family split by whether virtual dispatch is language-dependent.

- **`includes` is deliberately NOT enrolled in the inheritance family, and that exclusion is the substance of the fix.** The item posed the open question as "is `includes` a member of the family?", framing it as the Ruby mixin edge type. It is not one relationship: `includes` is a registered two-meaning value whose own entry reads "File or class includes / sources / mixes-in another unit's ...", emitted by nine producers of which **eight mean file inclusion** (latex, make, meson, puppet, requirements, rst, scss, twig). Measured on the Rails app *postal*: 41 `includes` edges — 39 `class -> module` Ruby mixins, and **2 `file -> external_symbol` edges from an SCSS `@include`**. Adding the bare string to the set would have made a stylesheet `@include`, a Makefile `include` and a LaTeX include directive into class is-a edges in slice expansion, ranking centrality and the dead-code ancestor walk — the mirror image of the hand-rolled copies. The family is resolved by a predicate over the *edge* instead, discriminating on the already-registered `evidence_type="ast_includes"` ("Edge inferred from a runtime mixin declaration"), which exactly one emit site produces. A test pins the SCSS case as producing no dispatch, so a future widening cannot pass silently.

- **Ruby mixin dispatch works and Solidity's does not, and the difference is measured rather than asserted.** On *postal*, type-hierarchy dispatches rise **53 → 58**. On *openzeppelin-contracts* — 516 `inherits` edges, 545 `overrides` edges — the delta is **zero**, because a second cause on a different axis was waiting behind the first: Solidity emits contract members as `kind="function"` (2,025 members of type-like containers, none of `kind="method"`) while `link_type_hierarchy` indexes candidates with a literal `sym.kind != "method"`. The `inherits` edge now reaches the dispatch map and finds it empty. Filed as its own invariant rather than folded in, since it closes on its own evidence; a test pins the still-broken Solidity shape so this entry cannot be read as claiming otherwise.

- **A Ruby mixin target is now a dispatch candidate even though `module` is not a type-like kind.** `type_hierarchy` selected candidates by `kind in type_like_kind_names()`, which excludes `module` — correctly, since Python and Go modules carry the same kind and are not types. The edge is the better evidence: a producer that emitted a mixin-evidence `includes` edge has already asserted that both ends participate in method resolution, so inheritance-edge endpoints join the candidate set. Endpoints with no contained methods contribute nothing, so the candidate set widens without the output widening.

- **`build_inheritance_maps`' two maps are merged rather than `update()`-ed over each other.** `link_type_hierarchy` unioned them with two `dict.update()` calls, which **replace** the value for a shared key — so a type both extended (`interface Solid extends Shape`) and implemented (`class Box implements Shape`) lost one entire set of children. Honest scope: latent by construction, not observed live — all three measured repositories emit zero `implements` edges between them — and closed here because widening the family raises the odds of a shared key.

- **The vocabulary cannot re-fork silently.** `find_partial_inheritance_family_literals()` fails CI on any core module that enumerates part of the family by hand, with the audit-0018 rule (enumerate all or none) and its exemptions: per-language analyzer packages, `language`-guarded expressions, and linkers whose declared `depends_on` cannot produce the missing spelling. Two structural filters keep it honest rather than merely quiet — a one-member mention is a lookup-table row, not a partial enumeration (`scip/edges.py` maps `("is_implementation", "implements")`), and the registry module itself may enumerate what it defines. **It also reads `==` chains**, not just literal collections, because the site that caused the defect was `if edge.edge_type == "extends": ... elif ... == "implements"` — a guard blind to that shape would not have caught the bug it is named for. Verified by turning it on the real defect before the fix: it fired on `build_inheritance_maps()` by name. `type_hierarchy` also now declares `solidity` in `depends_on`, which it always handled and never declared — the guard's own exemption logic reads that declaration, so an inaccurate one made the guard quiet for the wrong reason.

- **The AST-literal helpers move to a shared `_literal_scan` module** rather than being copied into the second linter that needs them. Putting the duplicate in the module whose job is preventing duplicates would have been a fine joke and a bad decision.

- **Solidity emitted every contract member as `kind="function"`, so 2,025 members per repo were invisible to every consumer keyed on `kind == "method"` (INV-lapas).** Containment now decides the kind, as it does in every other OO analyzer: a function inside a `contract` / `interface` / `library` is a `method`, and a file-scope free function (legal since Solidity 0.7) stays a `function`. `constructor`, `modifier` and `event` keep their own kinds — they are distinct constructs, not functions with a receiver. Combined with INV-nosoz, openzeppelin-contracts goes **0 → 877** Solidity `dispatches_to` edges; neither fix produces that alone, which is why they were filed and measured separately. The `interface_dispatch_parity` solidity cell, a strict xfail, now XPASSes and is retired — the second cell that gate has given up this cycle.
- **Identity note: `kind` is a slot in both `Symbol.id` and the typed `stable_id`, so contract-member ids CHANGE.** This is a producer correction rather than a scheme change — the formula is untouched and the input is now right — but the values are not comparable across the boundary. Regenerate; do not diff Solidity identities across it.
- **The kind vocabulary had six readers, and the sixth was in another package.** Four `kind == "function"` literals inside `solidity.py` (the enclosing-symbol span index, the `using X for Y` library-call resolver, and both halves of the `overrides` walker) plus two in `hypergumbo_core.linkers.solidity_abi`. The cross-package pair is what actually broke: the ABI linker silently stopped minting call-site nodes — **1,250 of them, taking 6,689 resolved call edges with them** — with nothing raised, just a count dropping. Reading the diff did not catch it; re-running the corpus did. Both packages now read one `SOLIDITY_CALLABLE_DECLARATION_KINDS` in core, pinned from the producer side where the kinds originate, because a constant duplicated across a package boundary drifts into a terminal rather than into CI.

- **A multi-boundary I/O primitive now declares WHY it has several, and one predicate answers the question for every consumer (INV-vaduk).** INV-zumin had already built the mechanism — `simultaneous` as a declared per-row marker, `simultaneous_boundaries_for` as the shared predicate, and `meta["io_boundaries"]` carrying every boundary instead of losing all but the first to YAML row order — and INV-kaduh wired `io_mode` into the C/C++ analyzers. Verified on the live catalogues: scala `Process.apply`, objc's two `NSURLConnection` request methods, and erlang/elixir `filelib.ensure_dir` all resolve to both boundaries. What was left is that **absence of a discriminator was overloaded**.
